# BACKLOG — Outstanding Work

> **This file is the list of outstanding activities.** The *how-we-work* rules live in **[CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md)**; closed/shipped work is archived in **[plan-archive.md](plan-archive.md)**. Split out of the former `plan.md` on 2026-07-09.
>
> **⚠️ Before starting ANY row — Existing-Work-First (CONTRIBUTING.md § Existing-Work-First Policy):** `git status` + `git log` first (uncommitted/fresh work matching the row = it's done or in progress — finish it, don't redo it); check for a second live session (`ps aux | grep claude`) before editing shared files; grep to *disprove* the row's premise (rows go stale — four rows have closed as ALREADY SHIPPED). A §RESUME entry says where the last session *stopped*, not what the tree looks like *now*.
>
> **Legend:** `[ ]` open · `[~]` in-progress / recurring · `[x]` closed (kept here for context; migrate to plan-archive.md over time).

### §POT-PROMOTE — potential.md Seed Batch → 12 Tracks (⚠️ PLANNED 2026-07-23)

> **Status: ⚠️ PLANNED — specs only, no HTML edited.** This batch promotes the **16 OPEN + unblocked seeds** from `potential.md` (the ones that need neither §VM-01 nor a load-bearing constraint broken) into **12 coherent `§`-tracks**, merging where `potential.md` itself says the work is one surface (W1+S4+M1 → one codex; W4+W5+P2 → the shipped `textVariants` rail). The **BLOCKED seeds stay in `potential.md` by design** — C5/H4/B4/R1/R3/S2 need **§VM-01 Inc A** (the `choice`/`wait` opcode) and X3 needs **§VM-01-F** (the gate expression evaluator); promoting one would grow a private branch mechanism, exactly the *"new single-use term"* the **Host/Script Separation Policy** forbids. `potential.md` §POT-C2's actionable half already shipped as **§NPC-01-C** (`meta.enemy` ⚔ footer); only its faction-response half remains, blocked. §POT-C3 became **§NPC-01-SF5** above (the drift re-scoped it into the NPC-card track).
>
> **Evidence standard.** Every anchor below was **re-measured live 2026-07-23** at `ENGINE_VER = 'r2h-3.104.0'` (`roll2hit-v3.html`, **37,870 lines**), not carried from `potential.md`'s `43bd09c` snapshot — several of that snapshot's claims had already drifted (C2/C3 surfaces shipped via §NPC-01; `worldTruth` now renders at Dear Friend). **Re-grep every anchor before editing anyway** — line numbers are hints, they drift (§DX-01e). **Each track is a spec, not code:** build one increment, verify, mark it, stop. A track that grows to a chain/system/arc gets a `lab-reports/lab-report-*.md` **before any HTML edit** (Lab Report Policy). ⚠️ Restart the WBAPI server before any write session (Hazard #1). *(`api.sh post monster` — the old Hazard #2 — was fixed 2026-07-30 by §DX-01c and is now the normal path.)*

#### §CODEX-01 — The read-only Codex: surface what the engine already knows (merges §POT-W1 + §POT-S4 + §POT-M1) 🔴

**The finding (measured).** The game has **no rules surface of any kind** — `grep -ci "helpScreen|rulesTab|glossary|codexScreen|showRules|showHelp"` = **0** across 37,870 lines. The entire tutorial is **one NPC monologue** (`yael.impartial[0]`), and §PLAY-01-D had to add a special-case guarantee (`yaelOnboardingSeen`) purely to deliver it once because the auto-active Slums quest shadowed it. Meanwhile the engine privately knows a lot it never transmits: `_magicTierAllowed` = `level ≥ magic×5`, `XP_LEVELS`, `CONDITION_ADV`, `_monsterLevel`, `EFFORT_XP_PCT` (`const EFFORT_XP_PCT@24426`), the d100 loot weights. Two neighbours have the same shape: **downtime verbs exist with no hub** (`downtime` = 0 hits, but fishing with 20 ranks + `FISHING_GUIDE_TEXT` (`const FISHING_GUIDE_TEXT@26654`), 5 vendor nodes, **38** `sleep:true` rest nodes, Hunt Mode, 12 `type:'craft'` quests all ship — the game never tells you a place *offers* them); and **world lore lives only as prose** — `WORLD_DB` (`const WORLD_DB@6279`) is `{label,icon,monsters[]}` with no political/historical field, and **219 `meta.worldTruth` lines** now render only as the §NPC-01-C `✦` footer at Dear Friend (fav≥2), so most are still unread at any reachable favor.

**The thesis (why one surface, not three).** This is the **textbook §PLAY-01 "honesty" class** — *the engine knows things it won't transmit* — the exact pattern §PLAY-01-A proved safe (the objective chip: surface the known, diegetically, UI-only, **zero balance risk, no design call**). `potential.md` §POT-M1 explicitly says *"Merge with §POT-W1 + §POT-S4 into one codex surface."* Do **not** add `culture:`/`power:` fields to `WORLD_DB` first — that is authoring, and the content already exists unread; this track is a **renderer**, not new lore.

**Increments (each a shippable slice):**
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-A — the Codex shell + Rules/Glossary tab (keystone).** A read-only overlay (mirror the objective-chip / void-modal UI patterns) with a Rules pane that reads the engine's *own* constants live — skill-check formula, magic-tier rule, XP curve, effort-XP %, loot weights, `TIER_LABELS`. No new data: it reflects existing constants so it can never drift from balance.
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-B — Gazetteer pane (folds §POT-W1).** Per-region lore aggregated from already-authored strings — `NODE_MAP[].text` + the region's `worldTruth` lines — as a browsable read-only index. Still a renderer; authors nothing.
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-C — "What this place offers" downtime board (folds §POT-S4).** At the current node, list the verbs that actually apply here (rest + cost, vendor, fishing if a rod-node, craft, Hunt) by querying node/state — the honest "you have time; here's what's here" surface §POT-S4 wants.

**Invariants:** render-only — **Free-Movement untouched** (no mover code, no quest-state in a step); outside all four parity fences (no `js/*.js` twin — inline engine JS, edited directly); **seeded-RNG N/A** (no rolls). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline-JS hand-edit — stop the server first, commit early). **Verify:** new `tests/integration/codex.test.js` (overlay opens/closes; Rules pane reflects the live constants; Gazetteer lists ≥1 `worldTruth`; downtime board matches the node's real verbs) + eyeball (§7½). Baseline reds unchanged (I1/I2, R2/R3).

#### §TIDE-01 — The world visibly changes after events (merges §POT-P2 + §POT-W5 + §POT-W4) 🔴

**The finding (measured).** §GR shipped `node.textVariants:[{flag,text}]` (`85faf9b`) — recorded as *"display-only, API-authored, **reusable for any 'node changes after an event'."*** The renderer is generic and idle: `_renderNodeText`-side loop at `node.textVariants@34607` (`for (const _v of (node.textVariants||[]))… if (S_story[_v.flag]) …`). Measured consumers: **exactly one** (`flag:'fishmongerRowRestored'@8802`, the §GR payoff). So a **built, tested, API-authorable mechanism has 1 of 401 possible consumers.** Two beats want it and have never used it: **(W5) the apocalypse is narrated in past tense and never becomes world state** — `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` (`const VOID_TIDE_EVENTS@22368`) narrate the Void's advance and `_addVoidPressure` (`function _addVoidPressure@26970`, called on sleep at `Increment void pressure at each tide event@36359`) advances a counter, but **no node has ever become ruined**; and **(W4) the 1367-vs-cyberpunk blend is unjustified in-world** — §1367 already fixed this class once (Events A–F were parked at the HKG cyberpunk node and had to be relocated to thematic nodes).

**The design (already legal, already implemented).** CONTRIBUTING's Free-Movement policy **explicitly blesses W5's shape**: *"If a future feature needs a place to feel impassable until something happens, it must do so by **changing terrain/`CELL_GRID`/`IMPASSABLE_CELLS` state** (the cell genuinely becomes/stops-being sea), not by consulting quest state inside the mover."* `IMPASSABLE_CELLS` is a computed set (`const IMPASSABLE_CELLS@9871`) → the Void tide rewrites *terrain/cell state* (the cell genuinely changes), never gates a step. W4/W5 node-text beats are **pure authoring against shipped rails** — `textVariants` needs **zero code**.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§TIDE-01-A — the W4 anachronism account (zero code) ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-23.** One `textVariants` beat giving the 1367-courier-in-Neon-Undercity blend a diegetic account, keyed on an existing flag. Pure `./api.sh` authoring; proves the idle renderer on a second consumer. **✅ Done:** added `textVariants:[{flag:'cyMaintenanceDecoded', text:…}]` to **HKG** (Neon Undercity, `8328`), keyed on the existing flag set by **`quest_d0207_a4`** ("The Cipher" — the moment the player decodes that a **Scholar King** built the Undercity three centuries ago, matching the Froberger cipher fragment). The variant re-reads the same descent (30 steps · forced turnstiles · two androids + data wraith checkpoint · Bruhns already past — all functional beats preserved) with the neon reframed as the Scholar King's **bioluminescent resonance channels**, so the anachronism reads as ancient engineering, not a genre error. Deliberately keyed on `cyMaintenanceDecoded` (a4), **not** `cyOriginKnown` (a5), so it does **not** spoil the deeper "the builder's name is *yours*" reveal. **Write path:** offline one-shot through the **WBAPI core** (`WBAPI.load → editStructuredField('node','HKG','textVariants',…) → save(GAME)`) — byte-identical to what the server's `PUT /api/node/HKG` does (server routes array fields to `editStructuredField` at `wbapi-server.js:10958`), invoked in-process to honor **no-background-processes** (no server spun up). **Verified:** WBAPI roundtrip ok (len 1448, `inserted:true`); git diff = **HKG +1 line only**; inline `<script>` parse **0 errors**; new `tide-node-textvariants.test.js` **3/3** (flag-unset shows base + not variant; `cyMaintenanceDecoded:true` shows variant + not base; AMS first-consumer regression still swaps → rail is generic); `check:invariants` — **2 pre-existing J14/J15 junction violations, unchanged** (confirmed identical at HEAD via stash; **not mine** — logged as §JUNK-01 below). Not committed (user rule).
- [x] **§JUNK-01 ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-28 via §DX-01a** — option (b)-adjacent: J14/J15 removed outright (§WALK's 0-junction model restored) and the road they stood in for actually laid (`build-roads.js --apply`); `check:invariants` I1/I2 + `check:roads` R2/R3 all green. Full record: the §DX-01a ship row. Original row: **2 pre-existing invariant violations: J14/J15 junction nodes reference a `junction` terrain absent from `WORLD_DB` (found during §TIDE-01-A).** `check:invariants` reports **I2** (`junction:true=2` occurrences in source) + **I1** (`J14→junction`, `J15→junction` — 2 nodes reference a terrain absent from `WORLD_DB`). **Confirmed pre-existing** at HEAD (`git stash` diff — identical count before/after the §TIDE-01-A edit), so not introduced by recent work. Either (a) add a `junction` terrain entry to `WORLD_DB` (matching the `junction:true` node convention), or (b) reassign J14/J15 to a real terrain + drop the `junction:true` marker per §WALK's junction model (0 junctions was the §WALK close-out claim — these 2 may be strays that survived). **Terrain/graph change → via WBAPI**, and re-run `check:invariants` (target: I1/I2 clean) + `check:roads`. Low urgency (both are junctions, not player-facing settlements), but it's the only thing keeping `check:invariants` red.
- [ ] **§TIDE-01-B — Void-tide node-text ruin (authoring).** Wire `textVariants` on the nodes `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` names, keyed on the Void-pressure flags that already advance, so a place *reads* as ruined once the tide reaches it. Still zero engine code.
- [ ] **§TIDE-01-C — Void-tide terrain rewrite (the real mechanism; **lab report first**).** Have the tide flip specific cells' terrain/`IMPASSABLE_CELLS` state when pressure crosses a threshold — the genuine "the area was altered by [event]" beat. ⚠️ This touches cell/terrain state + reachability → **lock the cell list + thresholds in a lab report**, and **re-run `check:reachability` (100% from LHR) + `check:roads` after** (a rewrite must not orphan a settlement). **Trap:** change terrain state, never gate the mover.

**Invariants:** A/B are data-only (Free-Movement N/A). C changes terrain/cell state **only via `IMPASSABLE_CELLS`/`CELL_GRID`** — never quest-state in the mover; **no jump travel**; reachability must stay 100%. ⚠️ Restart the server before the write session (Hazard #1). **Verify:** A/B — eyeball the node text after the flag flips (§7½); C — `check:reachability`/`check:roads`/`check:invariants` green + `check:walk` + eyeball. Sync `world.md` §Void Tide + `maps.md`/`story.md` for any cell change.

#### §XP-02 — First-arrival exploration XP (completes §XP-01) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** §XP-01's user directive was *"all action earns XP"* and it shipped **three** grant sites — fled enemy, missed attack, failed check (`EFFORT_XP_PCT = 0.25`, `const EFFORT_XP_PCT@24426`; fail-XP at `a failed check still earns effort XP@7003`). Yet **exploration — the one thing the game is built around — grants nothing.** `S_story.visited` is a bare boolean per node whose **only** consumer is map tinting (`_getNodeMapColor`, `function _getNodeMapColor@27557`; `.visited` = 17 refs, all cosmetic). Walking somewhere new earns nothing.

**The design.** This is an **unfinished §XP-01**, not new mechanics: add a fourth grant site keyed on *first* arrival at a node (guard on the existing `visited` boolean flipping false→true) using §XP-01's existing dials. **Bound it** so it can't out-earn combat (mirror §XP-01's cap: exploration XP ≤ the flee value) and so backtracking pays nothing (the `visited` guard already gives first-arrival-only for free). No seeded roll needed (a flat/level-scaled grant), so **RNG N/A**.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§XP-02-A — first-arrival grant ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-23.** On the `visited[node]` false→true transition, award a small bounded XP grant; make it level-aware if desired (a trivial node at L18 pays little) but keep it flat-simple first. **✅ Done:** new dial `const EXPLORE_XP = 10` (next to `EFFORT_XP_PCT`/`EFFORT_MISS_PCT`, `~24070`) + `_grantExplorationXp(node)` helper, hooked in `storyCollectLoot` (`~29655`, the sole `visited`-setting site) as `if (!S_story.visited[node.code]) _grantExplorationXp(node)` — **before** the visited flip, so it fires exactly once per node; backtracking pays nothing (the guard), and a `if (node.junction) return` guard skips routing junctions. XP is granted **inside `storyRender` after the free step already landed** (never gates movement); UI (message + any level-up modal) is **deferred via `setTimeout`** so the render finishes first, and `_levelUpQueue` is sliced-captured before the deferred read. Accrues telemetry to `S_story.explorationXp`. **Bounded** ≤ the weakest starter flee value (10 ≤ protofleder `round(12·16·0.25)`=48), so exploring can never out-earn a fight. **RNG N/A** (flat grant, `check:rng` green). **Verify:** new `exploration-xp.test.js` **2/2** (first arrival grants EXPLORE_XP once; re-visit grants 0; bounded ≤ protofleder flee; e2e telemetry through the real render path); inline `<script>` parse 0 errors. **Regression caught + fixed:** the full integration suite surfaced **1** real break — `quest-runtime-uqf.test.js:9509` (§MATH-01 "arrival at ZERO … xp+gold") calls `storyCollectLoot` on an unvisited node and asserted `xp===350+500`; now correctly `350+500+EXPLORE_XP` (the test was proven to be MY regression by passing on HEAD via stash-diff; the sibling arrival tests at `9538`/`9560` assert only gold/state, unaffected). **Full suite = 635 passed**; the 4 `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` + 1 flaky `multiplayer-presence` failures were **confirmed pre-existing** (identical on HEAD; worldbuilder tests never touch `roll2hit-v3.html`). `check:walk` sub-checks parity/behaviour/terrain/roomsparity/questparity/gateast/rng/questgraph all **green**; only the **pre-existing** `check:invariants` (J14/J15 → §JUNK-01) + `check:roads` (R3 sea-overlap + 3 off-component settlements = the §DX-01 TGS/SPB baseline) stay red, both **unchanged** by this edit. Docs synced: `mechanics.md` §XP (three-dial table) + `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`. Not committed (user rule).
- [ ] **§XP-02-B (optional) — a surfaced "unexplored" signal.** Pairs with §CODEX-01/§POT-H2's fog-of-war idea; defer unless wanted (no fog-of-war state today — do **not** add one speculatively).

**Invariants:** Free-Movement untouched (XP is granted *after* the free step lands, never gates it); outside all parity fences; if any randomness is ever added it draws the seeded stream (`_seededNext()`), never `Math.random()`. **Verify:** `tests/integration/exploration-xp.test.js` (first arrival grants once; a re-visit grants nothing; total exploration XP ≤ the §XP-01 cap) + `check:walk`. Sync `mechanics.md` §XP + `docs/mechanics/`.

#### §CLUE-01 — Cumulative multi-clue mysteries (no new opcode) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Mysteries are the file's best content (`quest_scar_01` "The Open Ledger"), but the shape is always **one `skill_check` → pass/fail → done** — no clue accumulation, no partial knowledge, because `execBits` is straight-line and cannot carry a result forward. **But the parts already ship:** `S_story.knowledge[]` is a clue store (the `reward` bit's `knowledge` field), and knowledge items already render — `.inv-item-knowledge` CSS exists (`.inv-item-knowledge@1546`, `.inv-item-knowledge@3238`). A **cumulative** mystery needs no VM change; only a **branching** one needs §VM-01.

**The design.** Author multi-clue mysteries as *existing* UQF: several quests each granting a `knowledge` clue, then a final quest gated `{all:[flags/knowledge…]}` (or `{any}`) that delivers the reveal `narrative`. **No new opcode, no `execBits` change** — this is a **content pattern demonstrated end-to-end**, not an engine track. ⚠️ Do **not** hand-author a *branching* chain: `skill_check` "smuggles its own branch" and CONTRIBUTING names it *"the exception to retire, not the pattern to copy."* Cumulative-only here.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§CLUE-01-A — confirm + document the pattern.** `./api.sh advise` on a scratch clue quest; write the canonical "cumulative mystery" recipe into `quest.md` (like the "collect at node" pattern) so future mysteries reuse it.
- [ ] **§CLUE-01-B — one exemplar arc.** Author a 3–4-clue mystery (clues via `knowledge` grants at distinct nodes → a `{all}`-gated reveal), UQF-only, with an integration test. Lab report only if it grows past a handful of quests.

**Invariants:** UQF data only — Host/Script separation kept (no new bit kind, no `_legacy_fn`); Free-Movement N/A (gates list missions, never block steps); seeded RNG for any check. **Verify:** `./api.sh advise` clean on each quest + `tests/integration/clue-mystery.test.js` (clues accumulate in `knowledge[]`; the reveal gate opens only when all are held) + `check:walk` (`check:questgraph` proves no soft-lock). Sync `quest.md`.

#### §BOSS-01 — Declarative boss phases + terrain-conditioned encounters (merges §POT-B3 + §POT-B2) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` (10 refs) is **20 flat stat blocks** — no phases (`grep -c "phases:"` = **0**). The only staging is §PLAY-01-B's **one-time enrage at ≤30% HP**, tier-scaled, guarded by `S.opp.enraged` (reset `// §PLAY-01-B: one-time low-HP Void enrage@24639`, fire `S.opp.enraged = true@25211`). And **terrain is in the room but never in the fight**: no legendary/lair actions, no environmental advantage — combat reads terrain only via the `_VOID_TERRAIN_RE` name heuristic; the `ROOMS` kernel knows `terrainAt` but the fight never asks.

**The design (generalize shipped code into data).** Both halves have a **ready-made safe pattern**: §PLAY-01-B added its AI branch in `_storyEnemyTurn` (`function _storyEnemyTurn@25191`) — **outside the kernel** — leaving `DUEL:CORE` (`DUEL:CORE:START@10238`) git-diff-verified untouched. Do the same here.
- **B3 → `phases`:** a declarative `phases:[{atPct, atk, dmg, msg}]` field on `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` generalizes the enrage (which **is** a one-stage phase with working `S.opp.enraged` lifecycle) into data — squarely Host/Script-Separation-compliant (new shapes go in the grammar/data, never a new single-use term), reusing the enrage lifecycle rather than inventing one.
- **B2 → terrain-conditioned encounters:** read `terrainAt`/the node's terrain in `_storyEnemyTurn` (outside `DUEL:CORE`) to grant an environmental modifier, mirroring §PLAY-01-B's branch site.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§BOSS-01-A — `phases` field (B3).** Add the declarative field + a phase-transition check in the enrage site; migrate the existing enrage to be `phases:[{atPct:30,…}]` so current behavior is byte-identical, then author a 2-phase boss.
- [ ] **§BOSS-01-B — terrain modifier in `_storyEnemyTurn` (B2).** A small, data-described terrain→encounter effect, kept entirely outside the kernel.

**Invariants:** ⚠️ **`DUEL:CORE` (`DUEL:CORE:START@10238`) is parity-fenced** — never edit the inlined copy; the effect lives in `_storyEnemyTurn` (host, outside the fence); if `js/duel.js` is touched at all, re-inline + `scripts/check-duel-parity.js`. Enrage/phase rolls draw the **seeded** stream. Free-Movement N/A. **Verify:** `tests/integration/boss-phases.test.js` (enrage-as-phase byte-identical; a 2-phase boss transitions at each `atPct`; terrain modifier applies only on the right terrain) + `scripts/check-duel-parity.js` green (kernel untouched) + `check:walk`. Sync `monsters.md` (`EPIC_BOSS_POOL` `phases` shape) + `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`.

#### §CHAR-01 — One-time point-buy at character creation ✅ ALREADY SHIPPED 2026-07-23 (the §POT-C1 open slice) 🟢

> **⚠️ CORRECTION (2026-07-23) — §CHAR-01-A was ALREADY SHIPPED; the "design call" is moot. grep-before-building win.** The finding below claimed `grep -c "pointBuy|standardArray|_ASI_LEVELS" = 0` and concluded "the player never chooses them." **That grep used the wrong terms.** The shipped point-buy is named `CC_BUDGET`/`_CC_COST`/`_cc_scores`/`_showCharCreate`/`story-charcreate-modal`. Measured live: a complete **27-point D&D-5e point-buy** (`_CC_COST=[0,1,2,3,4,5,7,9]` for scores 8..15, `CC_BUDGET=27`, `37748`) in the **"⚔ Choose Your Origin"** modal (`4960`) with **custom + hard** origin tabs, +/- steppers clamped 8–15 with live budget enforcement (`_ccRefresh`/`_ccSpent`), **wired** `btn-continue-new`/`btn-gameover-new` → `_showCharCreate()` → `btn-charcreate-begin` → `storyNewGame(scores)` (`37816`) which writes `S_story.abilityScores` + derives `atkBonus`/`hpMax`. So the player DOES author the character the 2,631 checks are about. It just had **no test** — now locked by `char-creation.test.js` **4/4** (budget=27 + cost curve; modal + custom/hard tabs + begin button; custom Begin writes scores & derives ATK/HP; hard Begin ships the fixed array). **Same class as §GR/§DESIGN-03/§DUNGEON-01/§1367/§MATH-01 — "planned" work that was already live; grepping the real symbol names (not the seed's guessed ones) closed it.** Optional residual only: add an explicit **standard-array `[15,14,13,12,10,8]` preset** button as a third origin (convenience, not a fork) — logged as §CHAR-01-B below; the core one-time point-buy needs nothing.

**The finding (measured — SUPERSEDED by the correction above).** Fighter-only is **explicit design** (`FIGHTER_FEATURES`; the L10 entry: *"No style choice in this world — your body has become the weapon"*) and races/classes **must not** be built — they would invalidate the DC curve across 2,850 quests. **But** `abilityScores` (STR/DEX/CON/INT/WIS/CHA) drive **all 2,631 `skill_check` bits** via `_rollSkill` — the player DOES choose them at new game via the shipped point-buy (the original claim of "never chooses" was a wrong-search-term artifact, see correction).

**The design (honest, bounded, no rebalance).** A **one-time point-buy / standard-array at `storyNewGame()`** — no class, no race, **no DC rebalance** (total points fixed so the curve is unchanged in aggregate; a player merely *distributes* what was already implicitly flat). Fits `_S_DEFAULTS()` per §STATE-INIT (declare nothing new — the field exists; just let new-game write it). **Trap:** keep the point total balanced against the existing DC curve — this is redistribution, not power creep.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§CHAR-01-A — the point-buy screen at new game ✅ ALREADY SHIPPED (verified 2026-07-23).** The one-time allocation UI at `storyNewGame()` exists and is wired (see the correction above): the 27-point-buy "⚔ Choose Your Origin" modal writes `S_story.abilityScores`, guarded to fresh new game only (`_showCharCreate` clears autosave via `storyNewGame`'s `_S_DEFAULTS()` reset, respecting §STATE-INIT's single-source shape). **No design call was needed** — point-buy is the live system. Locked by `char-creation.test.js` 4/4. Not committed (user rule).
- [ ] **§CHAR-01-B (optional) — a standard-array preset origin.** Add a third origin button seeding `_cc_scores = {15,14,13,12,10,8}` (the classic 5e array) as a one-click convenience next to custom/hard. Pure additive UI, same `storyNewGame` path, no rebalance. Low priority — the point-buy already covers this allocation manually.

**Invariants:** writes an **existing** `_S_DEFAULTS()` field (no new persistent field); DC curve unchanged (fixed point total); Free-Movement/parity N/A; no rolls (RNG N/A). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline-JS). **Verify:** `tests/integration/point-buy.test.js` (new game writes a valid distribution; total matches the fixed budget; a mid-game save is untouched) + `check:walk`. Sync `mechanics.md` §Character + `index.md` State Fields. **Ask-gate:** the array-vs-point-buy choice is a user design call (Loop-vs-Ask).

#### §MESH-01-HIRE — Give the hireling a voice (the §POT-C4 open slice) 🟢

**The finding (measured).** No party — and none wanted (`DUEL:CORE`/`S_story`/XP are single-combatant; MESH multiplayer is the better answer and ships). But the **§MESH-01g hired-guide bot** (`#hireling-section`, `hireling-section@2862`+) is *"a single-player companion, daily fee"* with **no `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry** — the one "companion" in the game is a daily charge with no voice.

**The design.** Give the hireling a dialogue key so it becomes a character, not a fee. Tiny, self-contained. Optionally surface a line when hired/paid.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§MESH-01-HIRE-A — a dialogue key for the guide bot.** Add an `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry (with `meta`) for the hireling and render a line at hire/daily-fee time.

**Invariants:** data + a small render hook — Free-Movement/parity/RNG all N/A. ⚠️ Hazard #1 if the render hook is inline JS. **Verify:** eyeball the hire flow (§7½) + a smoke assertion that the line renders. Sync `story.md`/`docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md`.

#### §RIPPLE-01 — Give the favor & mission-bit tracks consumers (merges §POT-P3 + §POT-W3) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Two near-dead relationship tracks have no *consequences*: **(P3)** `mission_bit` is the second-most-authored opcode — **2,448 receipts** (`_flagToLabel` rewrite `function _flagToLabel@26081`+, `token.day` timeline, `_takeMissionBit` gate-safety all shipped) — but they are read **only by gates** (i.e. only to decide whether a *later mission lists*); nothing ever *reacts* to a token you carry. **(W3)** rest is **identical at all 38 `sleep:true` nodes** and costs nothing; the file has a full favor model that a haven could hang on, but a haven's quality never tracks its resident NPC's favor.

**The design (now unblocked by §NPC-01-B).** §NPC-01-B took the NPC card from ~20 → ~203 reachable NPCs — so **NPCs now have a render surface to react *on***. P3: render a reaction line on an NPC's card when you carry a relevant mission-bit token (a receipt they'd recognize). W3: let a `sleep:true` haven's quality (rest message / a small bonus) track the resident NPC's favor — legal because **resting is not movement** (no quest/flag refuses a step). Both give the 16-bit favor track + the 2,448-token ledger their first *reactive* consumer.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-A — NPC reacts to a carried token (P3).** On the NPC card, when the player holds a mission-bit the NPC is keyed to, show a recognition line. Render-only; reuses §NPC-01's card. **Depends on §NPC-01-B (shipped).**
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-B — haven quality tracks resident favor (W3).** The rest message (and optionally a tiny rest bonus) at a `sleep:true` node varies with the resident NPC's favor. **Trap:** resting only — never touch the mover.
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-C (design call) — widen the favor track.** With ~203 card-bearing NPCs, the 16-`favor`-bit track is too thin to earn the ladder; overlaps **§NPC-01-D**. Coordinate — do not double-scope.

**Invariants:** render + rest only — **Free-Movement untouched** (resting is not a step); outside all parity fences; RNG N/A (or seeded for any bonus roll). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline JS). **Verify:** `tests/integration/npc-token-reaction.test.js` (card shows the line only when the token is held) + a rest-quality assertion + eyeball (§7½). Sync `story.md`/`docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md`. **Coordinate with §NPC-01-D** on the favor-widening.

#### §MUD-01 — A text/MUD-style view over the shipped room engine (the §POT-S3 unblocked half) 🟡

**The finding (measured).** `ROOMS:CORE`'s `describeCell` (`function describeCell@10150`; kernel `ROOMS:CORE:START@9985`) already produces full room prose + per-direction exits with `__roadDestination` hints — but its full output is exercised mainly by `tests/mud-harness.mjs` (**269/269**); the player sees only node text + the §NAV-01-FU road-hover tooltip. *"Describe a vivid environment"* is close to what `describeCell` **already returns.**

**The design.** A text/MUD-style view is **mostly a renderer over shipped output** — surface `describeCell`'s prose + exits as an in-game panel. The *"I declare how my character interacts"* half is **§POT-R1 (BLOCKED on §VM-01 Inc A)** and is explicitly **out of scope** — this track ships only the **read** half (the view), which is unblocked and cheap.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§MUD-01-A — the descriptive view.** Render `describeCell(world, pos)`'s prose + exit list as a read-only in-game panel (reuse the harness's proven output). No input parser (that's §POT-R1).

**Invariants:** ⚠️ **`ROOMS:CORE` (`ROOMS:CORE:START@9985`) is parity-fenced** — read its output, never edit the inlined copy; if `js/rooms.js` is touched, re-inline + `scripts/check-rooms-parity.js`. Render-only; Free-Movement N/A. **Verify:** reuse `tests/mud-harness.mjs` expectations for the rendered strings + eyeball (§7½). Sync `maps.md`/`docs/spec/spec-corridors.md`.

#### §HINT-01 — State-aware hints (needs a design call first) 🟡

**The finding (measured).** Quests carry a `hint:` field, **widely authored** (`grep -c "hint:"` = **1,057**), stating the stat + DC outright (e.g. *"See through the mockery — WIS Insight DC 11"*). Today's hint is a **constant string** — it doesn't know your inventory, level, or favor.

**The tension (why this needs a design call, not a loop task).** §POT-M2 wants a hint that knows *your* state — but this collides head-on with §PLAY-01's **honesty theme**: a hint that *hides* the DC to seem cleverer would be **less** honest, not more. The value depends entirely on which way it goes. **Do not build before the design call is made.**

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§HINT-01-0 — the design call (ASK).** Decide the shape: does a state-aware hint *add* context ("you already hold the Van Helsing route map") while **keeping** the DC visible (honesty-preserving), or does it replace the static string? Present the options; do not default. Only then scope A.

**Invariants:** TBD by the design call; if built, render-only. **Verify:** TBD. **This is an Ask-gate, not a loop item.**

#### §INNER-01 — A mechanic for the inner arc (the §POT-X2 design track; lab report first) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Yael's monologue promises an inner arc outright — *"You came here to fight demons… **the ones behind your own eyes are older and meaner, and that is the real fight."*** There is **no mechanic for that fight**. The one shipped inner-arc mechanic is §GR-D's **Froberger Entry 42** (`entry42Written`/`entry42Text`/`entry42Read` (`entry42Written: false, entry42Text@23124`); `quest_ng_02` "The Open Page" (`quest_ng_02@11048`); carried across NG+) — the blank page you may fill at ≥3 dear friends, at the very end of the game. Entry 42 is **the proof the shape works and the template for more.**

**The design (a design call + lab report, NOT a loop task).** Extending the inner arc is a **new narrative arc** → per the Lab Report Policy it needs `lab-reports/lab-report-inner-arc.md` **locking data shapes before any HTML edit**, and it needs a **user design call** on what the mechanic *is* (a reflective journal like Entry 42, a fear/resolve track, moral weight — several are possible and they are not interchangeable). This row is a **placeholder for that design conversation**, not buildable inline.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§INNER-01-0 — design call + lab report.** Decide the mechanic's shape (with the user); lock data shapes in a lab report; only then scope increments. Reuse Entry 42's cross-NG+ persistence pattern as the template.

**Invariants:** TBD by the lab report; likely render + a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field (declare once, per §STATE-INIT). **Verify:** TBD. **Design-call + lab-report gated.**

- [ ] **(Optional) organize `archive/` + `lab-reports/`.** `archive/` holds old cleanup lab reports + the duplicate plan-archive; `lab-reports/` is 85 flat files referenced widely (risky to nest — defer unless desired). `1367-sources/` is a self-contained importer workspace — leave.

Merging them would require rewiring npm scripts + the CI matrix in lockstep — a medium-risk refactor with little payoff. **Defer** unless the checker count actually becomes a maintenance burden.

### §VM-01-G2b-FU — Five act-gated Birka beats that can never fire (NEW 2026-08-04, 🟡 ASK)

- [ ] **§VM-01-G2b-FU — re-gate the five unreachable act-legged narrative beats.** **Measured at ship time of §VM-01-G2b:** `storyRender` assigns `S_story.actNumber = node.act || 1` on **every** render, before the npc-row region, and every Birka-region node is `act:1` — so an `actNumber >= N` (N ≥ 2) leg inside one of these blocks is permanently false. Dead: `birka-lamp-inquiry` (`>= 2`, `TLL`) · `birka-brynn-heartwood-letter` (`>= 4`, `TLL`) · `birka-yael-named-report` (`>= 6`, `LHR`) · `birka-s54-joint-witness` (`>= 7`, `LHR`) · `birka-quill-couperin-farewell` (`=== 8`, `LLA`). **The cascade:** `birka-lamp-choice` (Beat 2 of the §XXXV vigil arc) gates on `brynnKeeperStoryTold`, and the dead Beat 1 is that flag's **only writer** — so the choice, `brynnLightKept`, the §XXV farewell branch and the §XXVII Town Crier line are all unreachable too. **Why this is an ASK and not a fix:** §VM-01-G3 retired the identical dead legs from the quest stanzas by **dropping** them (the gate's other leaves already carried the staging), but these are narrative beats whose act leg IS the staging — something has to replace it, and the right signal (shards? a quest completion? a mission bit? a day threshold?) is a design decision. **And the docs disagree with the code on more than the act:** `world.md` §XXXIX designs the Yael Named Report for `LLA`/`HKG` at Act IV+, while the shipped block fires at `LHR` at Act VI+ — §AUDIT-03m-FU's lesson says do not annotate or "fix" an unverified claim into a confident-looking one, so neither number is to be trusted without corroboration. **Pinned:** `uqf-npc-row-hooks.test.js` asserts all five gates, both node acts and the cascade, so the fix must update the test. **Docs already annotated** (not rewritten) at all five sites in `world.md` and `story.md`.

### §VM-01-G4c-FU — The nine D1 surfaces the shipped grammar cannot express (NEW 2026-08-04, 🟡 THREE ASKS)

- [ ] **§VM-01-G4c-FU — three design calls, each blocking a group of D1 verbs.** **Measured at ship time of §VM-01-G4c**, which migrated the 4 of 13 D1 surfaces the grammar already expresses. The other 9 are not "the rest of the list" — each is blocked by a shape the VM does not have, and inventing one to satisfy two or three sites is the `itemsMinAny` failure the whole track exists to prevent. **(1) The delayed beat — Harbor Board (`STN`) · Solvak probe (`VS`) · Seal delivery (`VS`).** Each ends `setTimeout(() => storyMsg(…), 400/600/800)`. **`storyMsg` REPLACES `#story-move-msg`**, so the first line is *destroyed* after the beat — that pacing is authored, not incidental. `narrative` has no timing field and the kernel is pure (it cannot schedule), so folding both lines into the driver's buffer would make a transient line permanent. **ASK:** accept the merge (both lines survive, joined — arguably an improvement, and it is what §VM-01-G4c's migration already does *elsewhere*: Yva's speech and the firewood narrative used to be overwritten and now survive), or give the **host** driver a `delayMs` on `narrative` and keep the beat? **Recommendation: accept the merge**, because it is the same thing the slice already did at two other sites and it loses no words. **(2) Panel chrome — Surge Lock (`NUE`, the only `cost` `resource` site in the game) · Froberger sealed letter (`TLS`) · Codex Inquisitor (`NUE`).** Each hand-builds a `.sweelinck-variant` div with its own `border-left-color`, title colour, wrapper `margin-top` (8 vs 10), title `margin-bottom` (4 vs 6), body `margin-bottom` (6 vs 8) and, at Surge Lock, a button `background`. A **byte-identical** generic panel would need all six as registry fields — a CSS struct smuggled into a vocabulary. **ASK:** normalize the three to one panel look (a deliberate, small visual change, and then `panel:{title, body, accent}` is a real shape), or leave them inline as hand-styled one-offs? **Recommendation: normalize** — three sites is enough to make a shape, and the alternative leaves the only `resource` price in the game un-migrated. **(3) A ceremonia launch — Codex Inquisitor (also).** Its button calls `_rollCeremonia(questId)`: it *starts* a skill check rather than containing one, which is exactly why the `skill_check` scope fence does not need widening — but there is no bit that starts one. **ASK:** add a `ceremonia`/`roll_quest` bit, or leave the launcher inline? **Recommendation: leave it** until a second consumer exists (the §5 free-text rule: grow the shape at three, not two). **Also recorded, not a blocker:** at `NUE` the verb driver's completion re-render advances `npcVisitCounts['sq_revisit']`, because that block counts a **render, not an arrival** — so Sweelinck's once-per-run age line now lands immediately after the S49 click instead of on the next visit. It still fires exactly once, and the per-render counter is pre-existing (any `storyRender` re-entry at NUE already ticks it). Fixing it is a content-timing decision, not a migration step. **Not in this row:** the Reading Circle (a day cooldown — belongs to quests' `retryable`/`retryGateDays`, lab report §9.3) and the la_riva delivery (it lives inside CDG's `cq-boss-buttons` container, so it ships with **G4d**).

### §VM-01-G-FU-f2 — The three engine specials (NEW 2026-08-05, ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-06)

- [x] **§VM-01-G-FU-f2 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-06 `9c9fe42` — all three asks ANSWERED by the user 2026-08-06, each per the filed recommendation: (1) TLS → node field · (2) INN pricing STAYS · (3) patrol ordered-visit STAYS.** The one code slice: TLS authors **`finalBattle:{minLevel:20,minShards:7}`** in `NODE_MAP` (written through `WBAPI.editStructuredField` in-process, server stopped — 1-line data diff) and the new single helper **`_finalBattleReady(code)`** replaces the three hand-copied `=== 'TLS' && level>=20 && shards>=7` predicates at the encounter card + both quest-list Fight buttons — data + one reader, the §VM-01-G3 `onActivate` precedent, no grammar growth; a node without the field is never final. Decisions (2) and (3) are DECIDED-STAY, not open: the Innmother's waive stays one-inn until a second inn wants it (re-open only then, as a new row), and the patrol ladder stays an engine special serving its one quest (the §5 rule). **Evidence:** `vm01f2-finalbattle-field.test.js` **5/5 with 3 red on HEAD** (exactly the source/data/helper migration tests) and the 2 behaviour pins green BOTH ways (below-threshold plain Fight · at-20/7 THE FINAL BATTLE — Commander Bruhns + 🔥 FINAL FIGHT + mq_7's 🔥 Final Fight) — the honest no-op shape; §7½ eyeballed live both sides of the threshold; `check:walk` 16/16 (the behaviour gate printed ✓ then hung at 0% CPU — the documented detach artifact, 3rd sighting; all gates re-run individually exit 0); anchors:fix 112 hints in-slice; mechanics.md's Final Boss row re-anchored to live `TLS` (was legacy `CO`; `shards = 7` corrected to `≥`); full Playwright **935 passed / 4 failed (940)** — the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` set, `multiplayer-presence:171` flaked and retried green. **The below-front stack and the §11 inventory are now FULLY CLOSED — blocks and specials both.** Original row (the measurement + the three asks as filed): **Measured live at §VM-01-G-FU-f ship time** (the census's "genuine engine specials" row; the SSJ block that shipped beside them was the last *block* — these are not blocks but predicates woven into engine paths). **(1) TLS `isFinal` framing — 3 sites, and the duplication is the risk.** The predicate `(node|waypointNode) === 'TLS' && level >= 20 && shards >= 7` swaps `storyPreBattle` for `storyPreFinalBattle` and relabels the button 🔥 FINAL FIGHT, hand-copied at the encounter card and BOTH quest-list Fight buttons — three copies of one rule that must never disagree. **ASK:** a node field (`finalBattle:{ minLevel, minShards }` on TLS, read by one helper) vs stay engine-special? A node field is §VM-01-G3's `onActivate` precedent (per-node data driving an engine seam), not grammar growth. **(2) INN sleep pricing — 3 sites.** `freeBookingUnlocked` (written once at `innmotherKindness >= 5`, and `quest_inn_06`'s completion flag) zeroes `node.sleepCost` in the sleep flow, every site guarded `node.code === 'INN'` — the Innmother's kindness reward is invisible to every other inn in the game by construction. **ASK:** generalize to a node/data shape (e.g. a `sleepCostWaiveFlag` node field any inn could author) vs stay a one-inn special? **(3) Patrol ordered-visit — 3 arrival-path lines.** While `quest_city_watch_patrol` is active, arrivals write `patrolBA → patrolIN → patrolRouteComplete` in enforced LHR→TLL→MHQ order; the quest completes on the last flag. The gate grammar has no ordered-visit leaf, and this is its only consumer — the §5 free-text rule says don't grow a shape for one site. **ASK:** stay (an engine special serving one quest) vs re-express as a 3-quest chain (each leg `atNode`-completed, `unlock`-chained — no grammar growth, but three quest rows where the fiction has one) vs a `visitedSeq` gate leaf (grammar growth at one consumer)? **Recommendation: (1) node field, (2) stay until a second inn wants it, (3) stay** — but all three are the user's calls, filed not defaulted (the G-FU-d GCI precedent).

### §DX-02m — Unseeded `Math.random()` in state-writing paths (NEW 2026-08-04, 🟠)

- [ ] **§DX-02m — invariant #6 has no gate, and at least one path writes persisted state off the unseeded stream.** **Found by §VM-01-G2b's golden diff**, which is the honest way it surfaced: 16 of 17 combos were byte-identical across the migration and the 17th (`HKG`) differed **between two runs of the same code**. Cause: the CY Madness Gate (`_nodeHookBirkaCyMadnessGate`) rolls its WIS save with `Math.random()` and picks its madness line with `Math.random()`, then writes **`S_story.cyMadnessRoll`** and **`S_story.cyMadnessTable`** — persisted save fields. prompt.md §6 invariant 6 says game-state randomness draws the seeded stream (`_seededNext()`), so a save no longer determines this outcome. **Measured scope: 51 `Math.random()` call sites** in `roll2hit-v3.html`; how many reach persisted state is the row's first job (the CY gate is one, found by accident). **Nothing gates this today** — `check:rng` (gate #10) proves the three mulberry32 streams agree with each other, not *which* stream a state write draws from. **Suggested shape** (the §DX-02g/§AUDIT-03j house style): a scanner that classifies every `Math.random()` site as **COSMETIC** (allowed, with a reason) or **STATE** (must use the seeded stream), fail on an unclassified one — a percentage heuristic will not do, because most of the 51 legitimately are cosmetic. **Trap recorded by §DX-02f:** a test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws; converting a site changes which stream it reads, so any test pinning that outcome moves with it. **+11 named sites, the largest single-feature cluster yet (§DOC-02m, 2026-08-12):** the whole Yugurt Lake fishing path — eight in `` `function storyFishing() {@30392` `` (the DEX cast die, both Catch dice, the Type die, the species pick, the night-pool pick, the bait pick and the bait-search die) and three in the kill/lake-magic path around `` `function _lakeMagicBonuses() {@23420` ``'s consumers — every one feeding persisted state (`S_story.fishingCatchLog`, `S_story.inventory`, and through the battle `S_story.hp`). Same shape as §DOC-02l's epic negotiation rolls and §DOC-02k's Codex Core rolls: **the UQF quest path is seeded and every hand-authored surface that rolls its own dice is not**, because §VM-01-B moved the d20 inside `_resolveQuestUQF` and never reached the surfaces outside it. (Currently masked by §FISH-01 — the fishing path cannot be entered at all — so fix §FISH-01 first or the conversion is untestable.)

Ordering re-derived from `stat`, not from the preamble's arithmetic.
> **The headline is an inversion the program had not seen before.** Appendix A lists seven Layer-13 mechanics as *"described in this report but not yet implemented"* and **six shipped**, under the banner `` `LAYER 13: Short Rests & Necklace of Knowledge@25785` ``: the 3/day counter, the flat short-rest heal, the 2× off-inn award, the Necklace of Knowledge, bead acquisition on both rest paths, and the inventory section — with the specified bead record **byte-exact** to its field list. **Read against HEAD alone, every one of those rows reads as a live gap**, which makes this the cleanest argument in the program for the both-ways delta rule (instrument 6). The core formula survived verbatim too: `reward = floor(0.1 × AC × maxHP)` for heal and gold, all four loot sell values, all four potion heals.
> **And its worst section is a TABLE, which the transcription/narration rule says should be safe.** §II-A's five worked monster examples: **"Goblin Cutpurse" and "Orc Warlord" have 0 commits in the file's entire history**; Wererat is AC 13 against `ac:12` *at the archive as well as HEAD*; Ancient Dragon is HP 367 against `hp:546`, likewise both. One of five (Vampire Spawn 15/82) is exact. ***So the predictor is not table-versus-trace — it is whether the passage is a COPY of a source or an ILLUSTRATION composed to make a point.*** Those five were picked to display a difficulty gradient, so two were invented and two had their stats bent toward the curve being argued for. The same edit-to-persuade shows in §II-B, which transcribes `LOOT_TABLE` almost exactly (all four sell values, three of four slot counts) and **drops its Spell Scroll row**, folding those 2 slots into Minor Potions — which is precisely the 1.0 HP gap between the report's stated 28.75 HP/kill and the 27.75 its own source table yields. The live table pays **21.0**.
> **Three deltas worth keeping.** (1) **The self-funding thesis holds on two of three axes:** the heal is clamped to `hpMax` (`10 + CON mod` at level 1, `+d10 + CON mod` per level), so the heal axis **saturates** wherever `0.1 × AC × maxHP ≥ hpMax` — at level 1 that is nearly the whole pool, four of the report's own five rows are simply "full heal", and *"the dragon… heals you for 807 HP"* can never happen at any level. The engine is right; the rhetoric reads a saturating axis as a linear one. (2) **The rest architecture shipped inverted** — §III-B explicitly declines the 5e model for the short rest (*"Rather than Hit Die rolls…"*) and HEAD does exactly that, then ships **Hit Die rolls on the long rest**, so the specified DGQR full-HP restoration never landed. (3) The quest-boost pillar is **RETIRED**, not stale (tombstone at `` `§TIMELESS-01: Stalk Helpers@38269` ``). Stale counts: 42 nodes → **416**, 8 inns → **38**, ½-HP respawn → **1 HP** (1 at the archive too, so wrong when written).
> **Filed §DX-02t** — the short rest is **implemented twice**, and the post-battle copy at `` `const _rests = () => S_story.shortRests@7133` `` spends the same allowance while withholding the bead, the token, the Action Surge recharge and the `restedAtMin` gate credit, so `quest_d0206_a3` cannot be opened by that route. **The heal arithmetic is byte-identical between the two copies**, which is the lesson: a copy that is right on the lines it duplicates and wrong in everything it omits cannot be found by diffing. **Filed §AUDIT-03aa** — *"Boy Scouts"* names two mechanics with opposite rationales and two spellings, `` `Boy Scouts Award — doubled!@25858` `` (off-inn, the spec exactly) and `` `Boyscout Night! Double rolls@36317` `` (first-visit, firing **at an inn**). **Answered §DX-02n (a)'s open design call by measurement** — `_D100_TABLE` is canonical, `LOOT_TABLE` is a d20 table its own comment miscalls d100, and **both home docs already say it was deleted and replaced by a comment stub while HEAD still declares all 20 entries**; the archive shows it unread twelve days before the commit those docs credit (§DOC-02g's rule again — a migration note is a claim about the past). **6th §AUDIT-03v/w instance, and the widest surface in the cluster** — `` `full HP recovery@35759` `` and `` `heal to full & advance a day@35758` `` are **fixed engine strings on all 38 sleep nodes**, promising a heal the dice do not deliver; unlike v/w/y(b) this is not authored content on one node, which argues the wanted detector belongs in the engine-string universe (§AUDIT-03s's premise), not in quest data.
> **Evidence:** IEEE-shaped, ~90 lines of unmeasurable design philosophy deleted and replaced by a **37-row two-way delta table** plus a re-measured worked-examples appendix; **~30 new `symbol@line` anchors**, each verified verbatim and unique *before* writing, and none placed inside a markdown table (the §DOC-02f pipe-escape hazard); `check:anchors` **419 → 456 across 17 → 18 docs, 0 dead**; `check:legacycodes` / `nodeindex` / `dupkeys` / `noderegs` / `npcregs` all **exit 0**, run individually — the `check:walk` chain was never invoked, per the standing detach-artifact rule. **Docs-only: no HTML, no tests, no §7½ — the 935/4 (940) Playwright baseline stands untouched.** **Next: §DOC-02k — `lab-report-dungeon-ten-themes.md`, then the 2026-06-09 block alphabetically.**

> `lab-report-documentation-system-design.md` (2026-05-24, added at `59a9e0d`), **619 → 290 lines**. The oldest remaining report by mtime, alphabetically first in the shrinking 2026-06-09 block. **Existing-Work-First:** the same uncommitted user recolor is still in the tree (HP-bar gradient / light-green playfield / theme-ink die numerals, self-labelled *"user recolor 2026-08-06"*) — **left untouched and unstaged**; this increment is docs-only. **The first report in the program whose subject is the repo rather than the game**, and its subject is gone: `plan.md` was split into `CONTRIBUTING.md` + `BACKLOG.md` at `5e48dd7` (2026-07-09). That forced the method: a report about a corpus can only be judged at the size the corpus was, so everything was measured **both** against HEAD and against the archive build `32c10c5` (2026-05-24) and the report's own commit `59a9e0d`.
> **Report-rot: essentially zero, and unusually clean.** All seven §II design constants are **exact against the build it was written against** — `roll2hit-v3.html` at **14,377 lines** (the report's own headline figure, exact), **370** `MONSTER_POOL` entries, **66** `WORLD_DB` terrains, **76** `NODE_MAP` nodes, **107** `_S_DEFAULTS()` depth-1 fields, **41** `FROBERGER_JOURNAL` entries, **6** `NPC_DIALOGUES` keys — and the corpus figure *"37 Markdown documents"* is exact at `59a9e0d`. **The single wrong number is the single number it did not measure:** *"23 lab reports"*, cited twice, copied from `index.md`, which said 23 while **22** existed on disk. *Instrument 9's gradient with an unusually clean edge — the author's own measurements held, the citation did not.* **Two survival results worth keeping:** `FROBERGER_JOURNAL` is **41 at the archive and 41 at HEAD, the only design constant in the program that has not moved in 79 days**; and `_S_DEFAULTS()` survives at **104 of its 107 fields (97%)** across a 2.69× file growth and the §ARCH-01 migration, with only `hearthHome`, `lastCorridorCells` and `voidSignClicked` removed — the strongest structural survival the program has measured, and the one architectural prediction the report made that can be checked numerically.
> **THE FINDING — the bijection shipped and stopped being true, and it is visible on day one.** FC01 recommended a Doc Health badge *"visible at a glance"*; it shipped as `index.md` §"Doc Health Badge", and at HEAD every mechanical row is wrong and every row reads **✅**: HTML line count **36,933 vs 38,712** (+1,779), *"Lab reports on disk"* **85 vs 107** (+22), *"Lab reports in index"* **85** vs **87 unique filenames referenced** — that row wrong in *both* directions, with **26 reports on disk absent from the index** and **6 index rows naming files that do not exist**, all six deleted in one commit, **`120d617`**. `index.md` prints the repair procedure directly beneath the badge, so nothing is missing but the running of it: **a check that depends on a human running it reports green when nobody ran it.** The same failure produced the report's own lone error 79 days earlier. → **§DX-02s / `check:docindex`**, the cheapest gate in the family because every row is `wc -l`, `ls | wc -l` and a two-way set difference.
> **The sharpest single measurement — a bijection guarantees a link, not a truth.** FC05's shipped half works exactly as specified: `` `const S29_AUROS_THEORY =@27050` `` carries `// → doc: world.md §S29`, and §S29 exists and describes the scene. **Both ends state the trigger as `fav_auros >= 2`, and `fav_auros` occurs exactly once in 38,712 lines — in that comment.** It is not a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field and has no reader; the live guard is `_npcFavor('auros') >= 2` at `` `s29LineDelivered) {@32669` ``. `world.md` repeats the phantom field at **three** lines, having inherited it across the pointer. **No gate can reach either end** — #16 scans `*.md`, #13 phase 6 is comment-aware *by design* (the §AUDIT-03f lesson), and nothing validates a state-field name in prose. → **§AUDIT-03z (b)**.
> **FC02 closed by measurement after 79 days.** The report filed it as *"the count is verified (41 entries); the content comparison has not been done."* Doing it: **39 of 41 verbatim** after quote/whitespace normalisation, and entries **17** and **29** are entirely different text in the two artifacts (HTML 17 `MAN` *"The woman at the archive…"* vs txt `[Midlands — MI]`; HTML 29 `PDL` *"There is a question I should have asked…"* vs txt `[Island Settlement — IS]`). And `sources/froberger-journal-all-entries.txt` — a **live** home doc, named as such by the HTML's own FC05 pointer — is **outside every gate's universe**: `scripts/legacy-codes.js` drives from eleven `.md` paths and the string `.txt` does not occur in it, so all **41** entry headers still carry retired 26×16 codes while the HTML uses live ones. → **§AUDIT-03z (a)**.
> **Recommendation register (the most useful table in the rewrite):** FC01 ✅ · FC03 ✅ (`docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md` + `-economy.md`) · **FC05 half-shipped** — 93 `// → doc:` pointers live, 48 top-level CAPS consts still without one, doc half **0 commits ever**, superseded by §DX-01e's gate-enforced `` `symbol@line` `` anchors · **FC04's cadence never ran**, superseded in kind by this program · FC02 closed here. `index.md` records *"FC01–FC05 ✅ All complete 2026-05-25"*: **two of five closed without shipping** — right outcomes, wrong register, the same defect as the badge one level up, and exactly why §DOC-02 marks an unshipped claim **NOT SHIPPED** and keeps it.
> **Also filed: §DX-02s (b)** — two favor storage shapes and one gate grammar: `` `if (g.favorMin)@22064` `` reads `npcFavorability` only, while `` `fav_corelli: 0@23143` `` is a real field with its own writer (`` `S_story.fav_corelli = Math.min@31792` ``) and two readers, so **no quest can gate on Corelli's favor without a `_legacy_fn`** — invariant-#4 pressure with the declarative alternative already in the language (small call). **§DX-02n (e)** — `` `st.visited || st.visitedNodes@22055` ``: `visitedNodes` occurs once in the file, in that expression, has one commit ever, and was never a field at HEAD *or* in the archive — a **dead alternative in a fallback chain**, widening `check:deadconsts` a fourth time (after quest-entry fields, parameters and item objects), and invisible to both a top-level scan and a field scan because the identifier appears only as an operand. Corroborated **§AUDIT-03t** (HEAD also carries a new `act:0`, so the report's "8 acts" is now wrong in two directions) and **§AUDIT-03s**.
> **Small correction to a sibling:** §DOC-02b's HEAD `_S_DEFAULTS()` figure of **193** counts *lines that begin a top-level field*, and 87 of those lines declare more than one; the like-for-like depth-1 key count against the archive's 107 is **318**. Both are defensible measurements; they are not the same one.
> **Two recipes recorded as unreliable:** §II's own `grep -c "key:'"` returns **462** unscoped at the archive build (370 only within the `MONSTER_POOL` block) and **568/398** at HEAD — the recipe is correct block-scoped and wrong as written; `npm run stats` (§DX-01g) is the live replacement. And the report's §VI-C policy that lab reports *"are not updated after the fact"* is the one rule HEAD has **reversed in practice** — `CONTRIBUTING.md` upholds the narrow half (bare numbers and anchors in history are annotated, never rewritten) while §DOC-02 rewrites report prose against measurement; this document is the instance.

> **⟶ (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02g ✅ SHIPPED `70d49a0`: a code that RESOLVES is not a code that is RIGHT — and the lab report was the only source in the repo that had the history correct.** `lab-report-corelli-merchant.md` (Layer 61, the cross-act vendor), **193 → 196 lines** — the first increment that did not shrink, because the source was already short; the prose came down, a 12-row delta table and four findings went in. **Strongest survival yet: 19 of 20 identifiers resolve (95%)**, beating §DOC-02d — all 4 state fields, both helpers, all 5 item keys with every `readText` verbatim, `fav = min(3, purchase_count)`, the monotonic `count < index` gate, the three-tier opener — every one under its original name. **And 0 of 5 node codes are correct.** The mechanism survived a world migration untouched; the geography did not. **(1) The report is the correct record and two LIVE sources are wrong.** The engine comment above `CORELLI_APPEARANCES` and `world.md` both claim the old codes were `TL/RD/IS/WM/IN`; the archive shows **`DK/RD/BK/SQ/IN`** — the report's own list — and no header comment existed to misread until the repoint commit wrote one. First corpus correction where the wrong source is **the engine itself**; **new rule: a migration commit's comment is a claim about the past, written from memory rather than from the diff — check `git show <sha>^`.** And it is causal: believing stop 3 was `IS` is why `BK` was left alone. **(2) §AUDIT-03y — the `BK` collision, §AUDIT-03m's "worse than dead" class found for the first time in ENGINE DATA:** legacy `BK` = Broken Tooth Tavern, Visby = live `VBY`; `BK` still resolves, as a **Birka beach**, so `check:noderegs` passes while Corelli says *"Visby. Well."* and `NPC_DIALOGUE.BK` has Mordus say *"You're not from Visby"* — plus `'BK'` in `VENDOR_NODES` and 12 field references, with `VBY` in none of them. **(3)** `BK` also shares cell (10,197) with `LHR` and is non-primary (§AUDIT-03x), so stop 3 can never fire and **`encoded_letter` is unobtainable** — killing the retroactive decode, the feature the report's own post-mortem rated best; repointing to `VBY` fixes it **without** waiting on the design call. **(4) §AUDIT-03y (b)** — `false_warrant` is a **200gp purchase with no surface**: RETIRED not never-shipped (`git log -S`: shipped `194a810`, host `storyCorridorTravel` deleted by `85cc43e`/§CELL-11A), still sold at reachable stop 2, no Use button and no Read button. Third §AUDIT-03v/w-class instance in four increments — close all three with a detector. **The §DOC-02f internal gradient repeats exactly:** the transcribable is perfect — the *deleted* RD node verifies **5/5** against the archive — while the narrated is wrong, and **three of those were wrong the day it was written**: "modal" (always `storyMsg` + `insertAdjacentElement`), "remains readable" (`readableItems` filtered `type === 'readable'` at birth too), and **§4.2's pacing critique describes a shop that cannot exist** (stop 5 is `itemLabel:null` + `return`, at birth and now). **New instrument 10: a report's SELF-CRITICISM is a claim like any other — a post-mortem can be wrong about the thing it is apologising for.** Two-way sync in the same increment (engine comment + five `world.md` corrections + two ⚠️ callouts); filed **§AUDIT-03y** and **§DX-02q** (a `check:noderegs` phase for semantic collisions — the failure that passes "does it resolve?" perfectly). `check:anchors` **350 → 358 across 15 docs, all resolve**. **Next: `lab-report-crown-three-hags.md`.** *(Gate hazard re-confirmed: two `check:walk` runs in one Bash call reproduce the detach artifact every time — run it once, redirected to a file.)*

> **⟶ (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02f ✅ SHIPPED `ffe7720`: the accuracy of a design document is not uniform across it — the transcribed half had a zero error rate and the narrated half carried every delta, in one file by one author on one day.** On "continue", took `lab-report-circuit-map-theory.md` (696 lines, 2026-05-21, Layer 9 — Time-Warp Footpaths & Circuit Corridors) by the §DOC-02 ordering rule, re-derived from `stat`: oldest remaining mtime, alphabetically first in the shrinking 2026-06-09 block, and §DOC-02c's Layer-9 sibling as the row predicted. Rewrote it to **313 lines**, IEEE-shaped, 12-row delta table. **Existing-Work-First:** the uncommitted user recolor (HP-bar gradient / light-green playfield, self-labelled *"user recolor 2026-08-06"*) is still in the tree — **left untouched and unstaged**; this increment is docs-only. **Census: 24 of 50 named symbols resolve (48%)** — between §DOC-02c's 42% and §DOC-02e's 82% — and **0 of 26 node codes** resolve to the node they name, with `CI` the §AUDIT-03m worse-than-dead class again (live at `num:429`, "Chancery Court — The Officer's Pen", `act:NaN`). The corridor layer was deleted in three June passes (**§CELL-05** `6dea804`, **§CELL-11A** `85cc43e`, **§CELL-14** `30f18b4`) and superseded by §WALK/§NAV-01's 90×360 geo grid; Warp is now a **banned design** (invariant #3), `junction:true` a **CI failure**, and `storyMove`/`node.portal` both gone. Only one runtime fragment survives — `_setActivePath`@38195, amputated from three writes to two, still feeding the gold `mc-exit-active` exit arrow, which is the last item of the report's own §IX diagram. **The headline is not the removal, it is the internal gradient.** §IV–§V (data architecture + mesh construction) are **verbatim transcriptions with no measured error**: `WIRE_GLYPH` byte-identical in all eleven entries, `_routeSegments()` byte-identical, the seven junction coordinates exact including comment structure, and both `NODE_MAP` samples exact in *every* content field — the report's `CI` is HEAD's `LHR` (`num:1`, `name:'city'`, "City Streets — Birka", `npc:'City Guard Captain'`, `loot:'Bloodstained Map'`), the node survived and only the key was renamed. §VII (execution traces) is **narration**, and every trace-local delta lives there: `_corridorOnComplete`, `corridor-quest-count` and `corridor-pct` return **0 commits from `git log -S` across the entire repository history**; the shipped ids carry a `-name` suffix the report drops on all three; the `pendingBattle` shape is wrong in **3 of 4 fields** (`isCorridor` and the `'_corridor'` sentinel also 0 commits); and the encounter formula is missing a whole term — shipped `min(0.95, 0.1 + notoriety*0.015 + activeQuestCount*0.04)` vs the report's `min(0.9, 0.1 + activeQuestCount*0.05)`, so all five rows of its probability table are wrong. **This refines §DOC-02b's *"the half that points at code is the half that is right"* rather than confirming it: §VII points at code too — functions, DOM ids, line numbers — and it is the wrong half. The real predictor is whether the author could COPY the passage.** A table or a function body can be pasted; a ten-step call sequence must be reconstructed, and memory supplies plausible names the file never contained. **Sharpest single measurement: the Hunt/Warp dialog was built, styled, and never once shown.** At `32c10c5` the overlay, card and all three buttons exist with the exact labels the report quotes down to the emoji; every JS reference is two bulk close-all arrays and one `classList.remove('visible')` — **nothing ever adds `visible`**, no handler is bound, no field is written. The real `storyCorridorTravel` branches on the persistent `S_story.huntMode` toggle and emits a `storyMsg` either way, so there was never a per-leg choice: **the report's central thesis, *"the choice is the mechanic"*, is the one thing the layer never implemented.** Same verdict §TIMELESS-01 later recorded for the Stalk modal (*"already never shown — legacy/dead"*), reached independently, in the sibling report, about a different screen. **Corpus cross-check corrected a shipped §DOC-02 output for the first time.** The two Layer-9 reports disagree on `CI`, and the archive settles it in *this* report's favour: "The Thieves' Den" was never a node label but `HUNTING_GROUNDS['city'].displayName` — a table from §DOC-02c's own thesis — so that report's Appendix A conflated a terrain display name with a node label (`AL`/`BQ` are likewise reworded rather than transcribed). Also softened **§DOC-02c delta 7**: `targetTerrain`/`targetKeys` never existed as fields (correct), but the coupling **shipped derived** — `_getQuestTargetKeys()` walks active quests to `NODE_MAP[q.activateNode].battle.key` and `_stalkedMonsterPick` applies `BOOST = 6`, so Principle 3 did run. *A missing field is not a missing feature.* **New standing method, now accelerator six: HEAD cannot adjudicate a claim about the past — read the archive.** Both corrections came from `git show 32c10c5:roll2hit-v3.html`, not HEAD; `git log -S` returning zero proves non-existence, absence at HEAD proves nothing about 2026-05-21. **Filed §AUDIT-03x** — traced from the report's own §VIII Step-2 rule (*"no two nodes should share the same (r,c)"*), which §WALK-1.5 deliberately retired in favour of **locales**: **416 nodes occupy 244 cells, so 172 are non-primary** (worst cell holds 17), `S_story.currentCode` is assigned at exactly **two** sites and both yield the primary, `cellCodes()` is read only by the MUD kernel, and `_uqfActivateAtNode` keys on `node.code` — so **1,260 quests target a non-primary node, 486 already held by the §AUDIT-03e guard, leaving 774 across 135 nodes that cannot activate** (`BK` 89, sharing a cell with the starting node `LHR`; `WM` 312 behind `ERF`). The waypoint code already compensates by cell and tells the player they arrived at a node the engine then declines to render. Design call; overlaps §AUDIT-03d and probably sequences before it. Also filed a sixth **§DX-02n** member (`_setActivePath`'s dead `toCode` parameter — a dead *parameter*, invisible to a top-level scan, widening `check:deadconsts` again). **Gates: 16/16 green, but only after a repair this pass had to make first.** `check:walk` hit the known harness detach artifact again (stalled after `check:behaviour` with its ✓ printed), so per the standing rule the remaining gates were re-run individually — and `check:anchors` came back **red with 7 dead anchors, 5 of them left by §DOC-02e**, whose ship record says 16/16 green. None of the six symbols had actually moved: every line existed, unchanged, at its cached number. **An anchor whose text is not a *literal* substring of its line is DEAD even when the line is alive**, and the three ways to mint one are markdown table pipe-escaping (`\|\|` for `||`), an ellipsis standing in for elided code, and whitespace normalisation (`const d20  =` has two spaces). All 7 shortened to verbatim fragments; `check:anchors` now **348 anchors across 14 docs, 0 dead**. *Two lessons recorded in the §DOC-02g accelerators: shorten an anchor, never prettify it; and when the detach artifact fires, the gates that did not run are not green.* Docs-only; no HTML edit; the 935/4 (940) Playwright baseline is untouched. **Stated scope limit:** liveness and provenance only — the 24 surviving symbols were not audited for *behavioural* drift. **Next: §DOC-02g — `lab-report-corelli-merchant.md`, then the rest of the 2026-06-09 block alphabetically.**

> **⟶ (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02e ✅ SHIPPED `3d0ab1d`: the first report in the program that was RIGHT about something HEAD still gets wrong — and the first pair of reports caught disagreeing with each other.** On "continue", took `lab-report-ceremonia-roll-skill-checks.md` (537 lines, 2026-05-26, §DESIGN-03) by the §DOC-02 ordering rule — oldest remaining by mtime, alphabetically first in the shrinking 2026-06-09 block, re-derived from `stat` as the preamble requires. Rewrote it to **308 lines**, IEEE-shaped, with the standard spec→shipped delta table (17 deltas). **Census: 50 of 61 named symbols resolve (82%); 0 of 6 node codes; all 9 quests live; all fifteen Yael prose strings verbatim.** The headline shape is **content outliving container** — 100% of the prose survives, 0% of the field names do, the exact inverse of §DOC-02b's *"the half of a document that points at code is the half that is right."* §ARCH-01 retired all nine flat `check*`/`*Award`/`complete*` fields in one pass and re-expressed the type as a `bits:[{kind:'skill_check'}]` opcode; `_rollCeremonia`'s specified 6-step body survives line-for-line inside `_resolveQuestUQF`@6962 under a different name. **7 skill-check quests specified → 2,453 at HEAD.** **Finding 1, and the reason this increment matters to the program's design:** §7 of the report specified *"show the level-up modal immediately"* on a passing roll, and **HEAD does not do it** — `_checkLevelUp()` is a mutator that advances `S_story.level` and queues the level, the pass branch never drains the queue, and `_grantExplorationXp` hard-resets it on any step onto new ground, so the player keeps the level and loses its HP roll, ASI and feature. The *failing* branch, added later by §XP-01, does it correctly three lines away. Filed **§DX-02p**; blast radius is all 2,453 skill-check quests. **Until now every delta pointed at report-rot; this one points at engine-rot, which means the delta table is a two-way instrument and the program should stop assuming HEAD is the reference.** **Finding 2:** this report and §DOC-02d's were written four days apart against the same world and **disagree on 3 of 6 node codes** — `BA` is City Streets here and the Rough Bar there. The engine resolved every `activateNode` from the prose rather than the code, so the codes were never load-bearing, only misleading. *A node code in a design doc is a citation, not an identifier.* Also filed **§AUDIT-03w** (the courier guard quotes a 20gp bribe with `onFail:[]` behind it — §AUDIT-03v's class, second instance in two increments, so it wants a detector not a third hand-filed row) and **three additions to §DX-02n** (`ceremoniaYaelAct` 5 writes/0 readers, whose five `_legacy_fn` writers are a free invariant-#4 cleanup; `skillCheckAttempts[].failures`; and **`q.xpAward` — 53 occurrences, 33 on UQF quests, 0 readers**, the undocumented twin of the self-labelled-dead `q.reward`, which moves the proposed `check:deadconsts` scope from top-level consts to **quest-entry fields**). Docs-only; no HTML edit. `check:walk` **16/16 green (exit 0)** — note the empty-output/timeout artifact recurred, and the **exit code is the authority, not the captured stdout**. **Next: §DOC-02f — `lab-report-circuit-map-theory.md`, §DOC-02c's Layer-9 sibling (expect the same retired corridor symbols; census will be fast).**
