> **⟶ (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02d ✅ SHIPPED `bb9bd58`: the strongest survival in the program — 91% of its symbols resolve, 0% of its node codes do, and the report is the traceable origin of an identifier defect that later cost a whole audit row.** On "continue", took `lab-report-birka-beginner-arc.md` (345 lines, 2026-05-22) by the §DOC-02 ordering rule — the oldest remaining file in the 2026-06-09 bulk block, alphabetically first after a/b/c, re-derived from `stat` as the preamble requires. **Existing-Work-First:** the same uncommitted user recolor is still in the tree (HP-bar gradient / light-green playfield / theme-ink die numerals, self-labelled *"user recolor 2026-08-06"*) — **left untouched and unstaged**, this increment is docs-only; every number re-measured from HEAD, never read off a doc table. **Method was §DOC-02c's four-part census** (batched `grep -c` symbol census · node-code resolution against `docs/maps/node-index.md` · mechanism re-read at each live definition · `git log -S` history probe on every dead symbol). **The headline inverts §DOC-02c exactly: 29 of 32 named symbols resolve — 91% — while all 8 node codes are dead as written.** Every state field shipped under its specified name (`npcFavorability`, `roughWhiskeyUsed`, `yaelEscortUsed`), every function under its specified name (`_setNpcFavor`, `_npcFavor`, `_renderNpcCard`), six of seven quests shipped as UQF-1.0, all three gold rewards exact (80/40/60), all seven items live, and **both `_setNpcFavor` log lines are verbatim at HEAD** — *"looks at you differently now"* / *"says your name when you walk in"*. The mechanism then **outgrew its own spec by an order of magnitude**: §NPC-01-B derived the card-render map from `BIRKA_NPC_PROFILES` rather than hand-listing it, taking the relationship surface from ~20 NPCs to **204 profiles / 213 dialogues** with no change to the contract this report defined. **So the failure mode here is not rot; it is naming — and that is the row's reusable finding.** Five of the eight node codes were **born dead**, and the engine says so in its own source at `` `const birkaNpcs@35134` ``: *"No NODE_MAP entry ever existed for CI/IN/TV/BA/CY, so these cards previously rendered nowhere"* (§AUDIT-03p class, fixed by §PLAY-01-D/G; `CI` is now live as an entirely different node, Chancery Court `num:429`, so it passes every *"does this code exist?"* check while the sentence stays wrong). **And the report minted the identifier split §AUDIT-03n later had to repair:** it introduces the bard as *"Bard Tomas Couperin"* and then calls him **Quill** fourteen times, so he shipped as three identifiers at once — key `quill`, `name:'Bard Tomas Couperin'`, quest `quest_couperin_lute`; *"Pit Master Weckmann"* shipped under the key **`crov`**, a token the report never mentions; and the heading **"THE SIX BIRKA NPCs"** is followed by **five** profile sections, with Auros (`name:'Commander Seraphine Bruhns'`) never characterised and listed in the implementation notes as *"Auros-extended"*. §AUDIT-03n measured the bill fourteen months of engine-time later: seven registries keyed to the surnames the favor ledger never writes, **21 unreachable entries**, plus the victory screen's own `npcOrder` showing the **stranger** epilogue for Quill, Weckmann and Bruhns at any favor. *Durable lesson: a design doc that uses two names for one character mints two keys — the engine cannot tell they are the same person, and a favor written under one is invisible to a gate reading the other. Fix the name in the doc before the doc becomes the schema.* **Seventeen deltas recorded; four are the interesting kind.** (1) **`quest_drunk_fight` was never a quest and is left dangling** in `_hasActiveQuestFor`'s `npcQuests` — filed as §DX-02o. (2) **Two of the arc's three specified gates were never authored:** pit training's *"requires 1+ Birka quest complete"* and Void Below's *"requires Auros AND Weckmann both Friendly"* both ship `gate:{}` — while a **sibling quest in the same block** (`quest_brynn_firewood`) *does* gate on `favorMin:{brynn:1}`, so this is an authoring omission, not a missing capability, and the arc's finale is available on arrival. (3) **The drunk pit fight shipped its mechanics exactly and its consequences not at all:** `` `adv = 'dis'@25044` ``, `` `const drunkBonus = S_story._drunkFight ? 3 : 0@25071` ``, the one-time latch and the item consumption are all verbatim, but the nat-20 flavor line and Weckmann's *loss* reaction have **0 commits ever**, and because `_drunkFight` is consumed only in `_storyBattleVictory`, **losing the drunk fight produces no reaction at all**; the promised early Pit Legend Token also never existed (the branch grants favor and nothing else). (4) **The report's Step 4, "New Information", has no scene** — Auros's rune-tablet reading (*"the second district boundary… we are currently at the third"*) returns 0 hits and 0 commits, so the arc's thematic payload was written and never built. Two further deltas are expansions rather than losses: the three favorability states became **four tiers** (`impartial · questActive · friendly · dearFriend`) and §NPC-01-D added a second route to Friendly with no quest at all (`` `TALK_TO_FRIENDLY = 3@23513` `` talks on distinct game-days), and the two authored Rough Whiskey reactions became **eighteen** (six NPCs × three tiers) behind a *second* flag, `roughWhiskeyActive`. **What held is the whole design half, and it is load-bearing at HEAD:** favorability as a small integer with permanent visible effects; the dear-friend "second personal act", formalised as a six-entry `dearFriendBits` table whose every member is an act this report invented; and the Sweelinck payoff, which shipped **stronger** than specified — `_birkaVar` is resolved first and overrides all four curse-score bands, so at three or more friends the Birka variant is unconditional. **Its thesis is executable:** 8 of `_missionComplete`'s 12 bits are Birka relationship acts and the ending needs 8 of 12, so *"Not experience points. Not gold. Five names"* is a threshold in the shipped game, not a mood. **Three defects filed. §AUDIT-03u:** two player-facing strings still say the world has **forty-two nodes** — the Map Shop's Real Map (`` `STN:{ num:9@8636` ``) and **Yael's Level-1 tutorial monologue** (`` `yael: { meta:@10397` ``), the game's first paragraph of instruction — against **416** live; forty-two was the report-era battleground count in the retired 26×16 space, and the fix should describe scale without a literal rather than swap in a number that will rot the same way. **§AUDIT-03v:** Brynn promises a free room in `TLL`'s node text *and* as `quest_brynn_ledger`'s reward, and `storySleep` charges `sleepCost:5` both times, because the only free-lodging exemption is hardcoded to a different node and a different flag (`` `node.code === 'INN' && S_story.freeBookingUnlocked@36208` ``) — a double-*promise* with no payer; small design call, grant the nights or rewrite both strings. **§DX-02o:** the quest-id reference gate — **302 distinct `quest_*` literals in the file, 301 resolve, 1 does not**, and no gate can see it, because `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself and `check:noderegs` mentions `QUEST_DB` zero times; wants a `check:questrefs` phase with explicit `NOT_A_QUEST_ID` classification (the two legitimate `startsWith` prefixes and the `quest_item` type string). **Evidence:** report **345 → 280 lines**, IEEE-shaped (abstract · method · as-built inventory · 17-row delta table · node-code resolution · the naming section · defects · what it got right · scope note · Appendix A keeping every NOT SHIPPED line verbatim), **50 new `symbol@line` anchors**; `check:anchors` **284 anchors across 12 docs, 0 stale**; `check:legacycodes` **OK**; `npm run check:walk` reached its final `&&` link on the piped run — **all 16 gates green** — while the redirect form hung at 0% CPU, the known harness detach artifact, so **use the piped form**. **Docs-only: no HTML, no tests, no §7½ — the 935/4 (940) Playwright baseline stands untouched.** **Next: §DOC-02e** — `lab-report-ceremonia-roll-skill-checks.md`, then the rest of the 2026-06-09 block alphabetically (`circuit-map-theory` remains §DOC-02c's sibling — same Layer-9 subject, so its census will be fast and its delta table will overlap).

> **⟶ (2026-08-11) — §DOC-02c ✅ SHIPPED `cc4a07e`: the first report in the program that fails as a description of the engine — and it fails at the thesis, not at the edges.** On "continue", took `lab-report-battleground-circuit-path-quest.md` (889 lines, 2026-05-21) by the §DOC-02 ordering rule — the oldest remaining file in the 2026-06-09 bulk block, alphabetically first after a/b, re-derived from `stat` as the preamble requires. **Existing-Work-First:** the same uncommitted user recolor is still in the tree (HP-bar gradient / light-green playfield / theme-ink die numerals, self-labelled *"user recolor 2026-08-06"*) — **left untouched and unstaged**, this increment is docs-only; every number re-measured from HEAD, never read off a doc table. **Method was §DOC-02b's four-part census plus a fourth instrument this row adds to the program: a `git log -S` history probe on every symbol the census marks dead.** That probe is the only way to separate **RETIRED** (shipped, then removed — the report was true when written) from **NOT SHIPPED** (never existed under that name — the report was written from intent, not from the file), and *a report cannot make that distinction about itself.* **The headline inverts §DOC-02b exactly: 16 of 38 named symbols resolve — 42% survival against b's 91% — and of the 49 node codes it tabulates, none resolves to the node it names.** All three components carrying the thesis are gone. §TIMELESS-01 (`7952752`, 2026-06-26) deleted `HUNTING_GROUNDS` and the whole Stalk mechanic, leaving two tombstone comments; §WALK/§NAV-01 deleted all seven corridor symbols (`CORRIDOR_CELLS`, `CORRIDOR_TERRAIN`, `buildCorridorMap`, `_routeSegments`, `storyCorridorTravel`, `_wireGlyph`, `_corridorOnComplete`) **and** the `N`/`S`/`E`/`W` compass fields the wire mesh was drawn from — so the corridor layer had no graph left to render even had it survived. **Twelve deltas recorded; three are the interesting kind.** (1) **The §IX-C "implementation" listing never existed:** `git log -S` returns **0 commits ever** for `stalkModal`, `#story-stalk-overlay` and `#stalk-terrain-name`, while the feature they describe shipped three days later (`32c10c5`, 2026-05-24) under entirely different names — `storyStalk(nodeCode)`, `#story-stalk-modal`, `#btn-stalk-wait` — and §TIMELESS-01's own removal inventory records that shipped modal as *"already never shown — legacy/dead."* (2) **Two of its specified designs are now forbidden, not merely absent:** the Warp travel mode ("instant transit, no encounter") is banned by hard invariant #3, stated in the engine itself at `` `no jump travel. checkpointNode@26048` ``, and the junction nodes J1–J7 became a **CI failure** (`check:invariants` I1/I2, §DX-01a) with the tool that minted them deprecated and refused (§DX-01d). (3) **`targetTerrain` and `targetKeys` have 0 hits at HEAD *and in history*** — the quest-to-terrain coupling of §V, which is the report's own Principle 3 and the load-bearing half of its "closed information loop", **never had data to run on**; quests are UQF-1.0 (§ARCH-01). Two further formulas measured **invented rather than stale**, the same class §DOC-02b found in the same subject area: the quest-scaled `min(0.90, 0.10 + activeQuests × 0.05)` has no expression at any point (rate is per-terrain data, `` `const TERRAIN_ENCOUNTER_RATE@9892` ``), and the fixed tier vector `{35,35,25,4,1}` matches **no band** of the live 6-band `` `function _notorietyWeights@38207` `` — not even the lowest (`40/35/20/4/1`). **The most reusable finding is a name collision.** §KG-01 (`8168f0e`, 2026-07-08) introduced a **different mechanic under the retired feature's name** twelve days after §TIMELESS-01 removed it: live "Hunt Mode" is a d-pad toggle that doubles the wilderness encounter rate to a 0.8 cap with an 80/20 bias toward monsters at or below player level (`` `huntMode: false@23083` ``, `` `function _monsterLevel@38226` ``), sharing nothing with the corridor Hunt/Warp dialog but the word. A reader who greps `huntMode` from this report lands on a mechanic it does not describe. *Durable lesson: a retired feature's vocabulary is not free to re-use — the doc that named it does not get updated, so the collision is silent and permanent.* **What held, for 82 days and 6.1× the file:** `WORLD_DB`'s `{label, icon, monsters}` shape byte-exact with monster **object references** pre-resolved (no string lookup at encounter time, exactly as specified); `NODE_MAP.name` as the terrain key — the one structural relation every later track preserved; all 10 `NodeEntry` fields; the five-tier vocabulary, now the CI-pinned §DX-02g contract; and the story-vs-grinding XP taxonomy, whose *distinction* survives even though the battleground layer between them does not. **One node code is worse than dead:** `CI` resolves to a live but entirely different node (`num:429`, "Chancery Court — The Officer's Pen"), so every *"does this code exist?"* check passes while the sentence stays wrong — the §AUDIT-03m hazard class in its purest form. **Two defects filed. §AUDIT-03t: 36 of 416 nodes (8.7%) carry `act:NaN`**, found by checking the report's own `NodeEntry` typedef claim *"`act` — story act (1–8)"*, and the three readers fail three different ways without throwing — `` `S_story.actNumber = node.act || 1@34568` `` silently act-gates all 36 as **Act 1** (NaN is falsy), `` `ACT_NAMES[node.act]@34597` `` indexes a 9-element array with NaN so the act badge renders **"— undefined —"** and `#s-node-act` renders the bare word **"undefined"**, and the map panel renders **"Act NaN"**; no gate can see this, because `check:noderegs` checks that node *references* resolve, never that a node's own scalar fields are well-formed. **§DESIGN-04: the thesis question has no successor surface** — `HUNTING_GROUNDS` was deleted and nothing replaced it, so with 398 monsters across 111 terrains and 416 nodes there is no bestiary, no terrain index, no habitat surface, and the "lost in a field" failure mode the report was written to eliminate is by its own criteria unaddressed; Growing the grammar for one consumer would be the `itemsMinAny` failure, so both moved to **`NODE_HOOKS` verbatim** (G2's method — line-multiset diff, zero unmatched lines) until G4c-FU ask 2 answers the chrome question. > **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-04) — §VM-01-G4c ✅ SHIPPED `4c2a831`: the button mode gets its first consumers, and "13 D1 verbs" measured down to 4.** On "continue" took the board's next-up (1), the only row on it needing no design call. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `81908c1`, one other claude process, `:1367` verified **free** the whole session (Hazard #1 — this slice hand-edits inline JS), and the four blocks were read inline in the live file before anything moved. **Shipped:** four D1 surfaces as `NODE_VERBS` button entries — Sweelinck's S49 scene at `NUE`, Ori at `STN`, Yva at `TRD`, Brynn's firewood at `TLL` — plus the Junction Vignette's `[Help — 10gp]` converted **in place** to the `cost` leaf through a new `_uqfRunChain(bits)` (no mount, no re-render, `storyBlock` on a refusal), because that surface's lines are drawn once per node by `JUNCTION_VIGNETTES` and a re-render would replace them with *"…has moved on"*. That is the G4 design pass's **stated bar for `cost` not being single-use**, and it is met: two live consumers, two prices, two surfaces. **The finding is the count, and it is why this row is not the whole of G4c.** The lab report listed **13** D1 surfaces and sliced them as one unit. Read against the *shipped grammar* rather than against the census, **only 4 are expressible today**; the other 9 are each blocked by a shape the VM does not have — **a delayed second beat** (Harbor Board · Solvak probe · Seal delivery each end `setTimeout(() => storyMsg(…), 400–800)`, and since `storyMsg` **replaces** the single message slot, the first line is *authored to be destroyed*; `narrative` carries no timing and the kernel is pure, so it cannot schedule), **panel chrome** (Surge Lock · Froberger letter · Codex Inquisitor each hand-build a `.sweelinck-variant` with six independent style knobs — a byte-identical generic panel would be a CSS struct smuggled into a vocabulary), and **a ceremonia launch** (the Inquisitor's button *starts* a check, which is exactly why the `skill_check` scope fence needs no widening — but no bit starts one). Answering any of those inside this slice would be the `itemsMinAny` failure the track exists to prevent, so all three are filed as **§VM-01-G4c-FU** with a recommendation each. **Three schema growths, each with a measured reason:** **`group`** names a *dispatch position* — four blocks in four source positions cannot share one call site, and LIFO stacking is preserved only by dispatching where the block sat; it is §9.1's own D3 word, so G4d inherits it, and **every group has exactly one call site and every call site names a live group, asserted**, because an entry with no call site renders **nowhere** (§PLAY-01-G's silent rot, in a new place). **`bits` may be `fn(st)`** — the same `string | fn` shape `ambient` and `when` already have in this registry, for the two sites whose narrative the inline handler assembled at click (Yva gains a paragraph once the Harrow is solved; `_getS49SweelinckScene()` closes differently on NG+); the VM's grammar is untouched, `execBits` still receives a plain declarative array. **`btnStyle`, and a label-only verb IS its button — no wrapper**, which is the one the *evidence* caught rather than the design: the migrated blocks inserted a **bare** button afterend of `#story-text-box`, whose parent is a **flex column**, so a direct-child button is stretched to full width while the identical button inside a mount shrinks to its own text. **The golden-DOM capture reported that as identical** — same tag, class, label, style — **and the §7½ screenshot showed a full-width bar on one side and a small left-aligned chip on the other.** *A DOM diff proves markup, not layout*; the bounding box is part of the capture now. One correction to the design sketch: it wrote `{kind:'favor', npc:'yva', add:1}`, but `_setNpcFavor` writes an **absolute** level and only ever raises, so `add:1` would have **lowered** a favor already at 2 — shipped as `set:1`, pinned by a test. **Evidence, in the order it was built.** (a) A **23-combo golden capture, HEAD vs after**, over DOM markup *and* bounding box, message text, message class, gold, hp, inventory, favor, quests and flags: **11 byte-identical · 5 differ ONLY by the `id` the button now carries** (every "offered" state plus the unaffordable refusal — box included, so the four migrated surfaces are **pixel-identical**) **· 7 carry one delta, and it is G4b's, already named**: the driver re-renders when a chain ends, so a quest the verb satisfies **completes in the same beat** rather than on the next arrival. It is paid **exactly once** either way — §VM-01-G3 moved those payouts into the quests' own `onComplete` for precisely this reason — and the migration *recovers* two narratives the inline handlers destroyed (`_setNpcFavor`'s `🤝 …looks at you differently now.` used to overwrite Yva's entire speech; `storyCheckQuests`' own messages used to overwrite the firewood's). All four DUS states and all three junction states are byte-identical, including the `msg-block` shake class on a refused price — which is what proves the price conversion is a no-op and not a rewrite. (b) **§7½ eyeballed**: 9 screenshots of the live game, **6 byte-identical PNGs**, the 3 that differ being the click outcomes; this is where the flex-column layout bug was found, *after* the DOM diff had said "identical". (c) **`uqf-node-verbs-d1.test.js` 13/13, with 7 of 13 failing at HEAD and the 6 that pass exactly the ones asserting *unchanged* behaviour** (Yva's full chain, the favor floor, Sweelinck's NG+ closing, both junction states) — G2b's honest shape rather than G4b's all-red one, because most of this surface is supposed to be identical. Plus `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** with `check:questparity` at **25,030 bytes — the kernel was not touched again**; full Playwright **839 passed / 4 failed** (the run reported 838/5 and the fifth was the known `multiplayer-presence:171` flake, which passes **12/12** on its own). **Two pins updated, and both were right to exist:** `uqf-node-verbs.test.js`'s *"the DUS trio is the whole table"* becomes *"the head of the table"*, and **`uqf-quest-activation.test.js:120`, which pinned that clicking Ori paid *nothing at click time*** — a timing artifact of the inline handler, not the property the test was written for. The property is *"+300gp exactly once, from the quest chain"*, and it is now asserted against a **further** render. **Pin the property, never the incident.** Docs: lab report **§12⅞** + 2 new anchors (gate #15 caught the old `_renderNodeVerbs` anchor as a **dead symbol** the moment its signature grew `group` — exactly what a symbol anchor is for), `quest.md` §"the button verb", `prompt.md` §4 + §7 (baseline 826→**839**), `anchors:fix`. **Next: G4d** — CDG's D3 concurrent menu as grouped `NODE_VERBS` with `combat` bits, the two Class-E strays (Kenickie's market, the Lower Archive launcher) to `NODE_HOOKS` verbatim, and the la_riva delivery that shares their container. Then **§VM-01-G4c-FU**, once its three asks are answered.

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-04) — §VM-01-G4b ✅ SHIPPED `f7a60a5`: the first `choice` in the game's history, and the schema grew one mode because a choice has no button.** On "continue" took the board's next-up (1), the only row on it needing no design call. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `5d64e89`, one other claude process, `:1367` verified **free** the whole session (Hazard #1 — this slice hand-edits inline JS), and `grep -c NODE_VERBS` returned **0** in both `roll2hit-v3.html` and `js/quest.js`, so the row's premise held. **Shipped:** `NODE_VERBS` + `_renderNodeVerbs` — the third small vocabulary beside `NODE_PANELS` (flavour a node *shows*) and `NODE_HOOKS` (an interface a node *owns*) — and Kern & Sable's **76 inline lines** at DUS as its three entries (Q-NEXUS-00/01/02, the Torment Nexus overture). **The design finding is that §9.1's locked schema was for the wrong half of Class D.** The lab report locked `{id, nodes, when, label, bits}` — a **button** verb, which is the D1 shape it was measured against. A **D2 exclusive choice has no button**: the options *are* the surface. Giving Kern & Sable a `label` would have inserted a click the player never had to make, so the entry's mode is decided by what it carries — **`label`+`bits`** = a button that runs the chain on click · **`bits` with no `label`** = the chain IS the surface, so it runs at render and its first bit **must** park on a `choice` · **`ambient`** alone = a flavour line for a state with nothing to do. That third mode is where the slice earned its keep: running a chain at render is exactly how side effects land on every draw of a node, so a label-less non-suspending chain is refused with a warning — **and refused BEFORE its mount is created.** The first implementation refused *after* inserting the mount and the test caught it: **a refusal that leaves an empty div behind is not a refusal, it is a page that changed anyway.** **Two behaviour deltas, both named in the diff rather than discovered in play:** (1) the driver re-renders when a chain ends, so the Q-NEXUS-01 follow-up now appears **in the same beat** as [Ask] instead of on the next arrival, and the aftermath line lands the moment Q-NEXUS-02 completes — the fiction supports it (they are still at the counter, waiting), it is asserted in the test file, and `story.md`'s *"next `DUS` visit"* wording is **kept and annotated as authored intent** rather than rewritten; (2) the **empty 8px spacer div** the inline block always inserted — visible as dead space in the "listened and walked away" state — is gone. **Evidence, in the order it was built.** (a) An **authored-string multiset diff** between HEAD's block and the new table: **14 vs 14, identical, nothing added or lost**, with the three ambient lines unwrapped from the `innerHTML` wrapper the old code hand-built. This is G2b's line-multiset method adapted to a block that **could not** move verbatim — its shape changed, so only its content can be pinned, and pinning the content is what proves no prose was retyped from a screenshot. (b) A **golden-DOM diff of all four DUS states** against HEAD (temp harness, deleted): text and button labels **byte-identical, including the 6px/4px button spacing** — `_uqfRenderAsk` was given the first-button 6px that every hand-written option panel in the file uses, so the migration is pixel-identical rather than merely equivalent. What remains is the mount's `id`, a `data-uqf-option` attribute, one redundant wrapper `<div>` + `<em>` dropped from an already-italic `.npc-ambient` (CSS 2122 — zero pixels), and delta 2. (c) **`uqf-node-verbs.test.js` 10/10, with all 10 failing at HEAD.** That is deliberately **not** G2b's "some tests pass both ways" shape, and saying so is the honest part: the surface's DOM identity changed with the migration, so the before/after equivalence has to live in the golden diff, not in the after-state contract — and the test file says that in its own header. Plus `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** with `check:questparity` at **25,030 bytes — the kernel was not touched**, this slice is entirely host code (G4a spent the track's one parity-fence edit); full Playwright **826 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four; and **§7½ eyeballed** in the running game — all three states screenshotted, the sidebar ITEMS counter ticking **0→1** as the token lands through `reward`, and the newline-collapsing in `#story-move-msg` checked against HEAD and confirmed **pre-existing** (`storyMsg` sets `textContent` on an element with no `pre-wrap`, before and after). One thing factored rather than duplicated: `_mkAmbientLine` is now the single definition a `choice` prompt and a `NODE_VERBS` `ambient` both use, so they cannot drift. Docs: lab report **§12¾** + 5 new anchors, `quest.md` §"`NODE_VERBS` — where a verb is authored", `story.md` §Q-NEXUS + `world.md` annotated, `prompt.md` §4 + §7 (baseline 816→**826**), `anchors:fix` for the 113 hints my +39 net HTML lines drifted. **Next: G4c** — the 13 D1 verbs → `NODE_VERBS` (the button mode now has a registry to land in) plus converting the Junction Vignette `[Help — 10gp]` to `cost`, which is the parent's stated bar for the leaf not being single-use. Then G4d (CDG's concurrent menu + the two Class-E strays → `NODE_HOOKS`).

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-04) — §VM-01-G4a ✅ SHIPPED `b905733`: the `choice` opcode has a host end at last, and the `cost` leaf says the price out loud.** On "continue" the user answered the one ASK the G4 design lock was waiting on — **(a) refuse-at-click**, the recommendation — which unblocked the only row on the whole board that needed no further design call. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `32845cd`, `:1367` verified free the whole session (Hazard #1 — this slice hand-edits inline JS), and greps for `_uqfRunVerb` / `NODE_VERBS` / `cost` returned **nothing**, so the row's premise held. **Zero content moved, exactly as §10 sliced it** — G4a is the seam, G4b is its first consumer. **Shipped, kernel side (`js/quest.js`, inside QUEST:CORE → re-inlined, parity green at 25,030 bytes):** the **`cost`** opcode, registered next to `reward` because it is `reward`'s inverse and because `reward` is precisely how you get this wrong — `gold:-50` computes correctly with no affordability test, no refusal and the word *reward* on a price, Hazard #2's *a write into a real-but-wrong object never throws*. Contract `{gold?, resource?, count?, refuse?}`; **every currency is tested before any is spent**, so a mixed price can never part-pay — a property the design report measured toward but never named, and the one a naive leaf breaks silently. The leaf needed a mechanism the VM did not have — **chain failure**: a handler may set `ctx._halt` and `execBits` breaks on it. It is **deliberately never cleared in the loop**, because `ctx` is shared with the nested `execBits` a `choice` option runs: clearing it would let the bits *after* an unpaid branch run on. One `ctx` per run is what makes a sticky flag safe, and both drivers build a fresh one. **Shipped, host side (outside the fence, beside `_uqfPump`):** `_uqfRunVerb`/`_uqfRenderAsk` — the half Inc A never wrote. An `{ask:'choice'}` envelope now renders one button per option into the verb's own mount, resumes the **same** generator with the picked index, and on completion removes the mount and re-renders the node with the chain's narrative as `storyRender`'s **prefix** (`_resolveQuestUQF`'s route — the tail `storyMsg` would otherwise overwrite it, §BOARD-01-FU6). Two properties designed in rather than discovered: `storyRender` **abandons** a pending generator beside the sweep that takes its panel's DOM (Inc A's single slot assumed *"a choice resolves within one interaction turn"*; a NODE choice can sit across a render — and abandoning writes nothing, because `choice` applies the picked option's bits only *after* the pick), and a stale option button **re-checks that the slot still holds its own generator**, so a click on a survived panel is inert rather than resuming a dropped chain. **The scope fence is untouched:** a `choice` inside `skill_check`'s `onPass`/`onFail` still throws, so a `require('./quest')` server still needs no host driver. **Evidence:** new `uqf-verb-driver.test.js` **12/12**, and **the positive control is the shape that matters — against HEAD 10 of 12 fail, and the 2 that pass are exactly the two asserting *unchanged* behaviour** (a chain with no `cost` runs every bit; a `choice` in a skill-check branch still throws). `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** (`check:questparity` byte-identical both ways). Full Playwright **816 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four. **The first run reported 815/5, and the fifth red was mine and was correct**: `quest-runtime-uqf.test.js:28` pins the entire `HANDLERS` key list precisely so a new opcode cannot enter the table unrecorded — adding `cost` to the pin is part of the fix, not a concession to it, and it is the fourth time this repo has been told something by a test rather than by a grep. **No §7½** — nothing player-visible changed, because nothing calls `_uqfRunVerb` yet and `cost` has no consumer until G4c; the whole point of the slice is that it is provable before a single block moves. Docs: lab report **§12½ addendum** (the ASK answered + what shipped), `prompt.md` §4 (the `cost` + driver contracts, incl. *`cost` never belongs in a `when`*) and §7 (baseline 804→**816**), **`quest.md` — a new "Prices and choices" section** so an author reaching for a price finds the leaf instead of writing the three inline lines a sixth time, and `anchors:fix` for the 113 hints my +115 HTML lines drifted. **Next: G4b — Kern & Sable, the first `choice` in the game's history, shipping alone so it can be eyeballed (§7½) without 13 migrations in the same diff.** Then G4c (13 D1 verbs → `NODE_VERBS` + the Junction Vignette `cost` conversion, which proves the leaf isn't single-use) → G4d.

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-04) — §VM-01-G4 CHILD LAB REPORT ⚠️ DESIGN LOCKED PENDING ONE ASK `f340143` (the ASK is now ANSWERED — see NEWEST): the `choice` opcode Inc A shipped has no host end, and only a third of "Class D" is a choice at all.** On "continue" took the board's next-up (1) — the last migration slice, whose stated prerequisite was its own child design pass (the §VM-01-G lock: *"G4 OK in principle, but G4 gets its own child design pass first"*). Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `ca0113c`, **one** claude process, `:1367` verified free throughout, no `lab-report-vm01g4-*` existed, and every number re-measured live rather than carried from the parent. **The re-measurement is itself the track's evidence:** the region is **1,006 lines — 41% of the parent's 2,445** (front 33332→**33702**, in-region `node.code ===` 77→**29**), because G1+G2+G2b+G3 moved the front **forward 1,439 lines in seven days**. The parent's *"the front moves backward ~4/day"* is no longer true inside it. **Census: 22 blocks / 838 classified lines**, read end to end, not sampled. **Finding 1 — the keystone has no host end, and this reorders the whole slice.** Inc A built the coroutine correctly (`*execBits@22223`, the suspending `*choice@22319`, `_uqfPump@6827`, `_uqfPending@6824`) and the half that **renders the ask and resumes with an answer was never written**: `_uqfPump` has **exactly one** caller in the HTML — `_uqfRunToCompletion@6840`, which **throws** on an unresolved ask — **all four** live `execBits` entry points wrap in it (`6880`/`6883`/`30017`/`36843`), the fifth carries a comment saying *"a choice in onPass/onFail throws, by scope-fence"*, `_uqfPending` is **read by nothing** outside two integration tests, and **`renderChoiceBlock` occurs 0 times in `roll2hit-v3.html` and 0 times in `js/quest.js`** — it has never existed as code. §DX-01e-FU2 reached that from the anchor side; this reaches it from the call graph. **So a `choice` bit in any live quest today does not render a choice — it throws**, and G4 is `choice`'s first **HOST** consumer, not just its first content consumer. **G4a is therefore the driver, shipping with zero content migrated** — provable against the existing coroutine tests before one player-facing block moves. **Finding 2 — "Class D" is three shapes and `choice` fits one of them.** The parent's description fits the *click handler* of all 22 blocks and the *surface* of very few. Of **21 button surfaces: 13 are SINGLE VERBS** (one button, no alternative — a one-option choice is not a choice, it is a labelled effect chain with ceremony), **7 are exclusive CHOICES**, and **CDG's `cq-boss-buttons` is a CONCURRENT menu** whose Taz/Don/Cat-King confrontations are visible at once by design — mapping those onto an opcode that is *exclusive by construction* would be an outright **behaviour change**. So the vocabulary is **`NODE_VERBS {id, node, when, label, bits}`** in the `NODE_PANELS`/`NODE_HOOKS` house style (ordered registry, in-place dispatch → LIFO preserved by construction), with `choice` as a **bit inside it**, used only where the block is genuinely exclusive. Forcing one tool onto all of Class D would be the parent's own warning — *"this is how `itemsMinAny` happened, a shape grown for the wrong layer"* — coming true inside the slice written to honour it. **Grammar gap, measured rather than assumed:** the parent's *"`cost` leaf (gold≥N, consume) — ONE new opcode"* is half already shipped — **`item_remove@22302` covers every consume site** — and the missing half is **three currencies**: gold at **six** sites (Yva 50gp · the Junction Vignette `[Help — 10gp]`, which is the second consumer that proves the leaf isn't single-use · Kenickie's four prices), `surgeCharges` (spent permanently), and hp — and **hp is NOT a price**: the Memory Gate's −15 is narrated damage on a branch that always succeeds, so it belongs in that option's bits. Two shipped properties a naive leaf would silently change: **affordability is tested at CLICK, not at render** at every gold site, and **`reward` with negative gold "works"** arithmetically with no affordability test and the word *reward* on a price — Hazard #2's *a write into a real-but-wrong object never throws*. **Deliberately not migrated, each with its reason recorded so it doesn't read as an oversight:** Entry 42 (`S_story.entry42Text@34651`) and the ZRH void-toll rune (`S_story._voidTollSecret@34763`) capture **free text**, which `choice` cannot express *by design* (its resume value is an index *"so the data author never couples to presentation"*) and which a `prompt_text` ask for two sites would make the single-use term the Host/Script Separation Policy forbids — **grow it at three consumers, not two**; **Kenickie's Black Market (71 lines) and the Lower Archive launcher are Class E sitting in G4's region** and belong to `NODE_HOOKS` by G2's verbatim method — they fell between three slices (G2 deferred them, G2b found Kenickie *"is a CDG surface in G3's territory"*, G3 migrated activation not UI) and are recorded rather than dropped a fourth time; the Reading Circle is a **day-cooldown counter** whose repeat notion already lives in quests (`retryable`/`retryGateDays`). **The §VM-01-G2b act-leg thread is now closed for this region:** one comparison survives — `(S_story.actNumber || 1) >= 3@34691` at NUE — and **NUE is `act:6`, so it is vacuously TRUE, not dead**. Nothing to fix and nothing to file; deleting it would be an unverified rewrite of authored intent (§AUDIT-03m-FU). Recorded so the class is measured closed rather than assumed. **Slices locked: G4a** driver + `cost` (the only parity-fence edit in the track — `js/quest.js`, re-inline, `check:questparity`) · **G4b** Kern & Sable as the `choice` pilot (76 lines; no cost, no combat, no free text, one node — so the first choice in the game's history ships alone and can be eyeballed) · **G4c** the 13 D1 verbs → `NODE_VERBS` + convert the Junction Vignette Help to `cost` · **G4d** CDG's menu (as `combat` bits — **`combat`'s optional `nodeCode`@22135 already accepts the synthetic `CQ_TAZ` code those buttons use**) + the two Class-E strays. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0**; `check:anchors` **129 symbol anchors across 7 docs all resolve, 0 stale** — the lab report is the 7th doc and added 30 anchors, each verified individually, and **4 hints were off by one and were corrected by hand rather than left for `anchors:fix` to guess** (§DX-01e-FU: `--fix` picks the NEAREST hit). Docs only — **no HTML, no tests, no §7½**, Playwright untouched so the **804/4** baseline stands. **🟡 THE ONE ASK, and it blocks G4a:** when a verb is unaffordable, keep **refuse-at-click** (today's behaviour at all six gold sites, byte-identical, consistent with the track's telling-vs-asking thesis — **my recommendation**) or **gate at render** (tidier UI, but a real behaviour change at six sites and a quieter world)? The `cost` leaf's contract differs between the two, so G4a should not start until it is answered. **Next: answer the ASK → G4a; or §VM-01-G2b-FU / §DX-02m / §AUDIT-03d, all still waiting on their own design calls.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-04) — §VM-01-G2b ✅ SHIPPED `bedf2c1`: the ctx design that blocked this slice was ONE FIELD — and moving the 29 blocks read five narrative beats out of the game that can never fire.** On "continue" with every §AUDIT-03 row still waiting on a user design call, took the active engineering track's open slice — the one whose blocker was an *engineering* decision, not a user one. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `bedb19a`, one other claude process, WBAPI **stopped** throughout (`:1367` verified free before and after — Hazard #1, this slice hand-edits inline JS). **The blocker was answered by measurement, not design.** G2 deferred these because they "need a ctx-argument design"; of the **89** `storyRender` locals in scope at the region, the 29 blocks read **exactly one** — `npcRowDiv`. The scan's apparent `n`/`row`/`txt`/`fav` hits were `\n` escapes inside strings, a word inside a comment, and block-local declarations, and the two candidates anyone would expect — `keys` and `_npcNodeKey` — are read by **none** of them. So the contract is `{ npcRowDiv }`, built once beside the anchor it carries; `_runNodeHook(id, node, ctx)` forwards it; **21** hooks destructure it in the signature and **8** take `(node)` alone and are asserted never to name the anchor. **Two safety properties checked BEFORE the move, because they are what makes a verbatim move legitimate:** no **top-level `return`** (all 13 sit inside click handlers — one at block level would have gone from exiting nothing to exiting a hook early) and no **`var`** (nothing hoists out of one block for a later one to read). **The single piece of evidence I would keep if I could keep only one: a line-multiset diff of the two revisions — nothing was removed except the 2 old `_runNodeHook` lines**, and every added line is scaffolding (29 fn headers, 29 closers, 29 registry entries, 29 dispatch calls, 13 comments, the ctx). All 895 moved lines are byte-identical; they were not even re-indented. **Behavioural proof: a 17-combo golden-DOM/state diff, 16 byte-identical.** The 17th (`HKG`) differed — and **differed again between two runs of the SAME code**, because the CY Madness Gate draws unseeded `Math.random()` into two *persisted* `S_story` fields. That is a pre-existing invariant-#6 violation, not a regression, and the after-vs-after run is what proves it rather than my say-so; filed as **§DX-02m** (51 sites; `check:rng` guards stream parity, so nothing gates *which* stream a state write draws from). **The finding: five act-gated beats in this region cannot fire.** `storyRender` sets `S_story.actNumber = node.act || 1` on every render, before the region, and every Birka node is `act:1` — so the lamp inquiry (`>=2`), the Heartwood letter (`>=4`), Yael's Named Report (`>=6`), the S54 joint witness (`>=7`) and the Act VIII Quill farewell (`===8`) are unreachable. **It cascades**: `birka-lamp-choice` waits on `brynnKeeperStoryTold`, and the dead Beat 1 is its only writer, so the §XXXV vigil arc past its ambient line is dead too. Same structurally-dead act leg **§VM-01-G3** retired from the quest stanzas — the third slice of this track to find that special-case code keyed on node state rots silently, which is the row's whole thesis. **Filed, not fixed**: G3 could drop its act legs because the other gate leaves already carried the staging; here the act leg *is* the staging, so something must replace it — and `world.md` §XXXIX designs Yael's scene for `LLA`/`HKG` at Act IV+ while the code fires at `LHR` at Act VI+, so neither number is trustworthy without corroboration (§AUDIT-03m-FU's laundering lesson). **§VM-01-G2b-FU** carries it, `world.md` + `story.md` are **annotated at all five sites** (annotate, never rewrite), and a test pins all five gates plus the cascade so the fix has to update it. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** · new `uqf-npc-row-hooks.test.js` **8/8** with a **positive control** — against HEAD **5 of 8 fail**, and the 3 that pass are exactly the live-behaviour ones, which is the right shape: those test the game, not the migration · `uqf-node-hooks` 4/4 (its registry assertion now owns the G2 head explicitly) · full Playwright **804 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, with the known `multiplayer-presence:171` flake green 7/7 in isolation · inline `<script>` parses clean. **The `npcRowDiv` anchor class is now empty** (the only remaining references are the element's own construction, the ctx and the final insert), and G2's deferral list was approximate: **Kern & Sable is not an npc-row block** and **Kenickie** is a CDG surface in G3's territory — both recorded rather than quietly dropped. > **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-03) — §DX-02l ✅ SHIPPED `91d3650`: the deliberate-backup endpoint got the `./api.sh` wrapper it never had — and the sweep the row asked for turned out to be the patch chain's input, deleted before anyone reads it.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's staging question still unanswered, took the row the last board named as small. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `bca468f`, server stopped, one other claude process, and the premise re-measured before any edit — `./api.sh save` answered `✗ Unknown command "save"`, `api/wb.js` held **0** occurrences of `save`/`snapshots`, and the *author-facing* guide reached the surface with `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` in **three** places, including §17.13 itself. So the golden rule was broken in the document that teaches it. **Shipped API-first — endpoint before wrapper (prompt.md §2.5):** `GET /api/snapshots` (dir, per-file size/mtime, total, and whether the patch chain already holds each one) and `DELETE /api/snapshots`, nonce-guarded like every other destructive route; then `./api.sh save` and `./api.sh snapshots [--sweep] [--force]`. `snapshot` joins the nonce endpoint's `validTypes` as **the one type with no collection behind it** — the sweep destroys files, not entries, so there is no key to resolve and the lookup is skipped rather than run against `undefined`. **The row's framing of the sweep is what failed on contact, and it failed in the safe direction for once.** It called disposal *"a `rm` a human has to remember"* — but `archive-snapshots.sh` already disposes of these files, and the way it does it is to diff each one into a `milepoints/patches` delta **and then `rm` it**. A blunt sweep is therefore not a chore automated; it is **the patch chain's input destroyed before it is read**, because an unarchived snapshot is the only copy of that state. `--sweep` now deletes only what the chain already holds (a `<stem>.patch`, or the name in `_last.name`), **names `./archive-snapshots.sh` in the skip reason**, and leaves `--force` as the deliberate discard. **Evidence:** a live round trip on the real server *before* any test — `./api.sh save` → 5.2 MB backup beside the game file → `snapshots` listed it `unarchived` → `--sweep` **kept it and said why** → `--sweep --force` freed it, ending with `roll2hit-v3.html` at its **original shasum** and the root back to one file; `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js` **7/7** against a throwaway server on a scratch copy (the sweep deletes files — it must never be pointed at the repo), and **all 7 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — nothing here is vacuous); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **764 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, **0 flaky**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring surface only.** **The durable half is what the sweep is allowed to name:** a decoy suite (`roll2hit-v3-notes.html`, `-2026.html`, `-20260709.html`, `-20260709-0341.html`, `.htm`, `.html.bak`) survives a **forced** sweep sitting beside the game file, because only an exact `-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` stamp is reachable — that stamp is the entire guard between *delete the backups* and *delete the game*. The same file pins the row's own lesson as an assertion: **no author-facing doc may reach `/api/save` with `curl`.** **The lesson: an endpoint whose only documented client is `curl` is not a finished write path — the wrapper is what makes it real, and the gap is usually visible in the docs before it is visible in the CLI.** Docs: `api-user-guide.md` (§17.13 rewritten + both worked workflows + the endpoint card), `API-README.md` (new *Dated backups* section + 3 mapping rows), `wbapi-help.md`, `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `index.md`, `prompt.md` §3 + §9, **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7** (now carrying the reporting half — the directory is gitignored, so `./api.sh snapshots` is the only thing that will ever say what is in it), and `./api.sh help` gained a *Persistence* block under SERVER LIFECYCLE stating plainly that you do **not** need `save` after a write. **No side findings filed — the row was self-contained.** **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02j (measured, needs a per-group design call), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**
>
> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-03) — §DX-02k ✅ SHIPPED `2ead185`: the argless `save()` was not a stray test call. It was the server's own per-write path, and it wrote a 5.4 MB copy of the game into whatever directory the process started in, once per PUT, forever.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's staging question still unanswered, took the row the last board named as *"the one I can just do."* Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `0abe376`, server stopped, one other claude process, and the premise re-measured before any edit. **It held, and then failed in two directions.** **(1) The destination is the process CWD, not "next to the module."** `getStampedName()` returns a **bare filename**, and `fs.writeFileSync` resolves a bare name against `process.cwd()` — proved by loading the repo's HTML from a scratch directory and watching the 5.4 MB snapshot land *in the scratch directory*. It read as "the repo root" only because that is where the server runs. **(2) The argless callers were not scripts — the SERVER was.** `saveAndRestart` and `saveAndVerify`, the persist step behind **every** successful PUT/POST/DELETE, ran `WBAPI.save()` argless and then `copyFileSync(snapshot, GAME_FILE)`, leaving the snapshot behind; two more argless sites sat in the batch endpoints. **So the row's own proposed fix — *"make the argless form server-only"* — would have left the actual litter source untouched**, which is the second time in two sessions that a §DX-02 row's framing survived contact and its *scope* did not. The snapshot was never a backup of the pre-write state either: **it holds the NEW text**, so it is pure redundancy the instant the copy lands. **The row's own caution came back inverted, too.** `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` / `monitor-snapshots.py` *do* depend on the stamped name — but `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md` documents their input as a **deliberate `cp`**. The per-write stamping was not feeding that feature, it was **drowning** it: a curated session history reduced to per-keystroke noise, patch chain last advanced **2026-07-09**, no watcher running, **6 orphans / ~32 MB** sitting in the root — invisible, because `roll2hit-v3-2*.html` is gitignored. **Shipped:** `save(dest)` writes exactly where told and **refuses with no argument**, naming the alternative in the error text so the next author cannot just pass `getStampedName()` back in; **`saveStamped([dir])`** is the deliberate dated backup and lands **beside the source file** (`POST /api/save`, `wbapi save`, and the audit-fix + junction-nuke endpoints, which carried the same bare-name CWD bug); **`saveGameFile()`** is the server-only per-write persist — temp beside the game file plus an **atomic rename**, which leaves nothing to sweep *and* means a reader can never catch a half-written game file, keeping the temp and returning its path if the rename fails. **Evidence:** the CWD claim proved by scratch-directory probe **before** any edit; **the pre-fix test run demonstrated the bug on itself** — stashing the fix and re-running dropped **two more 5.4 MB snapshots into the repo root**, the whole defect in one observation; `dx02k-save-destination.test.js` **6/6**, **5 failing on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the passer is the `save(dest)` round trip, already true); live server round trip = **3 successful writes** (`act=1` idempotent, `act=2`, `act=1` back), each confirmed on disk by grep and through `GET`, ending at the **original shasum** with **0 new snapshots and 0 temp files**; `POST /api/save` still returns a dated backup beside the game file, so the archive surface is intact; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **756 passed / 4 failed** = the documented crud-arrays four (+6 new tests over the 750 baseline). **One honest wrinkle:** the first full run showed **5** failed — `multiplayer-presence:171` red on *both* attempts, where the baseline calls it a flake that retries green. It is 7/7 alone and a **second full run reproduced the documented baseline exactly** (4 failed / 1 flaky / 756 passed), so it is the known flake having a bad run, not a regression; the file spawns its own throwaway server and performs no writes. Root swept to **one** `roll2hit-v3*.html`, and two full suite runs added none back. **The durable half is the inverse assertion in the test: no shipped code may call `WBAPI.save()` with no destination** — the bug is invisible in behaviour (the write succeeds, the game is correct) and findable only by looking at a directory nobody named. **The lesson, and the natural sequel to §DX-02h's: "did it persist?" is only half of a write path's acceptance test — the other half is "and nowhere else."** Docs: **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7**, `index.md` (Version Snapshots row + `wbapi-core.js` persist surfaces), `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (which told authors `POST /api/save` was how a write reached disk — it already had), plus annotations on the three historical notes still tracing the old stamped path. **Side finding filed, not swept: §DX-02l** — `POST /api/save` has **no `./api.sh` wrapper**, so verifying it needed raw `curl`, the inverse of prompt.md §3's rule; worth pairing with an `./api.sh snapshots` list/sweep now that snapshots are on-request only. **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02j (measured, needs a per-group design call), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §DX-02l (small), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-03) — §DX-02h + §DX-02i ✅ SHIPPED TOGETHER `a047348`: the row said one monster was unreachable; 55 are. The doc that made it look rostered described a function that does not exist. And the endpoint for fixing it reported success while writing nothing — with GET agreeing.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the two rows the last board named as small. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `5cf04d4`, server stopped, one other claude process, and grep confirmed **both** premises before anything was written. **Both framings then failed, in the direction this repo keeps finding.** Scanning all 398 `MONSTER_POOL` keys for a reference outside `MONSTER_POOL`/`MONSTER_DROPS`, **55 have none** — the row's `void_rat_swarm` was not unique, its `voidTainted` twin **`void_wolf`** had the identical 2-reference shape, and ~50 more sit in the *partially* rostered Dark Fantasy Bestiary (`fleder` orphaned, `protofleder` live). **Why nobody noticed is the sharp part:** `world.md` stated in the present tense that both were *"injected into terrain encounter tables at runtime when `voidPressure ≥ 6` via `_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`"* — that function occurs **0 times in the file**, and **2 of the 5 terrains it named (`dark_forest`, `mountain_pass`) have never been keys**. The doc was the thing that looked like evidence (§AUDIT-03l's class), which is exactly why *re-measure, never read placement off a doc* is not optional. **The fix was then blocked by a third defect on its own path:** `PUT /api/terrain` accepted only `{label,icon}` and **persisted nothing** — it set the in-memory model, returned `ok:true` with the entity echoed, never called `save()`, and because the mutation lived in the loaded model **`GET` read the phantom edit back for the rest of the process's life**. That is worse than the DELETE family it mirrors (§DX-01d/i), where the entry at least reappeared on the next parse: here the read path *corroborated* the lie. Root cause was structural — `editField`/`editStructuredField` carried a `sectionMap` with **no `terrain` entry**, so `WORLD_DB` had no source-level writer at all, and the server's own advisory had been telling authors to `PUT {"monsters":[…]}`, an operation the endpoint rejected. **Fixed API-first (endpoint before edit, prompt.md §2.5), then authored through it in one call.** The roster got its **own** writer rather than reusing `editStructuredField`, because `WORLD_DB` is the one collection whose array holds **code identifiers** (`P.giant_rat`): the generic path would emit `["giant_rat"]`, which re-parses to strings with nothing throwing — `_buildIndexes` maps them fine — while the GAME reads those entries as stat blocks, so `_monsterLevel` would score the whole terrain 1. It re-parses the patched section and **proves the roster reads back before committing**, and `editField` now refuses `monsters` outright (insertStringField would otherwise add a *second* `monsters:` key — §AUDIT-03a's rot). **A live detail decided the validation:** `cat_quarter` ships a duplicate (`fluffy_cat` ×2), so duplicates warn instead of failing — a writer that cannot express what the corpus already holds cannot round-trip it. **§DX-02i** deleted `XP_BY_TIER` (user chose delete over annotate) after measuring it at **1.9×–6.5× below** the shipped `(S.enemy.ac||10) * (S.opp.maxHp||10)` at every tier — `deadly` a flat 1000 against a mean of **4,095** — so it was a different model, not a stale form of the shipped one; four dependents had to move, one of them `index.md`'s row whose own text (*"L12+ uses AC × maxHP"*) was also false. **Evidence:** the terrain defect proved by round trip **first** (`ok:true` + `GET` agreeing + 0 occurrences on disk); placement `git diff` = **1 line**; **golden parse diff across all 7 collections = 1 entry diff, `sewers fields=monsters`**, 0 lost / 0 gained; HTTP negative controls refused with the file **byte-identical by shasum**; `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **6/6** with **5 failing on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the passer is the inverse guard, already true); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **750 passed / 4 failed** = the documented crud-arrays four. **§7½ DONE:** the pre-battle overlay at SFT now renders **VOID RAT SWARM · Visby Sewers** with a styled green **EASY** badge, and `_weightedMonsterPick('sewers')` drew the swarm 172 / the wolf 241 times in 4,000; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion** — every terrain roster entry must parse to a **stat block, not a bare key string** — because a JSON-string roster re-parses without error and is invisible to every other gate. **The lesson, now proved in three directions (create §DX-01c, delete §DX-01d/i, update §DX-02h): a write path's acceptance test is a ROUND TRIP — and "GET agrees with me" is not that test.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING **Hazard #6**, `monsters.md` §"A pool entry is not content until a roster names it", `world.md` drift-corrected, both API docs (which documented adding monsters via a PUT that could not do it, in raw `curl`), `index.md`. **Side findings filed, not swept: §DX-02j** (the **53** remaining unreachable pool entries — decide per group, then a `check:monsterreach` gate in the §AUDIT-03j/n mould) and **§DX-02k** (argless `WBAPI.save()` stamps a snapshot into the repo root; two are sitting there now from earlier sessions, hidden by gitignore). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02j (measured, needs a per-group design call), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §DX-02k (small), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-03) — §DX-01e-FU ✅ SHIPPED `f4bc624`: the seed inbox's 36 pointers were migrated, and 35 of them pointed at unrelated code. The four claims they anchored had all gone stale in the same direction — the engine grew the capability this file is organized around lacking.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the last open §DX-01 row — mechanical, gate-fenced, no design call. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `45afe1f`, server stopped, single live claude session, and `--legacy` confirmed the row's count exactly (36). **Measured every cited line at HEAD before writing anything, and only `5327` → `const ENEMY_DB` had not moved:** `21722`, cited for `execBits`, is now a Mother-Mimic quest body; `25558`, cited for `BOSS_COMMANDER_AUROS`, an `sbo-refocus-bar` style write; `26822` is past the end of a section. **So the line is not evidence, and the method was to derive each symbol from what the PROSE means** — the same discipline §AUDIT-03j used when it re-derived identity from the live profiles instead of carrying the row's mapping. **All 32 symbols were verified to resolve to exactly one line before writing**, because `--fix` picks the hit *nearest* the cached number — a vague symbol like bare `execBits` (15 hits) would let the tool silently pick the wrong one, which is the original defect wearing the new convention's clothes. **The migration forced four stale claims into the open, each corrected in place with the file's own `~~strike~~ + ✅` drift idiom:** `execBits` is no longer *"a straight-line `for` loop — no branch, no suspend"* but a generator (`*execBits(bits, ctx)@22223`); `choice`'s *"empty handler"* is the engine's first **suspending** opcode (`*choice(bit, ctx)@22319`), and `renderChoiceBlock` — which the file says *"was never written"* — no longer occurs in the file at all; **`unlock` is authored 18 times**, §BOARD-01 having become exactly the consumer §POT-H1 predicted, so *"four dead opcodes"* is now three; and `textVariants` has two consumers, not *"exactly one"*. `item_check` is worse than recorded: `ctx._itemCheck` now occurs **exactly once in the file**, so the write reads back nowhere. **Evidence:** `--legacy` **671 → 635, exactly −36**, `potential.md` reporting **0**; audit **94 anchors across 6 docs, 0 dead**; **`anchors:fix` reports "no stale line hints to refresh"** — every number written was exact, the round-trip proof; **negative control on the real game file** (renamed `const _ssjNpcs` on line 32427 → gate exits 1 naming `potential.md:176`; restored, `shasum -c` **OK**, `git diff` empty); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **5/5**, the new test **failing on the pre-migration file** and passing after, restored byte-identical. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only. **The durable half is the new test, because the gate cannot provide it:** `check:anchors` fails on a dead *symbol*, but a bare `` `31732` `` resolves to nothing and is invisible to it — nothing else stands between the seed inbox and a slow slide back to line numbers. **The lesson: a doc read to BUILD FROM is a live doc, and "re-grep before building" is not optional when 97% of a file's own pointers are wrong.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING's Doc-Anchor Policy records that every live doc is now migrated; `potential.md` rule 3 carries a dated note saying plainly that **only the anchored claims were re-measured** — the rest is still `43bd09c`-era. **Side finding filed, not swept: §DX-01e-FU2** — the file's seven `BLOCKED` seeds and promotion rule 2 all wait on §VM-01 Inc A, which shipped; but `choice` still has **0 authors**, so the re-triage should follow one exemplar quest, not precede it. **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02h/i (both small), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-31) — §DX-01e ✅ SHIPPED `57d79d7`: a doc anchor now names a SYMBOL, and 43 of the 50 anchors in the live docs were pointing somewhere else.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the last open §DX-01 row — the one the board named first among the no-design-call alternatives. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `a19919f`, server stopped, 4 claude processes present but the game file untouched. **The premise held and understated itself.** Sampling 14 bare anchors at HEAD before writing anything, **9 pointed at unrelated code** — `22104`, cited for `_rollSkill`, had become a prof-bonus line; `31618`, cited for the §CELL-03 position sync, an `aurosBlueprintKnown` branch — and the bare form appears **720 times across 27 files**. **The convention shipped is one token, `` `symbol@1234` ``:** the **symbol** is the pointer (any literal substring of the line meant, optionally file-qualified — `` `js/wbapi-server.js:function seededNext@1147` ``), the **number is a cached hint** refreshed mechanically by `npm run anchors:fix`. It is §DX-02e/§DX-02f's lesson in a third place — *pin the property, never the generated coordinate* — because a line number is **generated** by every edit above it. **`scripts/resolve-anchors.js`** carries audit / `--fix` / `--legacy` (a migration tracker) / a lookup mode that replaces `grep -n` / `--selftest`, and is wired as **`check:walk` gate #15 `check:anchors`**. **Its failure condition is deliberately narrow — a DEAD symbol fails, a drifted number only warns** — because failing on drift would re-create the per-increment tax the row exists to remove. **Building the negative control is what found the real hole:** plain substring matching let `XP_BY_TIER` → `XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` still "resolve", so a *rename* — the exact case the gate is for — would have passed; resolution is now **identifier-boundary aware**, and a `placeholder` phase keeps `--fix` from re-pointing the docs' own `` `symbol@1234` `` example. **Migrated (live docs only):** CONTRIBUTING.md 10 · mechanics.md 3 · mechanics-combat.md 2 · **every bare anchor in BACKLOG.md's open rows** — `plan-archive.md` (211) and the lab reports (~300) keep theirs, because **history is annotated, not rewritten** (§DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m). **Re-anchoring surfaced two stale CONTRIBUTING claims, corrected in place:** the seeded-RNG bullet still said the client rolls encounters from `Math.random()` *"until §VM-01-B lands"* (it landed; the client draws `_seededNext()`, and only the *weighting* still diverges), and the `_legacy_fn` bullet cited a quest that *"rolls `Math.random() > 0.5` inside quest data"* when **no `_legacy_fn` line in the file contains `Math.random` any more**. **Evidence:** 7-phase `--selftest`; **negative control on the real game file** (renamed `const XP_BY_TIER` in place → gate exits 1 naming `BACKLOG.md:312`; restored, `shasum -c` **OK**); `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **4/4**, with the migration test **failing on the pre-migration docs** (stash of the 4 doc files → 1 failed / 3 passed); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; audit reports **56 anchors across 5 docs, 0 dead**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only. **Side work filed, not swept: §DX-01e-FU** (`potential.md`'s 36 bare anchors — the only live doc left on the old form). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02h/i (both small), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU, §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-31) — §DX-02g ✅ SHIPPED `f229ede`: two monsters carried a tier the engine has no entry for, and all four readers of that field answered with a wrong number instead of an error.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the row the last board named first among the no-design-call alternatives. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `fe3c14c`, server stopped, a 2nd claude process present but the game file untouched. **The premise held; the row's framing did not, and re-measuring before editing is what caught it.** The row called this *"an authoring-surface defect, not a gameplay one"* — but `tier` is read in six places and **every reader falls back rather than throwing**: `_voidEnrage` → `|| {atk:1,dmg:1,die:0}`, so The Warden (which matches `_VOID_ENEMY_RE`) ran its §PLAY-01-B low-HP press at the **weakest magnitude in the game** on a sealed Act-5 guardian; `_storyRollInit` → initiative `+0` instead of `+3`; `_weightedMonsterPick` → a flat `|| 10`, and because `_inferTerrain` answers with a *neighbouring node's* terrain, cells beside **TBS** infer `epic_goblin_cave` — so Kazrath drew as a **random wilderness encounter at 2.5× a `hard` monster's weight** at low notoriety; and both threat badges printed the literal **`RARE`** while asking for a `.threat-rare` CSS rule **that does not exist**. **The tiers were derived, not tasted:** `void_rat_swarm → easy` settled itself (6 of 8 nearest stat-neighbours `easy`; exact AC/HP/atk twin is Jackalwere), while `void_shaman` was a real fork — its two **exact** stat-block twins are both `hard`, but `_monsterLevel` puts it at 6, a level that is 43-medium / 4-hard — so it was **asked with both readings measured, and `hard` was chosen**. **Shipped:** two `./api.sh put monster … tier=` calls, nothing else in the HTML. **Evidence:** `git diff` **exactly 2 lines**; **golden parse diff across all 7 collections = exactly 2 entry diffs, both `fields=tier` alone**, 0 lost / 0 gained; new `dx02g-monster-tier-contract.test.js` **8/8** with **5 of the 8 failing on the real pre-fix file** (restored byte-identical; the 3 that pass there are the ones already true — neither half is vacuous); `check:walk` **14/14 exit 0**; full Playwright **740 passed / 4 failed** = the documented crud-arrays four, nothing new. **§7½ DONE:** the Void Shaman's Sanctum pre-battle overlay now shows a styled orange **HARD** badge where it used to print an unstyled `RARE`; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion** — the test pins that **no tier-keyed engine map ships partial** (all 8 tier literals + the 6 notoriety weight rows + the 5 `.threat-*` CSS rules must cover all five), because a map missing a tier is the identical silent-fallback defect from the other direction. **The lesson, and it is §DX-01c's in a third dimension: a lookup that falls back never throws — so "no error" is not evidence the value is known.** Docs: `monsters.md` gained the tier-contract section with the six-reader table, and its stale `del monster` warning (fixed 2026-07-30) was retired in the same pass. **Side findings filed, not swept: §DX-02h** (`void_rat_swarm` has exactly 2 references in the file — no roster, no battle, no quest; it is unreachable content) and **§DX-02i** (`XP_BY_TIER` is declared and read by nothing, and it encodes a *different* XP model than the one that ships). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §AUDIT-03o (measured, but a design call), §DX-01e (line-anchor drift — the last open §DX-01 row), §DX-02h/i (both small, both filed today), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-31) — §AUDIT-03n ✅ SHIPPED: the ending called three of your six friends strangers no matter how you played. > **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-30) — §DX-01d + §DX-01i ✅ SHIPPED TOGETHER: every `del` in the API reported success and changed nothing on disk. Not just `del monster`, not just junctions — the whole DELETE family, for every key.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the pairing the last board named first (*"the two `del` defects are one class — cheaper paired"*). Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `e4c03a0`, server stopped, a 2nd claude process present but the game file untouched. **Measured before editing, in-process against a COPY** (`roll2hit-v3.html` never written): `monster:fish_01`, `quest:mq_1` and `node:RUH` each returned `ok:true`, each left `_rawSrc` **byte-identical**, and each **came back after save+reload**. Both rows described the symptom correctly and **understated the scope by 3×** — every `delete()` in `wbapi-core.js` was the single line `delete WBAPI.<collection>[key]`, and the server's generic `DELETE` handler ended at `json(...)` with **no `saveAndVerify` at all**, so nothing reached disk even in principle. The precedent §DX-01i pointed at (`the node-delete splice at wbapi-core.js ~1260`) was **dead code with zero callers**, and its private brace-walker missed `/* */` comments and orphaned the `NODE_COORDS` row besides. **This is §DX-01c in mirror image — a write path that reports success without persisting — and the lesson is the same in both directions: nothing throws, so the failure is silent.** **Shipped:** `WBAPI.deleteEntrySource(section,key)`, one source-level excision for all four types with **verify-or-revert** (the section's depth-1 key *multiset* must lose exactly one instance of `key`, or the splice is rolled back and the delete fails loudly rather than corrupting the section) and clamped past nested START anchors so a pool delete can never reach the trophy-drops map; **the `check:dupkeys` scanner moved into `wbapi-core.js`** and imported back by the gate, so audit and writer share ONE scanner (two would drift — the §AUDIT-03f class); **cascades** (node→`NODE_COORDS`, monster→`MONSTER_DROPS`) so no orphan is left for `./api.sh audit`; the server's DELETE now routes through `saveAndVerify` with a new **`postVerify`** hook — a delete's proof is an *absence* in the re-parsed collections, which `expectedFields` cannot express — returning `deleteVerified:true`; and **`highway --execute` deprecated + refused up front** (before the coordinate fetch, so it needs no server) with the real recipe in the error. **Evidence:** `dx01di-delete-persists.test.js` **8/8**, including **the 20 largest nested UQF bodies excised one at a time** (largest 3,350 bytes of braces/strings/arrow-fns, each costing exactly one entry); **end-to-end through the live server twice, each a byte-identical no-op** — `post monster dx01i_probe` → `del monster` → `grep -c` **0** (the row's own reproduction, which used to leave **1**), and `post node ZZP` → `del node` → **0**, game file `shasum 018ee943…` unchanged both times; `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; full Playwright **717 passed / 4 failed** — the baseline is exactly the documented four in `worldbuilder-crud-arrays.test.js` plus the known `multiplayer-presence` flake (passed on retry). **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring-surface only; nothing a player sees changed.** **Docs:** a new **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #5** carries the class forward (*whenever you add a write path, the acceptance test is a round trip — save, re-parse, assert the change survived*), Hazards #2/#3/#4 updated to ✅, prompt.md §3 #4/#5, `api-user-guide.md` §10, `API-README.md`, and `./api.sh help` (§21 no longer advertises **eleven** `highway --execute` invocations that now die). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §AUDIT-03j (9 registry keys naming non-existent nodes + a `check:walk` gate), §DX-02g (2 field PUTs), §DX-01e (line-anchor drift — the last open §DX-01 row), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

`warrants-board` derives its stand-on cell from `NODE_COORDS[q.activateNode]`. **Evidence:** both suites **28/28 on three consecutive `--retries=0` runs** (no flake masking); full Playwright **710 passed / 4 failed** (the documented crud-arrays four); `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; diff = 2 test files +21/−7. No §7½ — nothing a player sees changed. **Durable fix (the row's real ask): prompt.md §7 now carries the FULL red set with the date last re-proved**, plus two lessons — *a test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws*, and (a second time after §DX-02e) *pin the property, never a fabricated or generated coordinate*. **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call), else §DX-01d+i (the two `del` defects together), §AUDIT-03j (9 registry keys naming non-existent nodes), §DX-02g (2 field PUTs), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

`check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; full Playwright **708 passed / 6 failed, all 6 proved pre-existing at HEAD by stash-diff**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — this is an authoring-surface increment; nothing a player sees changed.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING.md Hazard #2 rewritten as a working recipe that **keeps the defect class as the lesson** (*a write that lands in a real-but-wrong object never throws*), prompt.md §3, potential.md, `monsters.md` gains an Authoring section, `api-user-guide.md` §9.3. **Three side findings filed, not detoured: §DX-01i** — `./api.sh del monster` reports success and changes nothing on disk (model-only; the exact mirror of the create bug, and the same shape as Hazard #4's `del node`); **§DX-02g** — `void_shaman` (`tier:'rare'`) and `void_rat_swarm` (`tier:'low'`) are off-contract and silently dropped from the worldbuilder's tier-grouped picker; Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `31f66a2`, bug reproduced live (2,853 textual vs 2,851 parsed, exactly the two documented ids) before touching anything. **Root cause (proved by running `removeFns` in isolation on the region):** it processed comments as code — the §ARCH-01 Wave-1m/1n migration comments' arrow-fn examples (`activateCond:()=>true`, `completeFn:()=>…`) triggered the fn-stripper, whose expression scan swallows to the next depth-0 comma — which sits AFTER the following quest's closing brace, so the whole entry collapsed into `: null,` on a `//` line: valid JS, silent drop.
