### §VM-01 — The Quest VM → *No Word for Wait* (NEW 2026-07-16)

> **Diagnostic (parent) lab report:** `lab-reports/lab-report-javascript-mud.md` — a structural read of the entire `<script>` block (five layers, four execution traces: program/UI/movement/quest-acceptance), plus the follow-up verification recorded below. **Not** a quest-by-quest audit. **Inc A child lab report LOCKED 2026-07-21:** `lab-reports/lab-report-vm01a-execbits-coroutine.md` (design-review-before-implementation; locks the `execBits`→generator transformation, the `ask` envelope, the driver, the five call-site wraps, the three design calls, and the test plan — see §RESUME NEWEST banner above). Policy: **Host/Script Separation Policy** in [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md).
>
> **The unifying theme (matched to `story.md`): the engine tells, and cannot ask.** `story.md` is about a man who saw the Void and never managed to *ask anyone for help* — Froberger fixed it alone and it destroyed him. The quest engine commits the same act on the player: **you are told (`📋 <title>`), never asked.** Arrival + gate = `'active'`, announced in a `·`-joined message strip. There is no accept button — and the reason is not that nobody added one. **There is no state in which the VM is waiting for an answer.** (§PLAY-01 found the engine *knows what it won't transmit*; §VM-01 finds it **cannot receive**. Same sin, opposite direction.)
>
> **The architecture is already right — this track finishes §ARCH-01, it does not redesign it.** The file runs a proper embedded-scripting split twice over: three pure, world-injected, parity-fenced kernels (`MOVER`/`ROOMS`/`DUEL`, byte-identical to `js/*.js` with CI assertions), and a 258-line `QuestRuntime` executing 11,106 lines of `QUEST_DB` through the `BIT_CONTRACTS` opcode table. That 43:1 ratio is the achievement. **The defect is one small thing on top of it.**
>
> **Root cause — the VM has twelve opcodes and no jump instruction.** `execBits` (`execBits(bits, ctx)@22223`) in full: a `for` loop that looks up `HANDLERS[bit.kind]`, calls `h(bit, ctx)`, and ignores the return. No branch. No suspend. Three verified proofs, all grepped this session, none recalled:
>
> 1. **`choice` has a complete contract and an empty handler.** The contract (`choice:      { required@21997`) is rigorous — requires `prompt` + `options`, validates ≥2 options each carrying a `label` and a `bits` array. The implementation (`choice(bit@22319`) is, in full: `choice(bit, ctx) { /* Phase 2: renderChoiceBlock(bit.prompt, bit.options, ctx) */ }`. **Zero quests use it** (`grep -c "kind:'choice'"` → 0). It was never written because **a synchronous `for` loop cannot wait for a human being.**
> 2. **`item_check` writes a predicate nobody reads.** `ctx._itemCheck@22311` sets it; a full-file grep returns that line and nothing else. It is a conditional in a language with no `if` — it evaluates and evaporates.
> 3. **`skill_check` works only by smuggling.** It is the one bit that branches, and it does so by hardcoding the branch *inside the leaf*: `resolveSkillCheck` → `execBits(pass ? bit.onPass : bit.onFail)` (`execBits(pass ?@22259`). The sole conditional in the whole language is baked into one instruction rather than available to it.
>
> **Two missing words, and everything follows.** `wait` (no `yield` → no consent, no `choice`, no dialogue tree, no multi-step interaction, no `'abandoned'` status, no transactional bit chain — a throw mid-chain leaves half the effects applied with no rollback). `or` (no boolean nesting → each new OR shape has historically meant a new *term*; `itemsMinAny` was added for exactly one quest, `quest_wm_01`). **Prior art worth reading before Inc A:** Ink (Inkle) compiles narrative to JSON bytecode on a small VM and suspends at a `ChoicePoint` — structurally what this is; Yarn Spinner for dialogue; CEL / JSONLogic for Inc F (expression-AST-as-data, no parser needed).
>
> **Per Lab Report Policy: every increment locks its own child lab report (data shapes + flow) before any HTML edit.** The rows below are the *plan of a plan* — scope, evidence, dependency, and the decision each one carries. They are not implementation plans and must not be treated as one.
>
> **Dependency order:** A · B · C are independent (A is the keystone). D needs C. E needs C + the `_legacy_fn` purge. F is independent but cheaper after C. G needs A + F to be worth doing.

> *Closed increments A–F + F-FU (A/B/C `c22f4f0` · D `9f10bfe` · E `354b20a` · F `c6be7f8` · F-FU `549d6b4`) — full ship records moved verbatim → plan-archive.md §"Archived 2026-07-28 — §DX-01f". Only §VM-01-G below remains open.*

- [~] **§VM-01-G — *The migration front*: per-node special cases → node hooks — DESIGN LOCKED 2026-07-28 (`lab-reports/lab-report-vm01g-migration-front.md`): user chose Option C (layered) via Ask — NODE_PANELS ordered data table for the ~30 Class-A/B flavor/arrival blocks · NODE_HOOKS registered render fns for the ~9 Class-E bespoke UIs · Class C/D migrate INTO the VM per-arc (real `gate:`+`activateNode`; `choice` + a new `cost` leaf — Inc A's first content consumer); slice order G1→G2→G3→G4 confirmed; G4 OK in principle but needs its own child design pass first. Re-measure at lock: front at 33332, `node.code` comparisons 76→124 (77 above the front across ~58 blocks + 47 engine-region Class-F, logged as G-FU) — the front moves backward ~4/day, the row's thesis is measurable, not theoretical.** Original finding: `storyRender` (now `30887`–`35298`, **4,412 lines — the largest function in the file**) is two programs stacked: ~2,445 lines of hardcoded per-node special cases followed by a compact, generic, data-driven `_mkSection`/`_mkCard` engine that renders Fish/Encounter/Quests/Loot/Rest/World from data. Every `if (node.code === 'XX')` above the front is a missing component — `isFishingLake` is what one looks like when it went right. This is plain ECS, **the same disease as the gate language one layer up:** *when the data language lacks a shape, the file grows imperative code instead of vocabulary.* Two hazards the report names as load-bearing: **insertion order is LIFO** (every panel block inserts `afterend` of `story-text-box`, so later blocks render ABOVE earlier — the table must be order-preserving by construction) and **remove-then-recreate semantics** (render is re-entrant; remove managed ids first, then create the active set). The §PLAY-01-G lesson applies directly — **`birkaNpcs` had five NPC cards keyed to dead pre-§WALK node codes and rendered nowhere for months**; special-case blocks keyed on `node.code` rot silently. Slices: **G1** NODE_PANELS (~700–900 lines → one table + ~40-line renderer; provable no-op via golden-DOM parity) · **G2** NODE_HOOKS (verbatim extraction, golden-DOM parity) · **G3** Class C per-arc (WM/cat/VS/NG+ CI → real gates; arc tests + `check:questgraph`) · **G4** Class D per-verb (child report first) · **G-FU** the 47 engine-region exceptions. **⟶ G1 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 (`25c3710` + fixes `bf6f486`/`7a130cd`):** 12 order-safe Class-A/B panels (CI tagline · AO name-change · KS conversion · LT stoning · §SIREN teaser · LCA close · Malta snake · Sweelinck variant · Quest −1 door · IST/TBS/MCT approaches) migrated into the ordered `NODE_PANELS` table + one `_renderNodePanels` loop at the stale-sibling sweep; once-flags (`saulConverted`/`maltaSnakeEvent`) via the table's `once` field; LIFO stacking replicated by construction (table order = old source order, all inserts afterend of `#story-text-box`). **Proof: 24-combo golden-DOM before/after diff = byte-identical** (DOM + flag writes; temp harness deleted after the proof), `uqf-node-panels.test.js` 7/7 permanent regression, `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303**, `npc-card-map` 23/23, all `check:walk` sub-checks green (QUEST:CORE 22519 bytes identical), only J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baseline reds. **Implementation refinements over the lab report (grep-before-building):** (1) the report's ~22-block Class-A count included the Birka-region sub-blocks, which anchor to `npcRowDiv` not `story-text-box` — a different anchor class, excluded from G1 (candidates for a second table after G2); only the courts shipped) → remapped to LSO, `quest_sea_overseer`'s activateNode (`bf6f486`); the §LXII AO name-change notice used `!visited['HTY']` as its once-guard, but `storyCollectLoot` flips `visited[code]` earlier in the same render, dead since it shipped → dedicated `aoNameNoticeSeen` once-flag (`7a130cd`). Docs synced: story.md §SIREN injections + maps.md junction rows flagged ⚠ NOT AS-BUILT (the section's plan topology/coords diverge from NODE_MAP — a candidate for a future maps.md sync pass). **⟶ G2 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28: NODE_HOOKS — 7 Class-E bespoke blocks (~440 lines) leave storyRender as verbatim registered hooks, proven byte-identical.** Extracted: Void Archaeology (Layer 52, 5-node investigate UI) · Void Shaman Warden (Layer 56) · Corelli Merchant (Layer 61) · Codex Core Chamber (§D01-10) · La Riva row (Layer 78) · Scholar Workshop (Layer 81) · Mimic Meadows (Layer 81) — each hook body is the VERBATIM former block, still self-gating. **One design deviation, recorded in the lab report §8:** the sketched "single dispatch loop" is order-UNSAFE — Corelli's gate is the computed `_checkCorelliAppearance` schedule (any of 5 nodes), so no single dispatch position avoids crossing a same-node co-firing block, and a keyed `NODE_HOOKS[code]` map can't even express that gate. Shipped shape = ordered `NODE_HOOKS` registry (`nodes` = tooling metadata, `null` for computed schedules) + **in-place dispatch**: `_runNodeHook(id, node)` sits at the exact source position each block occupied, so LIFO stacking is preserved **by construction** — the G1 order-safety rule needs no per-block exclusion analysis, and G2b/G3 candidates aren't order-blocked. **Proof: 14-combo golden-DOM before/after diff byte-identical** (story-column HTML + move-msg + quest/flag probes, 0 page errors both sides; temp harness deleted); new `uqf-node-hooks.test.js` **4/4** (registry integrity · one live surface probe per hook · LIFO pin vs the inline ZRH Secret Gate · source guard: no block body remains inline); regression `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303** + `uqf-node-panels` 7/7 + `npc-card-map` 22/22 (332/332 in one run); all four parity fences byte-identical (`MOVER` 1847 / `ROOMS` 10836 / `QUEST` 22519 / `DUEL` 7987), `check:gateast` 76 / `check:rng` / `check:questgraph` green; only the documented J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baseline reds. Side-finding: fresh SZG legitimately renders no workshop panel (the node's own `loot` auto-grants the Prototype Wand on arrival, gating the wand button off) — test probe adjusted, not a regression. **Deferred to G2b:** the Birka-region nested UIs (Blue Shutters · Froberger Memorial · Pachelbel/Couperin ledgers · Pit Championship · Kenickie shop · Kern & Sable) — `npcRowDiv` anchor / Layer-41 scope, need a ctx-argument design; CDG/Tilbury/Visby mixed blocks are G3's territory. Hazard #1 respected (`:1367` verified stopped). **Next slice: G3 (Class C per-arc quest activation → real gates), or G2b.** `storyRender` (`30502`–`34862`, **4,360 lines — the largest function in the file**) is two programs stacked: ~2,400 lines of hardcoded per-node special cases (76 `node.code === '…'` comparisons across ~40 blocks; Birka NPC cards alone are `31810`–`32917`) followed at `32918` by a compact, generic, data-driven `_mkSection`/`_mkCard` engine that renders Fish/Encounter/Quests/Loot/Rest/World from data. **The migration front is a horizontal line at 32918 and the open question is whether it's still moving.** Every `if (node.code === 'XX')` above it is a missing component — `isFishingLake` is what one looks like when it went right (add the flag in `NODE_MAP`, the node gets a fishing card, ~8 lines). This is plain ECS. **It is the same disease as the gate language one layer up:** *when the data language lacks a shape, the file grows imperative code instead of vocabulary.* **Which is why it is last** — fix the vocabulary in A and F first and a real fraction of the 2,400 lines becomes expressible as data rather than needing a bespoke hook. **Lock first:** whether node hooks are `[bits]` (reuse the VM — attractive once A lands) or registered render fns; and the §PLAY-01-G lesson applies directly — **`birkaNpcs` had five NPC cards keyed to dead pre-§WALK node codes and rendered nowhere for months.** Special-case blocks keyed on `node.code` rot silently. That is the argument for this increment, and it is not theoretical. **G3 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — Class C is done, and the silent-rot thesis proved out three more times over** (full record: lab report §9). The 5 activation blocks (NG+/WM/TL/VS/cat — 15 quests + vs_02/vs_03/vs_warden/la_riva_02/03 adjacent) became real `gate:`+`activateNode` data with two new host-understood fields — **`onActivate`** (absent → `📋 title` · null → silent · `{msg,delayMs}` → the arcs' bespoke staged narrations, verbatim) and **`boardExempt`** (NG+ remembrance never posts) — and the activation loop was extracted to `_uqfActivateAtNode`, run at storyRender START so per-node UI keyed on `'active'` sees same-arrival activations (the Solvak-button lesson). **Zero new gate leaves needed** (`countMin` on `ngPlusRun`, `questsDone`, `notFlags` covered everything — the "act ≥ N" legs were structurally dead: `actNumber` = `node.act`, constant per node). **What grep-before-building found:** (1) audit waves `a721254`/`ea02faf` had APPENDED duplicate `activateNode` keys below the authored `activateNode:null` — last-key-wins had the whole cat chain + tl_02/03 + vs_02/03/warden + la_riva_02/03 mass-activating with vacuous gates, so the legacy stanzas' staging was already dead; (2) `710bb75`'s SF→LCY "fix" was a wrong-target dead-code remap (SF = Storefront = STN) — tl_01/tl_03 pointed at the wrong city; (3) **`NODE_MAP.VS` and `NODE_MAP.TL` had no `code` field — the entire VS arc and the TL Vonn path had NEVER run in live play**; fixed via `./api.sh put node` (+ two latent double-pay bugs in the revived Ori/seal buttons fixed by paying through the quests' own onComplete chains, keyed on quest-active). (1) `choice` cannot be driven from anywhere in the game.** Inc A built the generator, the `ask` envelope, `_uqfPump` and `_uqfPending` — but never the half that RENDERS the ask and resumes with an answer: `_uqfPump` has **one** caller in the HTML (`_uqfRunToCompletion`, which **throws** on an unresolved ask), all **four** live `execBits` entry points wrap in it, `_uqfPending` is read by **nothing** outside two tests, and **`renderChoiceBlock` occurs 0 times** in the HTML *and* in `js/quest.js` — it has never existed as code (§DX-01e-FU2 reached this from the anchor side; this confirms it from the call-graph side). So G4 is `choice`'s first **host** consumer, not just its first content consumer, and **slice G4a is the driver, shipping with zero content migrated**. **(2) "Class D" is three shapes and `choice` fits one.** Of 21 button surfaces: **13 single verbs** (one button, no alternative — a one-option choice is not a choice), **7 exclusive choices**, and **CDG's 5 concurrent verbs** whose three boss buttons coexist by design, so mapping them onto `choice` would be an outright behaviour change. The vocabulary is **`NODE_VERBS` `{id, node, when, label, bits}`** in the `NODE_PANELS`/`NODE_HOOKS` house style (ordered, in-place dispatch); `choice` is a bit *inside* it. Forcing one tool on all of D would be the parent's own `itemsMinAny` warning coming true inside the slice written to honour it. **Grammar gap measured:** `item_remove` already ships, so only the **`cost`** half of the parent's leaf is missing — and it is **three currencies** (gold ×6 sites incl. the Junction Vignette `[Help — 10gp]` that proves it isn't single-use · `surgeCharges` · hp), of which **hp is narrated damage on an always-succeeding branch, not a price**. `reward` with negative gold would "work" with no affordability test and the word *reward* on a price — Hazard #2's real-but-wrong-object class. **Deliberately NOT migrated, each with a reason:** Entry 42 + the ZRH void-toll rune capture **free text** (a `prompt_text` ask for two sites is the single-use term the Host/Script Separation Policy forbids); The `cost` leaf's contract differs between them. **⟶ ANSWERED 2026-08-04: (a) refuse-at-click** — so `cost` never contributes to a verb's `when`, and a `hideWhenUnaffordable` opt-in stays available later without changing anything shipped. **⟶ G4a ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 `b905733` — the driver + the leaf, zero content moved (lab report §12½).** Kernel (`js/quest.js`, in-fence → re-inlined, parity **25,030 bytes** identical): the **`cost`** opcode `{gold?, resource?, count?, refuse?}` registered beside `reward` — its inverse, and the reason it must exist, since `reward` with `gold:-50` computes correctly with no affordability test and the word *reward* on a price (Hazard #2's real-but-wrong-object class). **All currencies are tested before any is spent**, so a mixed price never part-pays. It needed a mechanism the VM lacked — **chain failure** via `ctx._halt`, which `execBits` breaks on and **deliberately never clears**, because `ctx` is shared with the nested `execBits` a `choice` option runs: clearing it would let the bits after an unpaid branch run on. An `{ask:'choice'}` renders one button per option into the verb's mount, resumes the same generator with the picked index, and on completion removes the mount and re-renders the node with the chain's narrative as `storyRender`'s prefix (§BOARD-01-FU6's route). `storyRender` **abandons** a pending generator beside the sweep that takes its panel's DOM (safe by construction — `choice` applies the picked bits only *after* the pick), and a stale option button is **inert** because it re-checks that the slot still holds its own generator. **The skill_check scope fence is unchanged.** **Evidence:** `uqf-verb-driver.test.js` **12/12** with the positive control that matters — **against HEAD 10 of 12 fail and the 2 that pass are exactly the two asserting unchanged behaviour**; `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0**; Playwright **816 passed / 4 failed** (the documented four). A **D2 exclusive choice has no button**: the options *are* the surface, so giving Kern & Sable a `label` would have inserted a click the player never had to make. The mode is therefore decided by what the entry carries — **`label`+`bits`** = a button that runs on click · **`bits` with no `label`** = the chain IS the surface, runs at render, and its first bit **must** park on a `choice` · **`ambient`** = a flavour line for a state with nothing to do. Running a chain at render is exactly how side effects land on every draw of a node, so a label-less non-suspending chain is **refused with a warning — and refused BEFORE its mount exists.** The first implementation refused *after* inserting it and the test caught that: **a refusal that leaves an empty div behind still changed the page.** **Two behaviour deltas, named rather than discovered:** the driver re-renders when a chain ends, so the Q-NEXUS-01 follow-up appears **in the same beat** as [Ask] instead of on the next arrival (the fiction supports it — they are still at the counter, waiting — and it is asserted, not left to be noticed); and the **empty 8px spacer div** the inline block inserted in the "listened and walked away" state is gone. **Evidence, in the order it was built:** (1) a **multiset diff of the authored strings**, old block vs new table — **14 vs 14, identical, nothing added or lost** (the G2b line-multiset method adapted to a block that *could not* move verbatim: its shape changed, so only its content can be pinned, and pinning the content is what proves no prose was retyped); (2) a **golden-DOM diff of all four DUS states** against HEAD — text and button labels **byte-identical including the 6px/4px spacing**, since `_uqfRenderAsk` was given the first-button 6px every hand-written panel used, leaving only the mount's `id`, a `data-uqf-option` attribute, one redundant wrapper `<div>`+`<em>` dropped from an already-italic `.npc-ambient`, and delta 2; (3) **`uqf-node-verbs.test.js` 10/10 with all 10 failing at HEAD** — **not** G2b's "some pass both ways" shape, and the difference is honest: the surface's DOM identity changed with the migration, so equivalence lives in the golden diff, not in the after-state contract. ### §DX-01 — Developer-Experience & Repo-Health refactors (NEW 2026-07-22)

> **What this is.** A curated batch of *large refactor-style suggestions and side tangents* surfaced while writing **`prompt.md`** (the operating directive) and re-reading the full project on 2026-07-22. **Every claim below was grep-verified against the live repo this session** — line/count/red figures are measured, not recalled (grep-before-building). **None of these were built** — they are logged so the main task (prompt.md + README/index sync) could finish without turning a tangent into a detour, exactly the finishing discipline `prompt.md §2.9` prescribes. Each is its own increment; **each still needs the normal gate** (lab report only if it grows to a redesign; API-first; doc sync; verify). Ordered by payoff-to-risk.

- [x] **§DX-01a — `check:walk` fully green ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 (also closes §JUNK-01).** Both 20-day baseline reds retired; **no checker was taught an allowlist — the data was genuinely wrong, both ways, and both reds were the SAME defect.** Reproduced live first (identical to the row's figures), then: **(1) J14/J15 removed** — hand-edit per Hazard #4 with the server confirmed stopped (Hazard #1): the two `junction:true` NODE_MAP entries + NODE_COORDS rows + TGS's dangling `S:"J14"` deleted; repo-wide grep proved zero other live references (maps.md's "J14" is the *historical* Malta-approach node 104, a different entity; milepoints/ is a snapshot). **(2) Key discovery: R2's three off-component "settlements" 7,217/8,217/9,217 were exactly TGS+J14+J15** — the §KG highway tool dropped the stubs and never laid the road its own signpost promised ("The Tungas–Station 7 (Dark) Road"; SVO sits at 14,217, straight south). **(3) `build-roads.js --apply` regen** (the block's own "do not hand-edit" path; config/roads-pins.json untouched, unrelated region): cell-diff vs HEAD = **394/411 unchanged**, and every change is one of three intended stories — the real Tungas–Station 7 road laid (col 217 rows 8–13, TGS↔SVO), the TLL→SPB corridor rerouted off Gulf-of-Finland sea cell 10,207 (R3) via the row-8 TUO-stack corridor, and a local southern re-carve. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **all 10 gates green** — I1/I2/I3 (416/416 reachable, 1 component), R1–R4 (244/244 settlements, 0 overlaps, 410 cells 1.5%), all four parity fences + gateast 76 + rng + questgraph (0 prober-gaps, 0 nondeterminism); `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303** (also proves the inline `<script>` parses). Docs synced: prompt.md §7 baseline-reds → **"NONE — any red is a real regression"**; index.md counts 418→**416** nodes (+ stats-refreshed lines/size, `npm run stats`). §DX-01d (deprecating the tool that created the stubs) stays open — scope-fenced, no server code touched.

> *§DX-01b ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-22 `bd951d7` — the "~17–18-failure env baseline" was disproved (17 deterministic stale tests, no flake); 16 fixed, 17 → 1, the git-stash-diff ceremony retired; the 1 remaining red became §BOARD-01-VOID-GATE (since shipped). Full record: plan-archive.md §"Archived 2026-07-28 — §DX-01f".*

- [x] **§DX-01c — `./api.sh post monster` FIXED ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `dca81ae`. WBAPI Hazard #2 is retired, and with it the API-first exception that made all 398 monsters hand-authored.** The clean fix was in scope, so the "make it fail loudly" fallback applied only to *bad input*. **The row's premise held exactly, and the root cause was one the row hadn't named: `MONSTER_POOL` is the only section in the file that NESTS another.** `MONSTER_DROPS:START/END` (5796/6269) sits *inside* `MONSTER_POOL:START/END` (5354/6270), so `insertBeforeSectionClose`'s `lastIndexOf('\n};', endAnchor)` resolved to the **trophy-drops map's** closing brace. Every created monster was spliced into a real-but-wrong object — **which is why it never threw.** Stacked on that, `serializeMonsterLiteral` wrote a schema the game does not have: `{name, ac, hp, atk, dmg, xp, tier}` — no `key`, no `dmgDie/dmgCount/dmgFlat` (so the monster had *no damage at all*), and the string tier through `Number()` → `tier:NaN`. **Shipped:** (1) `WBAPI._sectionCloseIdx` in `wbapi-core.js` — stops the search at the first nested START anchor; `insertBeforeSectionClose` now delegates, so all 12 create call-sites are covered and the class can't recur if another section is ever wrapped. (2) `WBAPI.monsters.create/validate/serialize` — the live 9-field contract, measured (398/398 entries carry exactly `{key,name,ac,hp,atk,dmgDie,dmgCount,dmgFlat,tier}`, plus `voidTainted` on 2); `key` is always emitted (a keyless entry is the §AUDIT-03e shared-slot class). (3) `serializeMonsterLiteral` **deleted**; the POST route delegates and returns **422 + the offending field list + a worked example, writing nothing**, for an incomplete body, a numeric tier, or the retired `dmg`/`xp`. (4) Seven server help/`howToFix` curls that *taught the broken body* corrected, plus `tier=boss` → `deadly` in `api-user-guide.md` (there is no `boss` tier — the example would now 422). **Verified end-to-end against the running server, not just in unit:** `./api.sh post monster key=dock_rat …` → git diff = **exactly one line, inside MONSTER_POOL, before its own `};`**; the old-style body (`dmg=3 xp=10 tier=1`) → 422 listing all six defects; game file restored to its original md5 (`d5afa4a3…`) afterward. `tests/integration/dx01c-monster-create.test.js` **7/7** — including a test that **pins the trap** (a naive last-`};`-before-END must still resolve *inside* MONSTER_DROPS, so a revert fails here rather than in the trophy map) and one asserting the six non-nesting sections are unaffected. `check:walk` **12/12 green**; full Playwright **708 passed / 6 failed, all 6 proved pre-existing at HEAD by stash-diff** (the 4 documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` + 2 previously-undocumented — see §DX-02f). **Docs:** CONTRIBUTING.md Hazard #2 rewritten as a working recipe that keeps the defect class as the lesson (*a write that lands in a real-but-wrong object never throws*), prompt.md §3 hazard #2, potential.md, `monsters.md` gains an Authoring section, `api-user-guide.md` §9.3. **Side-findings filed, not detoured: §DX-01i** (`del monster` is model-only) **and §DX-02g** (2 off-contract tiers).

- [x] **§DX-01i — `del monster` is model-only ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `2f467f3` (jointly with §DX-01d — see that row for the full record).** The row's premise held, and **the scope was three times bigger than it named**: measuring it on a copy of the shipped file showed `monster`, `quest` **and** `node` all returning `ok:true`, all leaving `_rawSrc` byte-identical, all three surviving save+reload — the whole DELETE family, for every key, not just monsters and not just junctions. The row's suggested precedent (`the node-delete splice at wbapi-core.js ~1260`) turned out to be **dead code nothing ever called**. Original row text preserved in the §DX-01d record below.

- [x] **§DX-02g — the two off-contract tiers ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-31 `f229ede`. The row called it an authoring-surface defect. It was four live gameplay surfaces, and every one of them degraded SILENTLY.** The premise held exactly — `void_shaman:'rare'`, `void_rat_swarm:'low'`, the other 396 on contract — but the framing (*"this is an authoring-surface defect, not a gameplay one"*) was wrong, and measuring it before editing is what caught that. **`tier` is read in six places and every reader FALLS BACK rather than throwing:** `_voidEnrage(tier)` → `|| {atk:1,dmg:1,die:0}`, and The Warden matches `_VOID_ENEMY_RE`, so its §PLAY-01-B low-HP press ran at the **weakest magnitude in the game** (+1/+1) instead of hard's +3/+3 — on a sealed Act-5 guardian with a bespoke 4-item weighted drop table; `_storyRollInit` → `tierMod[t] ?? 0`, initiative +0 instead of +3; `_weightedMonsterPick` → `WEIGHTS[t] || 10`, and since `_inferTerrain` returns a *neighbouring node's* terrain, cells adjacent to **TBS** infer `epic_goblin_cave` — whose roster holds `void_shaman` — so Kazrath drew as a **random wilderness encounter at 2.5× a `hard` monster's weight** at low notoriety; `_fleeChance` → `?? 0.4` (moot for both — both match `_VOID_ENEMY_RE`, and Void enemies enrage instead of fleeing); and both threat badges rendered the literal **`RARE`** while asking for a **`.threat-rare` CSS class that does not exist**. **The tiers were derived from the corpus, not tasted:** `void_rat_swarm → easy` settled itself (6 of its 8 nearest stat-neighbours are `easy`; its exact AC/HP/atk twin is Jackalwere, `easy`). `void_shaman` was a genuine fork — its two **exact** stat-block twins (AC15/HP65/atk6 — Bandit Captain, Pirate Captain) are both `hard`, but the engine's own `_monsterLevel` puts it at 6, where 43 monsters are `medium` and only 4 are `hard` — so it was **asked with both readings measured, and the user chose `hard`**. **Shipped:** two `./api.sh put monster … tier=` calls (server restarted first, Hazard #1; stopped again after). **Evidence:** `git diff` = **exactly 2 lines**, alignment preserved; **golden parse diff across all 7 collections = exactly 2 entry diffs, both `fields=tier` alone**, 0 lost / 0 gained (416 nodes · 2,853 quests · 398 monsters · 273 npcs unchanged); new `dx02g-monster-tier-contract.test.js` **8/8**, of which **5 fail on the real pre-fix file** (restored byte-identical afterwards — the 3 that pass there are the ones that were already true); `check:walk` **14/14 exit 0**; full Playwright **740 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing new (740 = the 732 baseline + these 8). **§7½ DONE:** the pre-battle overlay at Void Shaman's Sanctum now reads a styled orange **HARD** badge instead of an unstyled `RARE`; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion:** the test also pins that **no tier-keyed engine map ships partial** — all 8 tier-keyed literals plus the 6 notoriety weight rows and the 5 `.threat-*` CSS rules must cover all five tiers, because a map missing a tier is this same defect from the other side and equally silent. **Docs:** `monsters.md` gained a *"the `tier` contract — five values, and it is not cosmetic"* section with the six-reader table and both derivations; its stale ⚠️ *"`del monster` only removes from the in-memory model"* warning (fixed 2026-07-30 by §DX-01d/i) retired in the same pass. **Two side findings filed, not swept: §DX-02h** (`void_rat_swarm` is unreachable content — 2 references in the whole file) and **§DX-02i** (`XP_BY_TIER` is declared and read by nothing). *(Original row: 2 monsters carry a tier outside the 5-value contract and are invisible in the worldbuilder picker — NEW 2026-07-30, found while measuring §DX-01c. The worldbuilder's monster `<select>` builds `byTier` from `TIER_ORDER` and pushes only `if (byTier[m.tier])`, so both are silently dropped from the picker, and `tierColors[mp.tier]` falls back to grey `#aaa`. §DX-01c's writer now rejects off-contract tiers, so no new ones can be created — these two predate it. **Risk:** trivial (2 field PUTs); the only judgement is which tier each belongs to.)*

- [x] **§DX-02h — the unreachable monster was one of 55, and the write path that was supposed to fix it reported success without saving ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `a047348`.** The premise held exactly — `void_rat_swarm` had 2 references, no roster, no battle, no quest — but **its framing was wrong twice**, and both were found by measuring before editing. **(1) It was not unique.** Scanning all 398 pool keys for a reference outside `MONSTER_POOL`/`MONSTER_DROPS`, **55 have none** — including its `voidTainted` twin **`void_wolf`** (identical 2-reference shape) and ~50 of the *partially* rostered Dark Fantasy Bestiary import (`fleder`, `cave_bear`, `koshchey`, `shaelmaar`, `ysbaddaden`, while neighbours `protofleder`, `warg`, `alp`, `basilisk` are live). The other 53 are filed as **§DX-02j**. **(2) The reason nobody noticed was a doc that read as if it shipped.** `world.md` §Void-Touched Monsters stated, in the present tense, that both were *"injected into terrain encounter tables at runtime when `voidPressure ≥ 6` via `_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`"* — that function occurs **0 times in the file**, and **2 of the 5 terrains it named (`dark_forest`, `mountain_pass`) have never existed as keys**. The doc was the thing that looked like evidence (§AUDIT-03l's class). **The row's own fix was blocked by a third defect: `PUT /api/terrain` accepted only `{label, icon}` — and persisted NOTHING.** It set `WBAPI.worldDb[k][f]` in memory, returned `ok:true` with the entity echoed, and never called `save()`; **`GET` then read the phantom edit back for the rest of the process's life**, so unlike the DELETE family (§DX-01d/i) the read path *corroborated* the lie. Root cause: `editField`/`editStructuredField` carried a `sectionMap` with **no `terrain` entry**, so `WORLD_DB` had no source-level writer at all — and the server's own advisory already told authors to `PUT {"monsters":[…]}`, an operation the endpoint rejected. **Shipped, API-first (endpoint before edit, prompt.md §2.5):** `terrain` added to the section map; a dedicated **`WBAPI.editTerrainRoster(key, monsterKeys)`**, because `WORLD_DB` is the one collection whose array holds **code identifiers** (`P.giant_rat`) — `editStructuredField` would emit `["giant_rat"]`, which re-parses to strings and silently drives `_monsterLevel` to 1 for the whole terrain (§DX-01c's real-but-wrong-object, third dimension); it re-parses the patched section and **proves the roster reads back before committing** (`deleteEntrySource`'s refuse-rather-than-corrupt), the server re-verifies after the disk reload, and `editField` now **refuses** `monsters` outright (patchStringField would find no quoted value and insertStringField would add a *second* `monsters:` key — §AUDIT-03a's rot). **Duplicates are allowed, not rejected**, because `cat_quarter` ships one (`fluffy_cat` ×2) and a writer that cannot express what the corpus holds cannot round-trip it — it warns. **Then one `./api.sh put terrain sewers monsters=…` placed both void kin** (user-chosen: `sewers`' single node is **SFT "Visby Sewers"**, act 5, and Visby is where `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS[21]` sights the first Void Walker; levels 3 and 4 sit inside its `giant_rat`→`vampire` band). **Evidence:** the defect proved by round trip first (`ok:true` + `GET` agreeing + **0 occurrences in the file**); `git diff` on the game file for the placement = **exactly 1 line**; **golden parse diff across all 7 collections = exactly 1 entry diff, `sewers fields=monsters`**, 0 lost / 0 gained (416 nodes · 2,853 quests · 398 monsters · 111 terrains); negative controls through HTTP (unknown field, unknown monster key) both refused with the file **byte-identical by shasum**; new `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **6/6**, of which **5 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the one that passes is the inverse shape guard, already true); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **750 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, nothing new. **§7½ DONE:** at SFT the pre-battle overlay renders **VOID RAT SWARM · Visby Sewers** with a styled green **EASY** badge (AC 12 · HP ~18 · ATK +4), and the engine's own `_weightedMonsterPick('sewers')` drew the swarm 172 and the wolf 241 times in 4,000; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion** — *every* terrain roster entry must parse to a stat block, not a bare key string — because a roster written as JSON re-parses without error and is invisible to every other gate. **The lesson, now proved in three directions (create §DX-01c, delete §DX-01d/i, update §DX-02h): a write path's acceptance test is a ROUND TRIP, and "GET agrees with me" is not that test.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING Hazard **#6**; `monsters.md` §"A pool entry is not content until a roster names it" (with the 55-of-398 measurement and the whole-roster-not-a-delta warning); `world.md` §Void-Touched Monsters drift-corrected; `docs/api/API-README.md` + `api-user-guide.md` (which had documented adding monsters via a PUT that could not do it, in raw `curl`). *(Original row: )* The whole file holds **exactly 2** references to the key: its own `MONSTER_POOL` entry (`void_rat_swarm:@5653`) and its `MONSTER_DROPS` trophy (*Void Shard*, sell 15, `void_rat_swarm:@6191`). It is in **no** `WORLD_DB` terrain roster, on **no** `node.battle`, in **no** quest and in **no** `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` — so unlike every other pool entry it can never appear in play, and the §DX-02g row's own assumption (*"both are live in terrain rosters, so they do appear in play"*) was true only of `void_shaman`. Now that its tier is on contract it is at least *selectable* in the authoring picker, which is what makes placing it cheap. Decide: place it in a Void-tainted terrain roster (it carries `voidTainted:true`, so the §TIDE-01 / Void-pressure surfaces are the thematic home) or retire it with its drop. **Grep first** — a swarm is exactly the kind of entry a planned arc reserves. **Risk:** low (one `./api.sh put terrain … monsters=` or one delete, which now actually persists — §DX-01d/i).

- [x] **§DX-02i — `XP_BY_TIER` deleted; it was not a stale copy of the shipped XP model, it was a different one ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `a047348`.** Premise held: one occurrence, its own declaration. **User chose delete over annotate**, and the divergence was measured before removing it rather than asserted: the shipped formula is `(S.enemy.ac || 10) * (S.opp.maxHp || 10)` (in `_grantMissEffortXp`, with the §XP-01 dials as percentages of that product), and against it the table paid **1.9×–6.5× less at every tier** — `deadly` a flat 1000 against a shipped mean of **4,095** across 33 monsters (min 1,649, max 12,012), `medium` 100 against a mean of 638. So adopting it would have silently cut the whole curve, not merely rescaled it. **Deleting it cost four dependents**, which is why the cheaper "comment it" option was on the table: `dx02g-monster-tier-contract.test.js`'s `TIER_KEYED_SITES` entry (would have failed *anchor not found*), `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js`'s resolver fixture (**swapped to `const EFFORT_XP_PCT`**, a live §XP-01 dial with exactly one occurrence), `check-npcregs.js`'s `NOT_NPC_KEYED` classification, and `index.md`'s Design Constants row — **whose text was itself wrong**, claiming the table was *"kept for reference — L12+ uses `AC × maxHP`"* when nothing consulted it at **any** level. A ten-line retirement note stands where the declaration was, naming the shipped formula and the measured ratios, so the next session that greps it finds the reason instead of a tempting table. **Side correction on the path:** the comment two lines below read *"Level 20 = 680,000 XP"* while `XP_LEVELS` ends at **195,000** — no entry has ever been 680,000; corrected in place (`index.md` had the right number all along). **Evidence:** `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0** (`anchors:fix` refreshed the 23 hints my ±9-line shift drifted); the §DX-02i assertion in `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **fails on the pre-fix tree**; full Playwright **750 passed / 4 failed**, the documented four. *(The illustrative `XP_BY_TIER → XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` rename examples in `scripts/resolve-anchors.js`'s comments are left alone — they are hypotheticals, and §DX-01e's ship record depends on that name for continuity.)* *(Original row: )* `const XP_BY_TIER = { trivial:25, easy:50, medium:100, hard:250, deadly:1000 };` had **one** occurrence in the file — its own declaration. Battle XP is computed as ≈`AC·maxHP` (and the §XP-01 effort grants are percentages of that), so this table is not merely unused, it encodes a *different* XP model than the one that ships — the exact "authoritative-looking but fictional" shape §AUDIT-03l warns about, one grep away from being wired up by a future session that assumes it is live. Decide: delete it, or comment it as a retired model. Do **not** wire it in without a balance pass — 2,850 quests and the whole level curve are calibrated against the shipped formula. **Risk:** none (delete) / high if naively adopted.

A *sentence* naming both tokens is prose and passes; a **copyable command** is an instruction and fails. Widened one notch in the row's own direction: the endpoint pattern is now `/api/(save|snapshots)` — `./api.sh snapshots` is a wrapper of the same vintage and was guarded by nothing. **The durable half is the positive control**, because narrowing an assertion is otherwise indistinguishable from deleting it: a second test runs the detector over four synthetic **instruction** shapes (fenced curl · inline-span curl · bare line · list-item `curl -XDELETE …/api/snapshots`) and asserts each is caught, then over four **prose** shapes (Hazard #7's own sentence · this test's own name in a doc bullet · *"use `./api.sh save`, never curl"* · a fenced curl to a *different* endpoint) and asserts none is. **Evidence:** file **8/8** (was 6/7); **negative control on a REAL doc** — appended a fenced `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` to `docs/api/api-user-guide.md`, the narrowed assertion went **red**, restored with `git checkout` + `shasum -c` **OK**; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **771 passed / 4 failed / 0 flaky** = the `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four alone, so the fifth red is retired and the 769/5 baseline recorded by §AUDIT-03k is superseded. **No doc prose was bent** — CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7 and prompt.md §7 still say what they said; `prompt.md` §7 records the retirement in the §DX-02e/§DX-02f house style. **The lesson, and the third instance of the class: the test was wrong, not the thing it tested** — and when a checker and a document disagree, the document that *records why the checker exists* is the last one you should rewrite. Original row follows. **§DX-02l-FU — the "no author-facing doc curls /api/save" assertion fails on PROSE about that very defect (found 2026-08-04 during §AUDIT-03k; a pre-existing red, not a regression).** `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js:190` flags any line in `api-user-guide.md` / `API-README.md` / `prompt.md` / `CONTRIBUTING.md` containing both `curl` and `/api/save`. **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7 — written by §DX-02l itself in `91d3650` — now contains the sentence *"verifying §DX-02k needed raw `curl`"* on the same line as `POST /api/save`,** so the gate reads a historical account of the defect as an instruction to commit it. Proved pre-existing: the test fails at HEAD with a clean tree (stash-verified, `shasum -c` OK), so the §DX-02l ship record's *764 passed / 4 failed* predates its own last doc edit. **This makes the standing baseline FIVE reds, not four**, until it is fixed. **Fix shape — a design call, which is why it is a row and not a detour:** either narrow the assertion to an actual *instruction* shape (a fenced command line / a line starting with `curl`, not a sentence mentioning both tokens), or reword Hazard #7 so the two tokens never share a line. Prefer the first — the second makes the prose serve the regex, and this repo has already paid once for a doc bent to a checker. **Risk:** low (test or doc, no engine).

Note the trap that hid this class: `world.md` described a runtime injection (`_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`) that **does not exist**, so the doc made two orphans look rostered; re-measure, never read placement off a doc (§AUDIT-03l). **Risk:** low per roster write (`./api.sh put terrain … monsters=` now round-trips, §DX-02h), but the encounter-weight balance of a terrain changes with every addition — add a few, eyeball §7½, do not bulk-dump 50 into one roster.

- [ ] **§DX-02n — two engine structures with ZERO readers, one of them carrying a doc comment that names a reader it does not have (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02b).** The §DX-02j class in a second dimension: not unreachable *content*, but unread *code and state*. **Two measured members.** (a) **`LOOT_TABLE` is dead data that documents itself as live.** `` `const LOOT_TABLE@24441` `` declares a full d100 loot table with the trailing note *"→ doc: docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md §D100 Loot Table (d100 result → item; used by `_rollD100Loot()`)"* — and the identifier `LOOT_TABLE` occurs **exactly once in the entire 38,707-line file**: on that declaration line. `_rollD100Loot()` reads `_D100_TABLE` (`` `const _D100_TABLE@24516` ``) instead. So the comment asserts a caller that does not exist, which is strictly worse than no comment, because the note is what a reader greps for and `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md` may be documenting the wrong table on its strength. **First job is to decide which table is canonical** — compare `LOOT_TABLE`'s rows against `_D100_TABLE`'s before deleting either; if they disagree, the *shipped* loot curve is `_D100_TABLE`'s and the doc needs re-anchoring, not the code. (b) **`S_story.ebReturnsCompleted` is a write-only shadow of `ebReturnDone`.** Both are initialised in `_S_DEFAULTS()` (`` `ebReturnsCompleted: {}@23086` ``, `` `ebReturnDone: {}@23085` ``) and both are written by the same function on adjacent lines (`` `S_story.ebReturnDone[ebCode] = true@30358` `` and `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted[ebCode] = true` at 30360). `ebReturnDone` has **12 read sites** — `_curseScore()`, `_missionComplete()`, the victory screen, the node tint, and the 20 `quest_e*_return` completions via `flagsPath:['ebReturnDone.XXX']`. `ebReturnsCompleted` has **zero**, and persists into every save file meaning nothing. **This is the quietest form of Hazard #2** — *a write into a real-but-wrong object never throws* — and here it is a write into a real-but-**unread** object, so even the round-trip acceptance test hazard #5 prescribes would pass it green: the field saves, reloads, and is still never consulted. **The durable half is a gate**, the §AUDIT-03j/n house style: a `check:deadconsts` phase that flags a top-level `const`, or a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field, whose identifier occurs only at its own declaration/write sites, with **explicit** classification (a deliberate reserve must be listed with a reason) so an unlisted one FAILS. Measure the corpus first — the two found here were found by hand while verifying an unrelated report, which is not a search. **Needs no design call for (b)** (delete the field + its two writes; it is unread by construction). **(a) needs a one-line call** once the two tables are diffed: delete `LOOT_TABLE`, or repoint `_rollD100Loot()` at it. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** for (b), grep proves 0 readers before and after, and a save round-trip is unchanged; for (a), a diff of the two tables recorded in the row, then `npm test`'s loot pins. **⟶ TWO MORE MEMBERS, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02h), both §CROWN-01 Amendment A.** (c) **`waneStoneDCReduced`** — one writer (`` `set:['waneStoneDCReduced']@11994` ``, `quest_wane_spawn.onComplete`), one declaration (`` `waneStoneDCReduced: false@23202` ``), **no third site in 38,707 lines**. Its lab report specifies it as *"future attempts on quest_wane_02 use DC:10"* — and `quest_wane_02` ships `retryable:false`, so there is no retry to discount even if a reader existed. A field written to record a rule the engine has no way to apply. (d) **`Marsh Seaweed`** — a *drop item*, not a field: it occurs **exactly once in the file**, at its own push (`` `name:'Marsh Seaweed'@7061` ``, from 5 monster keys), carrying `desc:'Mire seaweed. Burns to trace iodine salt at the inn hearth.'` **Nothing consumes it** — `quest_iodine_02`/`_03` require `Swamp Kelp` only. So it is vendor trash whose own description names a craft that does not exist (it is therefore also an §AUDIT-03v-cluster instance). **This widens the proposed `check:deadconsts` scope a THIRD time** — after `q.xpAward` (quest-entry fields) and `_setActivePath`'s dead `toCode` (parameters), now **item objects pushed to `S_story.inventory` whose `name` is never read back**. Neither (c) nor (d) needs a design call for deletion; (d) has the alternative of *giving* it a consumer, which is the more likely intent. **⟶ A FIFTH MEMBER, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02i), and it widens the scope a FOURTH time — to a dead alternative inside a fallback chain.** (e) **`visitedNodes`** — the activation-gate reader is `` `st.visited || st.visitedNodes@22055` ``, and `visitedNodes` occurs **exactly once in 38,712 lines**, in that expression. It has **one commit in the file's entire history**, and it is not a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field at HEAD *or* in the 2026-05-24 archive build. The live field is `visited`. So the right-hand branch is unreachable and always was: defensive code written for a field that was never declared. **A top-level scan cannot see this and neither can a field scan** — the identifier appears only as an operand, so `check:deadconsts` needs a phase for *alternatives in a `||` chain whose identifier has no declaration*. Deleting the branch is a no-op by construction; **no design call.** **⟶ (a) IS NOW MEASURED AND ITS DESIGN CALL IS ANSWERED, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02j) — the two tables were diffed and `_D100_TABLE` is unambiguously canonical.** `LOOT_TABLE` is a **d20** table (20 entries: 8 Minor · 2 Spell Scroll · 5 Healing · 3 Greater · 2 Superior — its own doc comment calling it a *d100* table is a third error in that one line); `_D100_TABLE` is 7 weight-100 rows including flashbang and gold, which `LOOT_TABLE` has no concept of. **The shipped loot curve is `_D100_TABLE`'s, so the fix is delete `LOOT_TABLE`, not repoint anything.** Expected potion healing differs materially — **27.75 HP/kill** under the dead table vs **21.0 HP/kill** live — so anything balanced against the doc was balanced against the wrong curve. **Two doc-side defects fall out, and they are the reason this is worth more than a deletion.** (i) **Both home docs already claim the array is gone.** `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md:253` says *"`LOOT_TABLE` | (removed) | Old d20 drop table — replaced by `_D100_TABLE` (§DROP-01 2026-06-05); definition now a comment stub"* and `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md:193` says *"`LOOT_TABLE` (old d20 array, Layer 25) removed; replaced by comment stub."* **There is no comment stub — HEAD still declares all 20 entries.** So two docs record a cleanup that never happened, which is the inverse of the usual rot and strictly more dangerous: a reader who greps the doc concludes the code is already clean. (ii) The archive kills the doc's own date — `LOOT_TABLE` had **exactly one occurrence at `32c10c5` (2026-05-24) too**, so it was already unread **twelve days before** the §DROP-01 commit the doc credits with replacing it. *(§DOC-02g's rule again: a migration note is a claim about the past, written from memory rather than from the diff.)* **Still no design call** — delete the array, delete the false pointer comment, correct both doc rows to *"removed 2026-08-xx"*. **⟶ SIX MORE MEMBERS FROM ONE FEATURE, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02k, §DUNGEON-01) — and one of them argues the gate must run the census in BOTH directions.** All six are `_S_DEFAULTS()` fields with writers and **zero readers**: (f) `voidFluxImmunityChoice` — one writer, always the literal `'fire'`, and it is the **entire reward** of `quest_d0209_a2`, a DC 12 Arcana check whose passText promises *"One spell effect will behave normally in this chamber"*; (g) `` `S_story.spiritDefeated = false;@21799` `` — **a `_legacy_fn` that sets a defeat flag to `false` at the moment of victory**, which is the sharpest member in the whole row because it is inert either way, so **no test and no gate can distinguish the bug from the intent**; (h) `mazeSolvedChecks` — three writers stepping 1 → 2 → 3, no reader, and the third writer is unreachable anyway (§AUDIT-03y); (i) `voidMazeEntered` — one `flag_write`, no reader, likewise unreachable; (j) `tribbleGladesFed` — one writer, no reader; (k) `cyMadnessTable` — one writer, no reader, and it directly contradicts its own lab report's §4, which states that `cyMadnessRoll` is *"the only persistent effect"* of failing the save. **The direction-widening point belongs to the sibling row §DX-02u:** `voidFluxCleared` is the **mirror image** of every member above — three readers and **no writer** — so it is not dead weight but a **broken dependency**, and a `check:deadconsts` scoped to *unread* identifiers walks straight past it. Whatever this gate ends up being, it must census **readers and writers separately and fail on either being zero**, with the gate-leaf flag names in `QUEST_DB` as the cheapest corpus to run it over (every one is a string literal).
>
> **⟶ EXTENDED 2026-08-11 by §DOC-02e — three more members, all found in one arc, and one of them changes the scope of the proposed gate.** (c) **`S_story.ceremoniaYaelAct`** — `` `ceremoniaYaelAct: 0@23151` `` plus **5 writes and 0 readers**. Its five writers are the Yael arc's `_legacy_fn` closures (`@21372`/`@21385`/`@21398`/`@21411`/`@21427`), each doing nothing but `S_story.ceremoniaYaelAct = N` beside a `mission_bit` that already records the same fact. **The 2026-05-26 spec declared it as a mirror** — *"mirrors `quests[q.id]==='done'` state"* — and a mirror is what shipped. Deleting the field removes **5 of the file's 154 `_legacy_fn` residues** as a provable no-op, which is an invariant-#4 cleanup for free. (d) **`S_story.skillCheckAttempts[id].failures`** — incremented at `` `failures: (prev.failures || 0) + 1@6999` `` on every failed retryable check; `_ceremoRetryBlocked` consults only `lastDay`, and nothing else reads it. Pure write-only. (e) **`q.xpAward` — 53 occurrences at HEAD, 33 of them on UQF-1.0 quests, and no reader anywhere in the file.** Every live mention outside a quest body is an §ARCH-01 migration comment; the only code hit is an unrelated battle-local `` `const xpAward@25292` ``. It is the **undocumented twin of `q.reward`**, which the engine *does* label — *"`q.reward` is intentionally NOT read — it is a dead/display-only field"*`@37045`. **An author reading `xpAward:200` beside a `reward{xp:200}` bit cannot tell which one pays**, which is the §DX-02n thesis at its sharpest. **Scope change:** the proposed `check:deadconsts` must therefore cover **quest-entry fields**, not only top-level `const`s — (e) is invisible to a top-level scan. **Needs no design call for (c)/(d)** (delete field + writes; unread by construction). **(e) needs a one-line call**: sweep the 53 fields, or label them dead in-file the way `q.reward` already is.

> **⟶ EXTENDED 2026-08-11 by §DOC-02f — one more member, a dead *parameter* rather than a dead field.** (f) **`_setActivePath(fromCode, toCode, dir)`'s `toCode` is never read.** `` `function _setActivePath@38195` `` is what survived the §CELL-11A corridor amputation: it shipped writing three things and now writes two (`lastExitCode`, `lastExitDir`), because `S_story.lastCorridorCells` died with `CORRIDOR_CELLS`. The middle parameter is still passed at its single call site (`` `_setActivePath(S_story.currentCode, destCode, dir)@28367` ``) and read nowhere in the two-line body. Harmless, but it is the same *evidence-of-an-amputation* shape as (b)'s shadow field, and worth having the sweep notice: **a dead parameter is a dead const the top-level scanner cannot see either**, so it belongs with (e) in widening `check:deadconsts` past top-level declarations. Needs no design call — delete the parameter and the argument together.
- [ ] **§DX-02o — a quest-id string literal in the engine can resolve to nothing, and no gate can see it (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02d).** §AUDIT-03j's class — *a missed lookup renders nothing* — in the **quest-id** dimension, which is the one node/npc-style dimension with no fence. **The live instance:** `` `crov:['quest_pit_training','quest_drunk_fight']@23644` `` inside `_hasActiveQuestFor`. There is no `quest_drunk_fight` entry in `QUEST_DB` — `git log -S` returns **1 commit**, the reference itself, so it was never a quest; the drunk pit fight shipped as an engine event (Layer 41's `_drunkFight` branch). `S_story.quests['quest_drunk_fight']` is therefore permanently `undefined`, `_hasActiveQuestFor('crov')` silently drops half its roster, and Weckmann's `questActive` dialogue tier can only ever fire for `quest_pit_training`. Nothing throws. **Corpus measured, and it is small:** **302 distinct `quest_*` literals in the file, 301 resolve, 1 does not** — the only other two misses (`quest_whisper_`, `quest_wane_`) are legitimate `startsWith` prefixes and must be exempted, not fixed. **The gap is structural, not incidental:** `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself (write-sets, gates, reachability) and never reads engine JS; `check:noderegs` mentions `QUEST_DB` **zero** times. **The durable half is a gate**, the §AUDIT-03j/n/m house style: a `check:questrefs` phase resolving every `'quest_*'` literal outside the `QUEST_DB` section against the live id set, comment-aware (the §AUDIT-03f requirement), with an **explicit** `NOT_A_QUEST_ID` classification list (the two prefixes, plus the `quest_item` type string) so an unlisted miss FAILS. **No design call** — the one live hit is a delete (drop the dangling id from the array) and the rest is the fence. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** the gate exits 1 on a planted bogus id and 0 at HEAD after the delete; `_hasActiveQuestFor('crov')` still returns true while `quest_pit_training` is active.

- [x] **§DX-02k — the argless `save()` was not a stray test call: it was the server's own per-write path, and it stamped a 5.4 MB snapshot on every PUT ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `2ead185`.** The premise held — `save(outputPath)` fell back to `getStampedName()` and left dated files behind — but **both of the row's framings were wrong, and the measurement is what showed it.** **(1) The destination is the process CWD, not "next to the module."** `getStampedName()` returns a **bare filename**, and `fs.writeFileSync` resolves a bare name against `process.cwd()`. Proved by loading the repo's HTML from a scratch directory: the snapshot landed in the scratch directory. It read as "the repo root" only because that is where the server runs — a script run from anywhere else littered *there*, silently. **(2) The argless callers were not scripts. The SERVER was.** `saveAndRestart` and `saveAndVerify` — the persist step behind **every** successful PUT/POST/DELETE — ran `WBAPI.save()` argless, then `copyFileSync(snapshot, GAME_FILE)`, and **never removed the snapshot**. Two more argless sites sat in the batch endpoints. So the row's own proposed fix, *"make the argless form server-only,"* would have left the actual litter source untouched. And the snapshot was never a backup of the pre-write state — **it holds the NEW text**; its only job was to be the byte source of the copy, which makes it pure redundancy the moment the copy lands. **The check the row asked for came back the other way, too:** `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` / `monitor-snapshots.py` do depend on the stamped *name*, but `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md` documents their input as a **deliberate `cp`** — so the per-write stamping was not feeding a feature, it was drowning one: a curated session history turned into per-keystroke noise (patch chain last advanced **2026-07-09**, no watcher running, **6 orphans / ~32 MB** in the root). **Shipped:** `save(dest)` writes exactly where told and **refuses with no argument**, naming the alternative in the error · new **`WBAPI.saveStamped([dir])`** — the deliberate dated backup, landing **beside the source file** (`POST /api/save`, `wbapi save`, the audit-fix + junction-nuke endpoints; the last four also had the bare-name CWD bug) · new server-only **`saveGameFile()`** — temp beside the game file + **atomic rename**, so nothing is left to sweep *and* a reader can never catch a half-written game file; on failure the temp is kept and its path returned. **Evidence:** the CWD claim proved by a scratch-directory probe **before** any edit; **the pre-fix test run demonstrated the bug on itself** — stashing the fix and re-running dropped **two more 5.4 MB snapshots into the repo root**, which is the whole defect in one observation; new `dx02k-save-destination.test.js` **6/6**, of which **5 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the passer is the `save(dest)` round trip, already true); live server round trip = **3 successful writes** (`act=1` idempotent, `act=2`, `act=1` back), each verified on disk by grep and through `GET`, ending at the **original shasum** with **0 new snapshots and 0 temp files**; `POST /api/save` still returns a dated backup beside the game file, so the archive surface is intact; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **756 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four (+6 new tests over the 750 baseline), with `multiplayer-presence:171` **flaky, retried green** — a first run showed it red on both attempts and a second full run reproduced the documented baseline exactly, so it is the known flake, not a regression; root swept to **one** `roll2hit-v3*.html`, and two full suite runs added none back. **The durable half is the inverse assertion in the test: no shipped code may call `WBAPI.save()` with no destination.** **The lesson: "did it persist?" is only half of a write path's acceptance test — the other half is "and nowhere else."** A stray write throws nothing, changes no behaviour, and is found only by looking at the directory. Docs: **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7**, `index.md` (Version Snapshots + `wbapi-core.js` persist surfaces), `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (which described `POST /api/save` as how writes reach disk — they already did), and annotations on the three historical notes that still traced the old stamped path (`lab-report-wbapi-architecture.md` §4.2, `data-code-migration-into-cells.md`, `cell-resume-prompts.md`). *(Original row: `save(outputPath)` falls back to `this.getStampedName()`, which writes a dated `roll2hit-v3-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` snapshot **next to the module — the repo root**. That is right for the server's autosave, but any test or one-shot script calling `save()` argless leaves a multi-MB file behind on every run; two such snapshots are sitting in the root right now from earlier sessions, invisible because the pattern is gitignored. `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` was fixed to pass `save(tmp)` explicitly. Decide: have `save()` refuse when the destination would be the repo root outside a server context, or add a `saveTo()` and make the argless form server-only; either way sweep the existing snapshots. **Risk:** low, but check `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` first — one of them may depend on the stamped-name behaviour.)*

- [x] **§DX-02l — the dated-backup surface had no `./api.sh` wrapper, and the sweep it wanted would have destroyed the history it exists to keep ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `91d3650`.** The premise held exactly as filed: `./api.sh save` answered `✗ Unknown command "save"`, `api/wb.js` held **0** occurrences of `save`/`snapshots`, and three lines of the *author-facing* guide (`api-user-guide.md` §17.13 and both worked-example workflows) told authors to reach the surface with `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` — the golden rule broken in the docs that teach it. **Shipped API-first, endpoint before wrapper (prompt.md §2.5):** `GET /api/snapshots` (the dir, per-file size/mtime, total, and whether the patch chain already holds it) and `DELETE /api/snapshots` (nonce-guarded like every other destructive route), then `./api.sh save` and `./api.sh snapshots [--sweep] [--force]`. `snapshot` had to be added to the nonce endpoint's `validTypes` — it is the one type with **no collection behind it**, since the sweep destroys files, not entries, so the key resolution is skipped rather than run against `undefined`. **The row's framing of the sweep was the thing that changed on contact.** It called the disposal *"a `rm` a human has to remember"* — but `archive-snapshots.sh` **already disposes of them**, and it does so by diffing each snapshot into a `milepoints/patches` delta and *then* `rm`-ing the file. So a blunt sweep is not a chore automated, it is **the patch chain's input deleted before it is read**: an unarchived snapshot is the only copy of that state. `--sweep` therefore deletes only what the chain already holds (`<stem>.patch` present, or named by `_last.name`) and **names `./archive-snapshots.sh` in the skip reason**; `--force` is the deliberate discard. **Evidence:** live round trip on the real server before any test — `./api.sh save` → 5.2 MB backup beside the game file → `snapshots` listed it `unarchived` → `--sweep` **kept it** and said why → `--sweep --force` freed it, with `roll2hit-v3.html` at its **original shasum** and the root back to one file; new `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js` **7/7**, and **all 7 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — nothing here is vacuous); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **764 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, **0 flaky**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring surface only; nothing a player sees changed.** **The durable half is what the sweep is allowed to name:** a decoy suite (`roll2hit-v3-notes.html`, `-2026.html`, `-20260709.html`, `-20260709-0341.html`, `.htm`, `.html.bak`) survives a **forced** sweep beside the game file, because only an exact `-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` stamp is reachable — the stamp is the entire guard between *delete the backups* and *delete the game*. The test also pins the row's own lesson: **no author-facing doc may reach `/api/save` with `curl`.** **The lesson: an endpoint whose only documented client is `curl` is not a finished write path — the wrapper is what makes it real, and "it has no wrapper" is usually visible in the docs before it is visible in the CLI.** Docs: `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (§17.13 rewritten + both workflows + the endpoint card), `API-README.md` (new *Dated backups* section + 3 mapping rows), `wbapi-help.md`, `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `index.md`, `prompt.md` §3 + §9, **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7** (which now carries the reporting half: the directory is gitignored, so `./api.sh snapshots` is the only thing that will ever tell you what is in it), and `./api.sh help` gained a *Persistence* block under SERVER LIFECYCLE saying plainly that you do **not** need `save` after a write. *(Original row: )* Verifying that the dated-backup surface still worked needed a raw `curl -X POST http://localhost:1367/api/save`, because `./api.sh save` answers `✗ Unknown command "save"`. prompt.md §3's golden rule is that authoring never falls back to raw `curl` — *"if `./api.sh` lacks a command you need, add the endpoint + a named `./api.sh` wrapper"* — and here the endpoint exists while the wrapper does not, which is the same gap read from the other end. Small: an `api/wb.js` command that POSTs `/api/save` and prints the `backup` / `primary` paths. Worth doing together with a `./api.sh snapshots` (list/sweep the root's dated files) now that they are produced only on request — the sweep is currently a `rm` a human has to remember. **Risk:** trivial; read-modify of the CLI dispatch table only, no game-file writes.


- [x] **§DX-01d — the junction-node CLI hazards ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `2f467f3` (closes §DX-01i in the same increment). Hazards #3/#4 are retired and a new Hazard #5 records the defect class.** Taken as the board's named "unambiguous pairing" while §AUDIT-03d's design call stays open. **The row's `del node J##` half was right about the symptom and wrong about the scope, and that was the finding:** measured in-process against a *copy* of the shipped file (real game file never written), `monster:fish_01`, `quest:mq_1` **and** `node:RUH` each returned `ok:true`, each left `_rawSrc` **byte-identical**, and each **came back after save+reload**. It was never junction-specific and never monster-specific — **every `delete()` in `wbapi-core.js` was the single line `delete WBAPI.<collection>[key]`**, and the server's generic `DELETE` handler ended at `json(...)` with **no `saveAndVerify` at all**, so nothing reached disk even in principle. `WBAPI.deleteNodeSource` — the one function that *did* patch the source — was **dead code with zero callers** (and its private brace-walker missed `/* */` comments and left the `NODE_COORDS` row orphaned anyway). **This is the exact mirror of §DX-01c: a write path that reports success without persisting. Same standing lesson both directions — the failure is silent because nothing ever throws.**
  **Shipped (1) the deleter:** `WBAPI.deleteEntrySource(section, key)` — one source-level excision for all four types, **verify-or-revert** (after the splice the section's depth-1 key *multiset* must differ by exactly one instance of `key`, else the splice is rolled back and the delete fails loudly instead of corrupting the section), clamped past nested START anchors so a `MONSTER_POOL` delete can never reach into the trophy-drops map (§DX-01c's real-but-wrong-object trap, mirrored). **(2) one scanner, not two:** `check-dupkeys.js`'s proven comment/string/regex-safe `scanTokens` **moved into `wbapi-core.js`** and is imported back by the gate (`WBAPI._scanTokens`), so the audit gate and the writer can never disagree about what an entry is — a private copy would drift, which is the §AUDIT-03f silent-drop class again. **(3) cascades:** a node takes its `NODE_COORDS` row, a monster takes its `MONSTER_DROPS` trophy — no orphan left for `./api.sh audit`. **(4) the server persists and proves it:** the generic `DELETE` now routes through `saveAndVerify` with a new **`postVerify`** hook (a delete's proof of success is an *absence* in the re-parsed collections, which the `expectedFields` path cannot express) and returns `deleteVerified:true`, or a 500 if the entry survived. **(5) `highway --execute` deprecated and refused**, up front before the coordinate fetch (so it costs nothing and needs no server), with the real recipe in the error: `ROAD_RUNS` + `build-roads.js --apply`, and `./api.sh reachability` as the authority for why waypoints were never necessary. `deleteNodeSource` kept as a thin delegating wrapper. **Verified:** new `tests/integration/dx01di-delete-persists.test.js` **8/8** — round trip per type, both cascades, **the 20 largest nested UQF bodies each excised individually** (largest 3,350 bytes of braces/strings/arrow-fns; each must cost exactly one entry), verify-or-revert, the `blockedBy` guards still blocking with the source byte-identical, and the highway refusal. **End-to-end through the live server, twice, each a byte-identical no-op:** `post monster dx01i_probe` → `del monster` → `grep -c` **0** (the row's exact reproduction, which used to leave **1**); `post node ZZP` → `del node` → **0**; game file `shasum 018ee943…` **unchanged** both times. `check:walk` **12/12 green (exit 0)**, `check:dupkeys --selftest` 4/4 on the relocated scanner. Full Playwright **717 passed / 4 failed** — the baseline is **exactly** the documented four in `worldbuilder-crud-arrays.test.js`, plus the known `multiplayer-presence` flake (passed on retry); 717 = the 710 baseline + these 8, minus the flake counted separately. **Docs synced:** CONTRIBUTING.md Hazards #2/#3/#4 updated and a new **Hazard #5** written to keep the class (*whenever you add a write path, the acceptance test is a round trip — save, re-parse, assert the change survived*), prompt.md §3 hazards #4/#5, `api-user-guide.md` §10 (cascade/verify/guard table), `API-README.md`, and `./api.sh help` — whose §12 `del` section now states the guarantee and whose §21 no longer advertises **eleven** `highway --execute` invocations that would die. *(Original row: "Fix or deprecate the junction-node CLI hazards — both documented as Hazards #3/#4 and the upstream cause of §DX-01a's J14/J15 reds; `highway --execute` drops sparse `junction:true` nodes and zero road cells, `del node J##` desyncs model from file.")*

- [x] **§DX-01e — Retire line-number anchor drift ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-31 `57d79d7`. The row asked for a convention; the measurement is what made the case: 43 of the 50 anchors in the live docs were stale, and every single one of CONTRIBUTING.md's ten.** **The convention:** an anchor is one token, `` `symbol@1234` `` — the **symbol** is the pointer (any literal substring of the line meant, optionally file-qualified: `` `js/wbapi-server.js:function seededNext@1147` ``), the **number is a cached hint**. That is §DX-02e/§DX-02f's lesson in a third place: *pin the property, never the generated coordinate* — a line number is generated by every edit above it. **Measured before building:** of 14 bare anchors sampled at HEAD, **9 pointed at unrelated code** (`22104`, cited for `_rollSkill`, had become a prof-bonus line; `31618`, cited for the §CELL-03 position sync, an `aurosBlueprintKnown` branch); repo-wide the bare form appears **720** times across 27 files. **Shipped:** `scripts/resolve-anchors.js` — audit (exit 1 on findings) · `--fix` · `--legacy` (migration tracker) · a lookup mode that replaces `grep -n` (`node scripts/resolve-anchors.js execBits`) · `--selftest`; **`check:walk` gate #15 `check:anchors`**; `npm run anchors` / `anchors:fix`. **The gate's failure condition is deliberately narrow: a DEAD symbol fails, a drifted number only warns.** Failing on drift would re-create the per-increment tax the row exists to remove, and `anchors:fix` refreshes all of them in one command. Resolution is **identifier-boundary aware** — found while building the negative control: plain substring matching let `XP_BY_TIER` → `XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` still 'resolve', so a *rename*, the exact thing the gate is for, would have passed. **Migrated (the live docs only):** CONTRIBUTING.md 10 · mechanics.md 3 · docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md 2 · **every bare anchor in BACKLOG.md's OPEN rows, 0 left**. Closed ship records, `plan-archive.md` (211) and the lab reports (~300) keep theirs — **history is annotated, not rewritten** (§DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m precedent). `potential.md`'s 36 are the only live-doc remainder → filed as **§DX-01e-FU**. **Two stale claims surfaced by re-anchoring and corrected in place** (both in CONTRIBUTING's Host/Script policy, both about work that has since shipped): the seeded-RNG bullet still said *"the client rolls the same encounter from `Math.random()` … until §VM-01-B lands"* — §VM-01-B landed, the client draws `_seededNext()`, and what still diverges is the *weighting*, not the stream; and the `_legacy_fn` bullet cited `quest_1367_f_plague` as *"rolls `Math.random() > 0.5` inside quest data"* when **no `_legacy_fn` line in the file contains `Math.random` any more**. **Evidence:** 6-phase `--selftest` (dead · stale · fresh · fix · boundary · qualified); **negative control on the real game file** — renamed `const XP_BY_TIER` in place, gate exits 1 naming `BACKLOG.md:312`, file restored and `shasum -c` **OK**; `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **4/4**, and the migration test **fails on the pre-migration docs** (`git stash` of the 4 doc files → 1 failed / 3 passed); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; the audit reports **50 anchors across 4 docs, 0 dead**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only; nothing a player sees changed. *(Original row: BACKLOG.md carries 182 bare 5-digit line anchors; they rot on every HTML edit, and nearly every §VM-01 row ends with "re-grep the anchors before the next edit — they will drift." Propose a lightweight, durable convention + a tiny `scripts/resolve-anchors.js`. **Payoff:** removes a per-increment tax and a whole class of stale-pointer bugs. **Risk:** low; adopt incrementally, no big-bang rewrite.)*

Everything else still on the bare form is **history** (`plan-archive.md` 211 · the lab reports ~300 · closed ship records) and is deliberately left alone — annotate, don't rewrite. Method: name what each anchor points at, keep the old number, then `npm run anchors:fix` (the tool refuses to guess a number for a symbol it cannot find, so a dead pointer surfaces as a gate failure rather than a silent re-point).
