### §AUDIT-03 — Quest-data appended-field rot (NEW 2026-07-28, surfaced by §VM-01-G3)

> §VM-01-G3 found that the bulk audit waves `a721254` ("80+ passText/failText/activateNode/desc/hint fields patched") and `ea02faf` ("wire npc field on all 985 quests") APPENDED fields to entries that already declared them — JS last-key-wins silently flipped authored values — and `710bb75` ("Fix 8 activateNode errors: SF→LCY, CQ→CDG, FR→AMS") remapped at least two dead codes to the WRONG target. G3 fixed the ~20 quests in its five arcs; these rows are the corpus-wide sweeps. All are data-only audits — grep/parse first, fix through `./api.sh` where expressible (⚠️ Hazard #1 for hand-edits).

- [x] **§AUDIT-03a ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — `check:dupkeys` is `check:walk` gate #11; the 2 corpus survivors fixed.** New `scripts/check-dupkeys.js`: string/template/comment-safe token scanner over all 11 WORLDBUILDER sections (~4,800 entries), stack-of-maps per object literal so a duplicate key at ANY nesting is caught while sibling array elements/function-body braces never false-positive; `--selftest` proves clean-pass + planted-dup-caught. **Corpus findings: exactly 2 survivors** (beyond G3's arcs): `quest_la_riva_01` + `quest_no_fishing_sign`, both authored `activateNode:null` + appended `activateNode:"CDG"` — both quests are CODE-activated (la_riva_01 by the Cat-King-defeat handler with Kenickie's staged monologue; no_fishing_sign by the coupon pull-tab), so the shadowing append mass-activated them at CDG, bypassing their staging. Fixed per the G3 pattern: appended line removed, authored `null` kept (hand-edit — dup-removal isn't expressible via PUT; server confirmed stopped, Hazard #1). Their `npc:"long_john_silver_sen"` stamps left for §AUDIT-03b (scope fence). **Wired into `package.json` `check:walk` (selftest + audit), which is the single CI step in walk-invariants.yml — an append can never silently flip an authored value again.** Evidence: `check:walk` all 11 gates green; `quest-runtime-uqf` 303/303; prompt.md §7 gate count synced 10→11. Note: MONSTER_POOL count reads 796 because that section legitimately holds both `MONSTER_POOL` + `MONSTER_DROPS` objects (2×398 — both audited); ITEM_DB is genuinely empty; FISH_POOL is array-rooted (fields audited at element level, entry count reads 0 — cosmetic).
- [x] **§AUDIT-03b ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — all 203 mis-stamped quests re-anchored to their real NPC; the vocabulary and the field patcher fixed underneath.** `ea02faf` had bulk-defaulted `npc:"long_john_silver_sen"` onto **203** quests. **Established first (step 1, prior session): `q.npc` is authoring metadata only** — the game client has ZERO quest-level `.npc` reads, so no gameplay risk and no golden-state diff was needed; consumers are worldbuilder display, `_questsByNpc`, `advise` (unresolvable key ⇒ *warning*), `audit` (**missing** field ⇒ *error* — the pressure that caused the bulk default), and the NPC delete-guards.
  **Result: 203/203 re-anchored correctly · exactly the 7 genuine Silver quests kept (`sen_c1a2/a3`, `sen_c4a1/a2/a3`, `sen_c7a1/a2` — every scene is built on him) · 196 changed · 0 unresolved · 0 collateral.**
  **Three defects found and fixed on the way (the row's own premises included):**
  1. **The accepted vocabulary was too narrow.** `_npcOk` (duplicated in two `wbapi-server.js` closures) and `advise` accepted only BIRKA_NPC + normalized `NODE_MAP` inline `npc` — so real, rendered speakers living in `NPC_DIALOGUES` (`jimmy`/`solvak`/`yva`/`rennau`/`kenickie`/`isolde_voss`/`benedikt_rasp`/`sandy_cat`) and the 20 `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` quest-givers would all have advise-warned. Replaced with **one shared `WBAPI.npcKeyOk` / `npcKeyVocab()` in `js/wbapi-core.js`** over four registries (BIRKA_NPC · NODE_MAP inline · NPC_DIALOGUES · EB_NPC_DIALOGUE), both server closures now delegate to it, and `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` is parsed for the first time (it lives OUTSIDE the WORLDBUILDER markers, so it is read off the raw source). **Vocabulary 270 → 297 keys**; a bogus key is still rejected.
  2. **`patchStringField` was not depth-aware — a silent no-op class.** The first batch reported `patched:196, errors:[]` but **6 writes never landed**: `editField('quest', id, 'npc', …)` matched the FIRST `npc:` token anywhere in the entry, which for `quest_wm_02/wm_03/tl_01/tl_03/vs_01/vs_02` is the **nested** `{ kind:'favor', npc:'…' }` inside `onComplete`. Those six happened to have a target key equal to the nested value, so the write was value-preserving (quote style only) — but any quest where they differed would have had its **favor bit rewritten instead**. Fixed with a new `firstTopLevelMatch(body, re)` helper: a single string- and comment-aware pass builds a depth map, and only a match at brace depth 0 of the entry body can be chosen (comment-awareness per the §AUDIT-03f lesson). Re-ran the 6 → all correct, favor bits untouched. **Proof of zero collateral across the whole corpus: all 16 `kind:'favor', npc:` values byte-identical before vs after (git-diff extraction), and the game-file diff is exactly 196 insertions / 196 deletions.**
  3. **The row's `hunt2 → tessie` derivation was wrong.** Tessie is not an `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` giver — enumerating the object gives 20 givers and she is absent; she exists only in `quest_hunt2_*` prose. Re-anchored `hunt2_01–04` to **`the_elder_fisherwoman`**, the §BOARD-01-FU6 referral source that hands the WRO thread off (the `hunt_01` narrative that names Tessie's road).
  **The ~12 OPEN per-quest calls were resolved by reading each quest's text** (recorded in the mapping script): `spark_01/02/05`→`magistra_elara_muffat` (LCY anchor; Inspector Wren-Pembury/"Aldous" has no key) · `spark2_01–05`→`mairén_fionn` (DNF anchor; Commissioner Fehn has no key) · `wis_00/06/07`→`solvak` and `wis_04`→`warlord_kael_mordus` and `alch_02`→`elder_fionn` (Roen has no key anywhere — §AUDIT-03i) · `wis_03`→`captain_vera_keel` (GCI's own NPC; the text says "Keel talked about the commission") · `vs_warden`→`solvak` · `va_01–04`→`benedikt_rasp` (va_04's text names him outright) · `ng_01–03`→`city_guard_captain` (LHR anchor) · `ca_01`→`magistra_elara_muffat` · BOO trio + `no_fishing_sign`→`the_fisherman` · `shore_01`→`the_tide_reader` and `sunken_02`/`depth_01`→`the_stone_inscription` (chain-sibling consistency) · `cat_06`→`jimmy_two-tails` ("Tommy" has no key) · `solm_01`→`proprietor_dusk` (the Map Shop scene) · `mol001_act2`→`bolli_bollason` (his household's men hold the ford) and `mol001_act3`→`gudrun_osvifr` (Helgi Osvifsson unkeyed → the chain namesake, = act1's own stamp). The anchoring rule is now written into `quest.md`.
  **API-first honored:** the purpose-built `POST /api/batch/npc` endpoint existed but had no wrapper, so **`./api.sh batch-npc <updates.json>`** was added to `api/wb.js` (+ both help blocks) — one parse and one save for the whole batch instead of 196 full-file rewrites. Hazard #1 respected throughout: the `js/*.js` edits were made with the server stopped, then the server was restarted on a fresh PID before each write session, and the `_monsterLevel` signature survived both saves.
  **Evidence:** `check:walk` **11/11 green** (exit 0, incl. `check:dupkeys` + the §AUDIT-03f parse-parity phase) · `quest-runtime-uqf` + `npc-card-map` **325 passed** · new permanent `tests/integration/audit03b-npc-anchor.test.js` **4/4** (the 7-keep corpus assertion · the only-10-tolerated-unresolvable-keys pin · the 4-registry `npcKeyOk` · the depth-aware-patcher regression) · `node --check` clean on `wbapi-core.js`/`wbapi-server.js`/`wb.js` · quest count **2,853** unchanged. Docs synced: `quest.md` gained an "`npc` field — authoring metadata, not gameplay" section (the four registries + the anchoring rule + the `batch-npc` recipe). Side findings logged as **§AUDIT-03g/h/i** below rather than fixed inline.

Only **`SF → LCY` was wrong** — already fixed by §VM-01-G3 (`SF` = the Storefront = `STN`). **The prior session's open question — "should one arc sit at two nodes?" — is answered and moot:** the La Riva arc is legitimately **bi-nodal** (Kenickie sends you east from `CDG` · Connie + Aldo at `AMS` · the account book goes back to Kenickie at `CDG`, hence `la_riva_03`'s `waypointNode:'CDG'`), and **5 of the 8 remapped lines no longer exist** — they were the appended duplicates §AUDIT-03a / §VM-01-G3 removed, so all three `la_riva` quests are `activateNode:null`, staged by code (`01` by the `CQ_KING` defeat handler, `02`/`03` by the AMS hooks). **The audit's real product — a corpus scan: 0 of 2,853 quests name a node that does not exist**, and it covered **`waypointNode`, which the original audit never checked at all.** **One data fix:** `quest_cat_06` was anchored to `jimmy_two-tails` (CDG's inline-name slug) while its three siblings use the `NPC_DIALOGUES` profile key `jimmy` — one character filed under two `_questsByNpc` headings (§AUDIT-03k); collapsed to `jimmy` via an in-process `WBAPI.editField` write (the §TIDE-01-A path, byte-identical to the server's PUT; no daemon, per the no-background-processes rule). **Most likely source of the whole class, measured and filed as §AUDIT-03l: `maps.md`'s LEGEND table — the doc an author reads to look up a node code — has 73 of 76 rows naming codes that do not exist** (it predates the airport-code naming; its `Node #` column is still correct and is the recovery key). The circumstantial case is strong: those three codes appear nowhere else, and `quest_tl_01` carries `activateNode:"SF"` beside a live `waypointNode:'STN'` **for the same node** — one field read off the table, the other off `NODE_MAP`. Neutralized today with a hazard banner above the table; the remap is its own increment. **Evidence:** golden parse diff across all 7 collections = **exactly 1 entry diff, `quest_cat_06`, `fields=npc` alone**, 0 lost / 0 gained, still 2,853 quests; new permanent `tests/integration/audit03c-node-refs.test.js` **4/4** (both fields resolve · the 3 dead codes stay dead + the labels that identify them · the 8 audited quests hold their re-derived targets · cat_06 on the profile key), with a **negative control** (re-planting `activateNode:"SF"` + `waypointNode:"CQ"` in memory — both caught, so the guard is not vacuous); `check:walk` **11/11 exit 0**; **367 Playwright green, exit 0 both runs** (`quest-runtime-uqf` + panels/hooks/activation 322/322 · the audit/NPC set 45/45). No §7½ (nothing player-visible — `quest.npc` has zero quest-level client reads per §AUDIT-03b, and no node reference moved). Docs: `quest.md` (both arc sections + the `npc` section's six measured alias pairs), `world.md` §XIX, `maps.md` banner.

The mechanism is confirmed end-to-end: `storyRender` passes `NODE_MAP[S_story.currentCode]`, `storyCheckQuests` → `_uqfActivateAtNode(node)` → `_questsByNode(node.code)` — so at a code-less node `node.code` is `undefined` and the arrival-activation lookup returns nothing. Four of the seven pairs are corroborated independently by `NODE_NPC_KEYS`' own node→key column (LHR→`yael`, TLL→`brynn`, MHQ→`quill`, LLA→`pachelbel`), the other three by `profile.node` or an exact name match. **Shipped:** `WBAPI.NPC_ALIASES` + `npcCanonicalKey()` in `js/wbapi-core.js`; the seven slugs **removed from `npcKeyVocab()`** (297→290) so a quest still carrying one advise-warns instead of validating alongside the real key; `editField` collapses **(quest, npc) only** on write — scoped deliberately, since normalizing `NODE_MAP`'s own inline npc would be the §AUDIT-03h bug; `_buildIndexes` indexes `_questsByNpc` under the canonical key, which is what covers a **hand-authored nested UQF block** (Hazard #3 never touches the API); the create path normalizes before the world-logic check (a create serializes a fresh literal and never reaches `editField`); and every write path now **reports** the collapse (`aliased` on PUT/batch, `aliasedNpc` on create) rather than silently rewriting what the author sent. **Corpus:** the 5 `city_guard_captain` quests — the `quest_ng_01/02/03` Froberger set, `quest_sir_jullean`, `quest_courier_release` — collapsed onto `yael` via `./api.sh batch-npc`, so she is one person again at **22 quests** (was 17 + 5). **A defect found on the way, in the verifier rather than the writer:** `saveAndVerify` compared the reloaded field against **what the caller sent**, so the first live PUT returned `ok:false — field mismatch after reload: npc` on a write that was entirely correct — the inverse of this repo's usual failure. It now verifies against what `editField` actually wrote. **Evidence:** the collapse was sent **as the alias** (`npc=city_guard_captain`) and `yael` landed on disk — the round trip IS the acceptance test; `git diff` on the HTML = **5 lines, nothing else**; live round trips on all three write paths (batch → 5 patched · PUT → `aliased{city_guard_captain→yael}` + `verified ok` · POST create → `aliasedNpc{commander_bruhns→auros}`), probe quest deleted with 0 residue; `./api.sh audit` **errors 0**; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0** with the gate's own `--selftest` catching **6/6** planted defects including both new alias shapes; new `audit03k-npc-aliases.test.js` **6/6**, and **all 6 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK); the npc/audit family **29/29**. **Two sibling tests were stale after the collapse and moved with it, annotated not rewritten:** `audit03g`'s ANCHORED map (the derivation had been right about the character and wrong about the key — the 68 total is unchanged) and `audit03c`'s `jimmy_two-tails` assertion, which said in so many words *"the slug still resolves — this is §AUDIT-03k, not a broken key"* and now asserts the opposite on purpose. **The durable half is gate #14 phase 5**, because nothing else can see this class: the key resolves, the write succeeds, the game is correct, and only an authoring surface nobody looks at is wrong. It fails on any authored `npc:` alias **and** on a NEW inline display name that collides with a live profile and is classified in neither `NPC_ALIASES` nor `NOT_AN_ALIAS`. **The lesson: a key that resolves can still be the wrong key — "does it exist?" and "is it the one this character is filed under?" are different questions, and only the second one notices a person split in two.** Docs: `quest.md` §"The `npc` field" (the 7-row alias table + the role-collision rule + the corrected anchoring row), `prompt.md` §7 (gate #14 gains phase 5), `index.md` (gate row + `wbapi-core` NPC-identity surface). **Side finding filed, not swept: §DX-02l-FU** (a pre-existing red, proved at HEAD without this row's changes). Original row follows. **§AUDIT-03k — one character, two accepted `npc` keys (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03g).** Guard Captain **Yael Scheidemann** is reachable as *both* `yael` (registry 1 — the `BIRKA_NPC` profile at LHR, occupation "city guard captain") *and* `city_guard_captain` (registry 2 — LHR's `NODE_MAP` inline npc string). Both pass `npcKeyOk`, and the corpus already splits: `yael` on `mq_1`/`sq_battling`/`sq_leveling`/`sq_2` + the 8 §AUDIT-03g anchors, `city_guard_captain` on the `quest_ng_01/02/03` Froberger set + the 2 §AUDIT-03g desk-guard scenes. So `_questsByNpc` indexes one person under two headings. The same shape holds wherever a profile and its node's inline string name the same NPC (`brynn`/`innkeeper_brynn`, `archivus_sweelinck`/`archivus_ptolemy_sweelinck`, `auros`/`commander_bruhns` — all four keys live in the 297-key vocabulary today). **Fix shape:** teach `npcKeyVocab()` an alias map (inline-string key → profile key) and normalize on write, so registry 2 resolves *to* registry 1 instead of alongside it; then one `batch-npc` pass to collapse the existing splits. **Risk:** low-medium (touches the shared vocabulary that `advise`/`audit`/the delete-guards all read; needs the §AUDIT-03b tests green after).

More importantly the row's recipe — *"look each `Node #` up and rewrite the code column"* — would have produced a **half-true** table: the `Grid Cell` column is the retired **26×16** projection while live `NODE_COORDS` runs **r 2–73 / c 154–249**, so remapping only the codes would have left an authoritative-looking doc whose coordinates are fiction. And `num` alone is **not** a sufficient key: **num 77 is held by two nodes** (`MJF` and `CDG`) and several terrain keys were renamed (`epic_goblin`→`epic_goblin_cave`, `desert_caravan`→`arabia`), so every match needs terrain/label corroboration. **Shipped instead — generate + quarantine:** new **`scripts/node-index.js`** / **`npm run nodes`** emits **`docs/maps/node-index.md`** from the same `wbapi-core` parse the `:1367` server reads (the `npm run stats` §DX-01g pattern) — all **416** nodes with code · `Node #` · terrain · act · **live cell** · 🛏 · label · inline NPC, plus a **LEGACY CODE MAP** (**83 recovered · 11 unresolved**) that is the Rosetta stone for every older doc, lab report and commit message. It auto-flags the nastiest class: **a legacy code that IS a live key but for a different node** — **`CI`** (Birka's streets historically; the *Chancery Court* live) and **`BK`** (the *Broken Tooth Tavern* = `VBY` historically; *Birka Shore* live). A naive "does this code resolve?" check passes on both. **New `check:walk` gate #12, `check:nodeindex`** — the committed index must be byte-identical to a fresh generation, so it cannot rot the way the legend did. **`maps.md` restructured:** a READ-THIS-FIRST table at the top routing every live question to its live source, the node-code section replaced by a pointer to the generated index, and **all six 26×16-era sections kept verbatim but re-headed ⚠️ HISTORICAL** (TINY MAP · FULL MAP · LEGEND · NODE NETWORK · COORDINATE INDEX · SLEEP NODES — the last of which listed 8 of the live **38** `sleep:true` checkpoints). **`index.md`:** the Town Cross-Reference remapped to live keys, with two corrections beyond the rename — its **Inn** column named `SF`/`IS`, *neither of which is a `sleep:true` node* (the real checkpoints are `SEN` and `NAS`), and Visby's `BK` was the Broken Tooth Tavern = `VBY`, not the live `BK`; `VENDOR_NODES` corrected `BA/MQ/SF/IS/BK` → `LLA/LGW/STN/PDL/BK`; the doc map gained the generated file. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; new `tests/integration/audit03l-node-index.test.js` **5/5** (index ≡ fresh generation · every live node present with live num+terrain · every LEGACY CODE MAP target resolves *and is justified by the legacy `Node #`* · both collisions stay flagged · `maps.md` routes to the index and marks its own tables historical); **two negative controls** — appending a bogus row to the committed index, and inserting a real node into `NODE_MAP` — each made gate #12 exit 1, and the game file was restored byte-identical (`git diff` empty). Docs synced: `prompt.md` §4 (the *never read a code off a doc table* authoring rule), §7 (11→12 gates), §9 cheat sheet. **Follow-up left open: §AUDIT-03m** below. Original row follows. **§AUDIT-03l — the docs answer "what is this node's code?" with codes that do not exist (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03c; this is the most likely *cause* of the 03c bug, not a sibling of it).** `maps.md`'s **LEGEND — Two-Letter Code Reference** is the table an author consults to write an `activateNode`, and **73 of its 76 rows name a code with no `NODE_MAP` entry.** It was written before the world adopted airport codes and was never migrated: `SL`→`BMA` · `CI`→`LHR` · `IN`→`TLL` · `TV`→`MHQ` · `BA`→`LLA` · `CR`→`KRN` · `CY`→`HKG` · `DK`→`LCY` · `MQ`→`LGW` · **`SF`→`STN`** · `MS`→`SEN` · `AL`→`GOT` · **`CQ`→`CDG`** · `AT`→`RAI` · … (full list reproducible in seconds — see the recipe below). **The `Node #` column is still correct and is the recovery key:** every legacy row maps to exactly one live key by `NODE_MAP[k].num`, so the remap is mechanical, not a judgment call. Three traps: (a) **`CI` resolves** — but to the Chancery Court (num 429), not Birka's streets, so a naive "does the code exist?" check passes while the row is still wrong; (b) `RD` (num 78) has **no live node at all**; (c) the table's **⚠️ PLANNED markers are stale** — the `CQ` row still says the Ally Cat arc is *"not yet in HTML"* when 7 quests have shipped at `CDG` for months. **Blast radius beyond that table:** the same legacy codes appear in `maps.md`'s ASCII region grid + adjacency tables, `index.md`'s region table (`CQ`, `SF`) and `VENDOR_NODES` line (says `BA/MQ/SF/IS/BK`; live is `LLA/LGW/STN/PDL/BK`), `world.md`, and `story.md` — plus the `Q-FR-01`-style **design IDs**, which are quest identifiers and should be **left alone**. **Recipe:** parse the table rows, look each `Node #` up in `WBAPI.nodeMap` by `num`, and rewrite the code column (script kept in the §AUDIT-03c session notes; it printed all 73 in one pass). **Consider a `check:walk` gate** asserting every node code cited in a docs table resolves — this pairs naturally with §AUDIT-03j's proposed registry-key gate. **Interim mitigation shipped 2026-07-29:** a hazard banner above the table naming the drift, the recovery key, and the `710bb75` incident it caused. **Risk:** low (docs only) but **high value** — this is a live authoring trap that has already produced one wrong-city bug that survived two months.
- [x] **§AUDIT-03n ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-31 — the ending stops calling three of your six friends strangers, and gate #14 keeps every NPC reference honest. The row named 12 dead entries in 4 registries; live measurement found 29 references across 7 registries plus five live gate sites.** **The row's premise held and its scope did not** — the same understatement as §AUDIT-03j, and in the same direction. A full-depth scan (every object literal at any depth whose keys are all-lowercase, matched against the live 297-key `npcKeyVocab()`) found the three surnames keying **seven** registries, not four: the row's `NPC_EPILOGUES` / `NPC_NG_PLUS_GREETINGS` / `NPC_ACT_THREE_LINES` / `NPC_CROSS_REFS` **plus `SWEELINCK_NAMING_LINES`, `ROUGH_WHISKEY_REACTIONS` and `FROBERGER_TRACES`**, each exactly 3-of-6 dead — **21 authored entries unreachable.** **And the row missed the dimension that mattered most: six references in LIVE CODE.** The epilogue's own `npcOrder`, and **five `_npcFavor('bruhns')` gate sites** guarding whole scenes that could therefore never fire — the §XXXVII Bruhns CO scene at TLS, the S6 Weckmann/Auros joint conversation at HKG (**which ANDs `_npcFavor('crov') >= 1` with `_npcFavor('bruhns') >= 1`, so one live key and one dead one made the condition unsatisfiable**), the Layer-67b S29 theory line, the L45-J Froberger cross-item beat, and **the "🗺 Show Auros the undercity survey" delivery button — the only consumer of a key item the player is actually granted** (`31091`), so that item was a dead end in the inventory. **Identity re-derived independently, not carried from §AUDIT-03j:** the live profiles read `quill` = *Bard Tomas Couperin* (MHQ) · `crov` = *Pit Master Weckmann* (HKG) · `auros` = *Cmdr Seraphine Bruhns* (HKG), and the ledger's writers (`_setNpcFavor` + the VM `favor` opcode) spend exactly the six profile keys — the surnames appear in no registry at all. **Shipped:** 21 registry keys + `npcOrder` + 5 gate sites renamed in one line-anchored pass with per-line assertions (27/27 matched or nothing writes), value-column alignment preserved; an explanatory comment above the seven tables in the §AUDIT-03j house style; **new `scripts/check-npcregs.js` = `check:walk` gate #14**, the npc mirror of #13 — 4 phases (registry keys incl. one nested path `TOWN_CRIER_LINES.npcs`; **explicit** classification of all 36 all-lowercase top-level tables across `NPC_KEYED`/`VOCAB_SOURCE`/`NOT_NPC_KEYED`, an unlisted one FAILS; `_npcFavor('…')`/`npcFavorability['…']` literals; `npcOrder` arrays). **It deliberately does NOT check npc-*valued* fields** — `NODE_MAP`'s inline `npc` is *supposed* to be a display name (§AUDIT-03h's inverse lesson), and quest anchors are already pinned by `audit03h-npc-normalize`. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **14/14 exit 0**; 4-phase `--selftest`; **negative control with the real historical defects** — the gate run against the pre-fix file named **all 29**, exactly matching the independent measurement, and the file was restored byte-identical; new `audit03n-npc-registries.test.js` **6/6**, and **all 6 fail on the pre-fix file**, so neither behaviour test is vacuous; the NPC/audit family **45/45**; full Playwright **732 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing else. **§7½ DONE — player-visible, and it is the point of the row:** at dear-friend favor the victory scroll now renders *"Quill settled the debt. He plays the song every Friday"*, *"Weckmann started a second class on Thursdays"* and *"Auros finished the theory… submitted it jointly with a Scholar King archivist"* in place of the three stranger lines; the Auros survey button renders at HKG (count 1, was 0); 0 page errors on both loads. **The tell, recorded as the lesson: the engine held two `npcOrder` lists that disagreed** — the town-crier one already used the real keys. When two lists of the same thing disagree, the one corroborated nowhere else is the wrong one. Docs: `story.md` §Epilogue (the consequence + the 3-row rename table), `world.md` §Cross-References, `prompt.md` §7 (13→14 gates + the exclusion), `index.md` doc map, and **dated KEY-DRIFT NOTES on the three lab reports whose design-time code blocks still show the surname keys** (annotate, don't rewrite — the §DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m precedent). **Side finding filed, not swept: §AUDIT-03o** — favor **3 is unreachable for 5 of the 6** NPCs, so the `entries[3]` epilogues this row just unlocked are still gated behind a tier nothing grants. Original row follows.
  **§AUDIT-03n (original) — four MORE registries key their entries to NPC names the favor ledger never spends (found 2026-07-31 during §AUDIT-03j; the npc-key twin of that row, and a measured extension of §AUDIT-03k).** §AUDIT-03j fixed `NODE_NPC_KEYS`/`NPC_FAREWELLS`, whose values were the profiles' SURNAMES rather than the profile keys. **The same three dead keys — `couperin` (=`quill`), `weckmann` (=`crov`), `bruhns` (=`auros`) — still key 12 entries across four more registries:** `NPC_EPILOGUES`, `NPC_NG_PLUS_GREETINGS`, `NPC_ACT_THREE_LINES`, `NPC_CROSS_REFS` (measured: 3 of 6 keys dead in each; `NPC_VOID_PRESSURE_LINES`/`NPC_NG_MEMORY_LINES`/`NPC_ROMANCE_PREAMBLES` are clean). **The sharpest consequence is in the ending:** the epilogue builder walks `npcOrder = ['yael','brynn','couperin','pachelbel','weckmann','bruhns']` and reads `S_story.npcFavorability[key]`, which is **always 0** for three of them — so the endgame renders the *stranger* variant for Quill, Weckmann and Bruhns no matter how close you got, while the fav≥2/fav≥3 lines authored for them are unreachable. (Note a sibling `npcOrder` at the void-pressure site already uses the real keys — the two lists disagree, which is the tell.) **Fix shape:** rename the keys to the profile keys in all four registries + the epilogue `npcOrder`, then extend `check:noderegs` with a 5th phase (or a sibling `check:npcregs`) asserting every npc-keyed registry key resolves in the 4-registry `npcKeyVocab()` — the npc mirror of gate #13, which would have caught all 12. **Risk:** low (renames + a gate); the lines already exist and are authored. **Verify:** an epilogue test at fav 3 for `quill`/`crov`/`auros` must render the dear-friend variant, not `entries[0]`.
- [x] **§AUDIT-03p ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 `ea19a88` — the Void's first crack now reaches 13 places instead of 5, and the blind spot inside the blind spot is closed: gate #13 reads function-local registries. The row's premise held exactly; what it could not know is that four of the sixteen keys were never nodes at ANY point in the file's history — they were born dead.** **Measured before touching anything** (Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `cd033cb`, server **stopped** — Hazard #1 — one claude process): the row's 11-of-16 count re-derived live against the 416-key `NODE_MAP`, and then each dead key traced through `docs/maps/node-index.md`'s LEGACY CODE MAP **plus** `git show cc562f5` (the Layer 59 commit that introduced the table) and the pre-remap `NODE_MAP` at that commit. **The finding worth more than the remap:** `SH`, `PH`, `MH` and `WM` matched **no `NODE_MAP` key at the commit that authored them** and appear nowhere in `maps.md`'s 92-row historical legend either — the table was written from *memory*, not from the world, so a quarter of it never fired even on the day it shipped. `WM` was later minted as a real node (Weimar Archive) that happens to fit its own cluster; `SH`/`PH`/`MH` never were and are **dropped, not guessed at**. The other eight remapped mechanically and each was **corroborated by label, not just by `Node #`** (`SL`→`BMA` Birka Slums · `CR`→`KRN` Birka Crypt · `BA`→`LLA` Rough Bar · `DK`→`LCY` *Harbor Docks — Tilbury* · `MQ`→`LGW` *Tilbury Market Quarter* · `MT`→`GVA` Mountain Pass · `HM`→`DUS` Frequency Row · `DF`→`ZRH` Unbanked Quarter). **One judgment call, stated rather than buried:** `ZRH` is filed under `wild`, not the `weimar` its legend row implied — it is a `defi_land` node whose two district siblings (`MUC`, `DUS`) already read `wild`, and *"The scholars' candles are burning down fast today"* at a quarter with no scholars would be new wrong content, not a repair. **Coverage 5 → 13 nodes, all live.** **The gate:** `check:noderegs` phase 5 (§AUDIT-03p) applies the SAME explicit classification — `LOCAL_NODE_KEYED` / `NOT_NODE_KEYED`, an unlisted codeish local literal **fails** — to indented `const/let/var NAME = {` declarations. Phases 1–2 only ever matched `^const`, which is the entire reason a 16-key table could rot for months inside a function while the gate reported green; the file has **3** such node tables (`CLUSTER`, `_vaSites`, `birkaNpcs` — the latter two already clean) and 2 compass ones (`ARROWS`/`DELTAS`, classified with a reason). **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0**; `check:noderegs --selftest` **6/6**, the two new plants being a dead key in a classified local table *and* an unclassified one; **negative control** — the gate run against the HEAD `CLUSTER` fails **exit 1** naming exactly the eleven dead keys, restored green with an empty diff; new `audit03p-void-flavor-cluster.test.js` **10/10** (source: every key live, every value a real `CRACK` bucket, the three phantoms absent; render: the crack line reaches `LHR`+`BMA`+`LCY`+`WM`+`GVA` at pressure 3, the ≥6 named-place line reaches `LCY`; two controls — pressure 2 renders nothing, and `CDG`, in no cluster, stays silent at pressure 6), and a **positive control** proving the tests are not vacuous: run against HEAD, **6 of the 10 fail** and the 4 that pass are exactly the already-live `LHR`/`WM` cases. Full Playwright **787 passed / 4 failed** — the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, plus the known `multiplayer-presence.test.js:171` flake, which is **7/7 green in isolation** and is not a fifth red. Docs synced: `story.md` §Pressure Cascade gains the live cluster→node table with the never-existed-keys note, `index.md` + `prompt.md` §7 record phase 5, and `npm run anchors:fix` refreshed the 12 hints my +7 lines drifted. **Original row follows.** **§AUDIT-03p — a LIVE engine table is keyed to dead 26×16 node codes, and gate #13 cannot see it (found 2026-08-04 during §AUDIT-03m; the §AUDIT-03j class, one scope level down).** `_voidFlavorLine(nodeCode)` — called from the node renderer at `roll2hit-v3.html:31747` — switches on a **function-local** `const CLUSTER` map (`roll2hit-v3.html:26776`) of 16 node codes. **Measured against the live 416-key `NODE_MAP`: 11 of the 16 are dead** — `SL`, `CR`, `BA`, `SH`, `PH`, `DK`, `MQ`, `DF`, `MT`, `MH`, `HM`. Only `LHR`, `STN`, `NUE`, `WM`, `MUC` resolve. The function returns `''` on a miss, so at void pressure ≥ 3 the Birka/Tilbury/Weimar/wild **flavor line silently never renders** at eleven of the sixteen places it was authored for — dead player-facing content, failing the way §AUDIT-03j's 42 references failed. **Why gate #13 (`check:noderegs`) is green anyway, and this is the interesting part:** it classifies and checks **top-level** registries; `CLUSTER` is declared *inside a function body*, so it is invisible to the scan. **`SL`/`CR`/`BA`/`DK`/`MQ`/`DF`/`MT`/`HM` remap mechanically** through `docs/maps/node-index.md`'s LEGACY CODE MAP (→ `BMA`/`KRN`/`LLA`/`LCY`/`LGW`/`ZRH`/`GVA`/`DUS`); **`SH`, `PH` and `MH` are in no map at all** and need identification from the cluster they were filed under, not a guess. **Fix shape:** remap the resolvable eight, classify the three unknowns explicitly, then extend gate #13 with a **function-local** node-keyed-object phase — the same widening §AUDIT-03n was to #13's registry list. **Do not batch this with the doc sweep**: it edits the game file (Hazard #1 — stop the server, commit early) and it is player-visible, so it needs §7½. **Risk:** low-medium. **Verify:** the eight remapped codes resolve; a void-pressure render at one repaired node shows the flavor line where it previously showed nothing; `check:walk` green with the new phase catching the pre-fix table as a negative control.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03o — favor level 3 is unreachable for 5 of the 6 canonical NPCs, so the `entries[3]` tier is authored-but-dead content (found 2026-07-31 during §AUDIT-03n; needs a design call).** §AUDIT-03n unlocked the fav≥2 *and* fav≥3 lines for Quill/Weckmann/Bruhns by fixing their keys — but **measurement of every writer to the ledger shows level 3 is only ever granted to two characters.** The writers are exactly: `_setNpcFavor(key, level)` (whose literal call sites are `_setNpcFavor('yael', 3)`, `('crov',1)`, `('solvak',1)`, `('yva',1)`), the VM's `favor` opcode (corpus-wide the `set:` values are **only 1 and 2** — 10 × `set:1`, 4 × `set:2`), the dear-friend auto-upgrade (`npcFavorability[key] = 2`, both sites), and one hardcoded `npcFavorability['kenickie'] = 3`. **So `brynn`/`quill`/`pachelbel`/`crov`/`auros` cap at 2**, and everything gated on 3 is dead: their `NPC_EPILOGUES[3]` lines (the richest ending text in the file), `FROBERGER_TRACES.crov`'s `minFav:3`, and the `weckmann_class` arc-epilogue condition `_npcFavor('crov') >= 3`. **This is a design call, not an audit fix** — three honest readings: (a) tier 3 is *meant* to be a rare apex only Yael reaches, in which case the other five NPCs' `[3]` entries should be demoted to `[2]` or deleted rather than left as dead authoring; (b) each NPC deserves a third personal act that grants 3 (content work, and the natural companion to §NPC-01-D / §RIPPLE-01-C's "the favor track is too thin to earn its ladder"); or (c) the dear-friend auto-upgrade should promote to 3 when a *second* personal bit lands. **Do not default it** — (a) deletes authored content and (b) is an arc each. **Coordinate with §RIPPLE-01-C and §NPC-01-D**, which are already scoped to widen this same track. **Risk:** low to measure, medium to author. **Verify:** whatever the choice, a test asserting the top tier each NPC can actually reach matches the top tier its tables author.
- [x] **§AUDIT-03q ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 `28c6f8a` — the class no gate could see is now a gate, and the reading pass that opened this row had itself missed one.** The row's premise held exactly: `check:legacycodes` is driven by the LEGACY CODE MAP, which records codes that *were* nodes and were renamed, so it has nothing to say about a code that was never a node at all — the §AUDIT-03p born-dead class. **Measured first, as the row asked.** Of the eleven tokens filed (`SH` `PH` `MH` `SK` `SB` `AO` `LT` `HR` `KS` `DR` `TS`), **exactly one is a live trap**: `SH` in `world.md`. `PH`/`MH` appear only in `story.md`'s own §AUDIT-03p note, backticked on an explanatory line; the other eight appear in **no live doc at all** — they are design-era arc codes from `lab-report-saul-paul-vignette-spec.md` and `lab-report-naval-campaign-layer.md`, reaching live prose only through `quest.md:527`, which already labels them *"design-era codes … shipped under real-world names"*. `quest.md` is HISTORY: annotate, never rewrite. So the row's guess — *"several are probably arc identifiers, not node codes"* — measured true for 8 of 11, and **no sweep was warranted**. **The finding.** §AUDIT-03m-FU caught *"Tell Pachelbel at SH"* by reading and fixed it; on the same page, **`world.md:600` still read *"Player asks Pachelbel at `LLA` or SH"***. Neither the line test nor a place cue can see that token, because the preposition belongs to the code **next to** it — a hand-read pass and a line-scoped detector have the same blind spot. The new signal closes it: **a line that has already put a live node code in backticks is talking about places, so a codeish token beside it is one too.** Fixed against the ENGINE, not the legacy map (the §AUDIT-03m-FU discipline): the lute handover is keyed to `key === 'pachelbel'` on the NPC card, and `birkaNpcs` puts Pachelbel at `LLA` — so the beat happens wherever he stands, and `SH` is annotated as never-a-node rather than guessed onto a tidy live code. **Shipped:** `scripts/legacy-codes.js` **phase 2** — a two-letter token resolving in **neither** registry, in a node context, in a SWEEP doc; `eachProseLine` + `localCue` extracted so both phases share one instrument (§DX-01d's *one scanner for gate and writer*). Classification is EXPLICIT for the third time in this family and for the third time for the same reason — a percentage heuristic is blind to a token that is *always* jargon: **19 tokens fire across the 10 swept docs and `NG` fires 47 times without ever being a node**, so each sits in `NOT_A_NODE_CODE` with a reason or in `BORN_DEAD`. The phase catches a second shape by the same test: a **real** historical code the `maps.md` legend never listed, so `npm run nodes` never put it in the map (`FR` = Fishmonger's Row → `AMS`). **The limit is stated, not discovered:** it hunts **exactly two letters** — the shape of the retired code space and of all eleven filed tokens. Widening to three admits a technical doc's whole acronym vocabulary (`NPC` 151× · `DOM` · `ATK` · `HTML`) for no measured gain, and every extra token is human classification work; **a three-letter code written from memory is NOT caught**, and a test asserts that rather than letting the silence read as coverage. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** (bare run, not piped) · five controls planted one at a time in a SWEEP doc — a bare born-dead code exit 1, an unclassified `ZQ` exit 1 *("classify it in BORN_DEAD or NOT_A_NODE_CODE")*, and `Q-TL-01` / `NG+` / *"Act IV … 20 HP"* beside a live node code each exit 0 · `audit03m-legacy-codes.test.js` **15/15** (+5), with a **positive control**: keep the new tool, restore HEAD's `world.md`, and **3 tests fail** — gate #16's own assertion, the phase-2 classification test and the engine-corroboration test — while the gate exits 1 · full Playwright **796 passed / 4 failed** (the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four; the run printed 795/5 because `multiplayer-presence.test.js:171` did not retry green, and that file passes **7/7** on its own). Docs synced: `prompt.md` §7, `index.md` gate row, this row. **Coordinates with §AUDIT-03p**, which owns the engine-side half of the same class.
- [x] **§AUDIT-03m ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 `b399318` — `world.md` and `story.md` stop speaking 26×16, and the sweep is a measured tool with a gate rather than a one-off pass. The row's premise held; its warning about a blind replace was the whole design problem, and the sharpest finding was that the CODES were the symptom, not the disease.** **Measured first, never carried from the row:** a naive word-boundary scan over every `.md` returns **4,663 hits — and most are noise.** `DC` is a difficulty class **435** times, `EB` abbreviates *Epic Battleground* **163** times, and `IS`/`AT`/`CO`/`ST` sit inside ordinary English. So `scripts/legacy-codes.js` (`npm run legacy`) reads the LEGACY CODE MAP out of the **generated** `docs/maps/node-index.md` (§AUDIT-03l's gate #12 keeps it live — the map is never re-typed) and counts a token only in a **node context**, with the ambiguous codes classified **explicitly** — the §AUDIT-03j/n house style, for the same reason: a percentage heuristic is blind to a code that is *wholly* ambiguous. That took 4,663 → **2,517 real references, 230 of them in live docs.** **Every doc is classified SWEEP / PENDING / HISTORY, and an unclassified one FAILS** — four turned up on the first run and were classified by reading them. **HISTORY is annotated, never rewritten** (§DX-02c / §AUDIT-03n precedent): lab reports, `plan-archive.md`, BACKLOG and `maps.md`'s quarantined tables hold **2,287** references and are left alone — the tool **refuses** `--annotate` on one, and a test proves it. **Shipped:** `world.md` **77 annotations / 81 lines** and `story.md` **193 / 160**, in two forms because one form cannot serve both cases — sparse lines get inline `` `LHR` (historical `CI`) ``, a run of 3+ codes gets live codes plus **one** trailing key (`*(historical: `CI`=`LHR` · …)*`), since ten interleaved parentheticals is a wall, not an annotation. **The finding that mattered more than the codes:** `world.md`'s node sections describe a **retired navigation model**, and annotating them alone would have laundered a false claim into live-looking codes. Measured at HEAD: **0 of the 416 live nodes carries an `N`/`S`/`E`/`W` field** (§CELL-01 stripped them), and `J1`/`J6`/`J7` are absent — so *"Node connections from CI (**verified against `NODE_MAP`**): N→SL · E→IN"* was true when written and is not a claim anyone can re-check. It now carries a **historical-model banner** naming all three retirements (edge graph · junction stubs · the `R03,C16` 26×16 projection, live coords being r 2–73 / c 154–249) and pointing at `./api.sh location <CODE>` as the authority; the junction-vignette paragraph was rewritten to say the stubs are **deleted** and that §AUDIT-03j rehomed seven of them. **Three tool bugs the review caught before they shipped, each a real one:** it wrote **inside fenced code blocks** (12 lines of a `world.md` milepoint block), it **nested backticks** on already-backticked codes (```` ``J2` (historical …)` ````, 11 lines), and it missed a **single-code table cell** (`| Yael | CI | … |`). All three are fixed in the tool and pinned by tests — the output was re-generated from a clean `git checkout`, never hand-patched around. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** (new gate #16 `check:legacycodes`); **negative control on the real doc** — a bare *"north of SL"* appended to `world.md` turned the gate **red (exit 1)**, restored with `shasum -c` **OK** and an empty `git diff`; new `audit03m-legacy-codes.test.js` **7/7**, of which **two are controls**: a **positive** control feeding six shapes that must still be caught (a heading run · a direction sentence · `Node CI` · an arrow list · a table cell · a bare code on an explanatory line) and a **negative** control feeding nine that must not be (`DC 10` · *bait search DC reduced* · *EB engagement audit* · `Q-TL-01` · an already-annotated code · a dense-form legend · a backticked code on an explanatory line · a table header · ordinary English) — because **a detector narrowed into uselessness and a deleted detector look identical in a green run** (§DX-02l-FU's lesson, applied deliberately). Full Playwright **778 passed / 4 failed / 0 flaky** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing else (771 + these 7). **No HTML touched, no §7½** — docs + tooling only; the WBAPI server stayed stopped (no writes needed). **Two limits, asserted rather than left to be discovered:** an ambiguous code is flagged only on a node-context line (precision over recall — the price of not mangling English), and **a fenced block is code**, so a dead code inside a *runnable example* escapes the gate. One did: `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`'s `./api.sh get node CY` — a copy-pasteable command against a node that has not existed for months — **fixed by hand to `HKG`**, and the class is recorded here rather than papered over. **Open tail: the 7 PENDING live docs (230 references)**, reported by name and count on every gate run — `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md` 95 · `story-arc-epic-battlegrounds` 41 · `story-arc-investigation` 27 · `story-arc-npc-dialogues` 22 · `story-arc-coastal` 13 · `story-flowchart` 12 · `story-arc-ngplus` 8. They are PENDING, not SWEEP, because **a gate that fails on work nobody has done yet is a red light you learn to ignore**; moving a file to SWEEP is the next slice and needs no design call. **Side finding filed, not swept: §AUDIT-03p** — a *live engine* table with the same rot, which gate #13 cannot see. Original row follows. **§AUDIT-03m — the prose still speaks 26×16 (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03l).** §AUDIT-03l fixed the *tables an author looks a code up in*; the legacy codes still appear in **running prose and cross-references** across `world.md`, `story.md`, `docs/story/*`, `docs/notes/*` and the lab reports (`"N of CI"`, `"east of DS"`, `Q-FR-01`-style design IDs, etc.). **Deliberately not swept here:** a blind token replace over prose is how you turn `AT`/`IS`/`SE`/`CA` into nonsense inside English sentences, and the **design IDs (`Q-TL-01`, `Q-FR-02`, §XIX/§XX arc names) are identifiers and must NOT be remapped.** Do it per-document, reading each hit, with `docs/maps/node-index.md`'s LEGACY CODE MAP as the key — and prefer *annotating* (`CI` → `` `LHR` (historical `CI`) ``) over silent replacement so the older records stay readable. **Risk:** low; **value:** medium (the authoring trap itself is closed by 03l's gate — this is legibility, not correctness).
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03r — `num:77` is held by two nodes (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02a).** `` `MJF:{ num:77@8784` `` (The Shale Drop, act 3) and `` `CDG:{ num:77@8798` `` (The Cat Quarter, act 1) both carry `num:77`. The Ally Cat lab report's own 2026-05-25 risk register said *"verify no existing node uses 77"* — it was not verified, and the collision has stood since. **Measured impact today: cosmetic.** `node.num` has exactly three read sites, all display (`s-node-num` HUD pad, the trail list, the travel destination line), so nothing looks a node up *by* `num` and nothing breaks; but it is a duplicate value in a field whose name and zero-padded rendering both read like a primary key, and the next reader who treats it as one gets the wrong node silently (Hazard #2 class). `maps.md:112` already records the collision in prose — this row is to **decide and enforce**: either renumber one node (a display-only change, but `num` is authored in ~416 places and the sequence has meaning to nobody but the HUD), or declare `num` explicitly non-unique and add a one-line comment saying so. **A gate is cheap either way** — `check:dupkeys` already walks `NODE_MAP` entries; a duplicate-`num` assertion is a few lines, and it is the only thing that stops the third one. **Needs a design call** (renumber vs. declare). **Risk:** low. **Verify:** whichever choice, a test that fails on a second node sharing a `num`.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03s — dead node codes in PLAYER-FACING engine strings, which no gate can see (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02a).** `check:legacycodes` (gate #16) scans `*.md` only; `check:noderegs` phase 6 (gate #13) scans **comparison literals and battle codes** only. Neither can see a retired code sitting inside a quoted string the player *reads*. **Measured: 7 occurrences of the `the XX node` shape across the HTML, 6 of them player-visible.** Five belong to the Ally Cat arc and all say *"the DF node"* for what is now `ZRH`: `sandy_cat`'s `NPC_DIALOGUES` line, `quest_cat_void`'s `desc` + `disposition` + `failText`, and the `CQ_BOSS` victory `storyMsg`. Two more are elsewhere: a quest `desc` saying *"Win the CY node battle"* (`CY`→`HKG`) and a tunnel `storyMsg` naming `DF`. One further hit (`the EB node`) is a source comment, not player text. **The fix is two-sided:** (a) rewrite the six strings to name the place, not the code — *"the strays near the Unbanked Quarter"* reads better than any code and cannot rot again, which is the §AUDIT-03m-FU discipline (fix against the world, don't launder a code into a tidier code); (b) add a **phase 7** to `check:noderegs` that scans *string literals* in the HTML for a legacy code in a node context, with the family's explicit-classification style so an intentional mention must be listed. Note the scope limit before starting: `the XX node` is one phrasing — the sweep should first measure how many *other* phrasings carry a dead code (`at XX`, `north of XX`, a bare parenthesised code) before the gate's shape is fixed, exactly as §AUDIT-03m-FU had to widen from a LINE test to four hand-classified cues. **Risk:** low (prose + one scanner phase). **Verify:** the six strings changed and eyeballed live at `CDG`; a planted control string exits 1.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03t — 36 `NODE_MAP` entries carry `act:NaN`, and two readers render `undefined` to the player (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02c).** Traced from the battleground report's `NodeEntry` typedef claim *"`act` — story act (1–8)"*. **Measured: 36 of 416 nodes (8.7%) have `act:NaN`** — `num` 181–443, the Avignon/Genoese, Nottingham, Bethune/Camelot and Roncevaux blocks, incl. `` `CI: { num:429@9229` ``. **Three readers, three different failures, none of which throws:** `` `S_story.actNumber = node.act || 1@34568` `` silently coerces to **Act 1** (NaN is falsy) so every one of these nodes is act-gated as if it were Birka; `` `ACT_NAMES[node.act]@34597` `` indexes a 9-element array (`` `const ACT_NAMES@9417` ``) with NaN and yields `undefined`, so the act badge renders **"— undefined —"** and `#s-node-act` renders the bare word **"undefined"**; and the map panel's info line renders **"Act NaN"**. This is the Hazard-#2 class in its data form — *a read of a real-but-wrong value never throws*. **The fix is two-sided:** (a) assign each of the 36 nodes a real act — most are late-arc historical content whose act is inferable from its neighbours, but a handful (the Chancery/Camelot restoration set) may be deliberately act-less and want an explicit sentinel instead, which is the one design call in the row; (b) add a gate — `check:invariants` is the natural home — failing on any `NODE_MAP` entry whose `act` is not an integer in `ACT_NAMES`' index range, since **no existing gate can see this**: `check:noderegs` checks that node *references* resolve, never that a node's own scalar fields are well-formed. **Risk:** low for the gate, low-medium for (a) — `actNumber` feeds act-gated quest/CSS behaviour, so changing 36 nodes off their silent Act 1 could surface content that was never reachable. **Verify:** the gate exits 1 on a planted `act:NaN`; the badge eyeballed live at `CI` and `RON` per §7½.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03u — the world has 416 nodes; two player-facing strings still tell the player it has forty-two (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02d).** Both are stale by 10×, and both are *onboarding* text — the two places the game states its own scale to a new player. (a) The Map Shop hands over the Real Map: *"he reaches under and sets the Real Map beside it — **all forty-two nodes** — and pushes it toward you"* (`` `STN:{ num:9@8636` ``). (b) Yael's Level-1 tutorial monologue, the game's first paragraph of instruction, opens *"Check your MAP. The known world has **forty-two nodes** and you can get lost in it, badly, fast"* (`` `yael: { meta:@10397` ``). **Forty-two was the report-era battleground count in the retired 26×16 space** — §DOC-02c's Appendix A lists exactly 42 codes — so both sentences are fossils of a map that was replaced by §WALK/§NAV-01's 90×360 geo grid. `npm run stats` says **416** nodes / **111** terrains. **Mechanical, with one judgement call worth making deliberately:** do **not** swap in `416`, which will rot the same way the moment a node is added — rewrite both to describe scale without a literal (*"the known world is larger than it looks"*, *"every node the cartographers have"*). **Related class, not yet measured:** a hardcoded world-scale number in *any* player-facing string is the same defect, and nothing greps for it; if a survey is cheap, count the others in the same pass. **Risk:** none (prose only). **Verify:** `grep -c "forty-two nodes"` → 0; both strings eyeballed live at `STN` and `LHR` per §7½.
- [ ] **§DX-02p — a level earned by PASSING a skill check delivers no level-up (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02e).** `_checkLevelUp()`@25671 reads like a predicate and is a **mutator**: it advances `S_story.level` immediately and pushes the new level onto `_levelUpQueue`. Every *benefit* of that level — the hit-die roll for max HP, the ASI at 4/6/8/12/14/16/19, the Fighter feature, the level-2/9/17 surge and indomitable charges — is delivered by `_showLevelUpModal`@25615 and its continue handler, and **the queue is the only channel between them**. Every drain in the file: `@7015` (skill-check **FAIL**, §XP-01 effort XP) · `@25187` `_storyEnemyFlees` · `@25300` battle victory · `@30080` `_grantExplorationXp` · `@38442` victory-overlay return · `@38676` modal chaining. **The skill-check PASS path is not among them** — `_resolveQuestUQF`'s pass branch@6989 runs `execBits`, credits the warrant, calls `storyRender`, and never checks the queue. **It is worse than deferral:** `_grantExplorationXp` opens with `_levelUpQueue = []`@30078 and re-runs `_checkLevelUp()`, which returns immediately because the level was already advanced — so **any step onto new ground discards the pending entry**. The level is kept; its rewards are gone. **The asymmetry is the tell:** the failing branch, written later by §XP-01, does it correctly three lines away — *failing* a Ceremonia Roll can open the modal, *passing* one cannot. **Blast radius: all 2,453 `skill_check` quests**, not one arc. **The 2026-05-26 §DESIGN-03 spec called for exactly this** (*"If `_levelUpQueue.length > 0`: show level-up modal immediately"*) and it was dropped in the §ARCH-01 migration — which is the argument for the spec→shipped delta table as a permanent instrument. **Needs no design call.** **Fix:** copy the fail branch's three lines into the pass branch. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** a Playwright pin on the *property* — cross an XP threshold on a passing skill check, assert the modal opened — never on the incident (*pin the property, never the incident*, §VM-01-G4c).
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03w — the courier guard quotes a 20-gold bribe the engine cannot accept (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02e).** `quest_courier_release` ships `onFail:[]` — the empty array — and a `failText` reading *"'Twenty gold or a writ from the Watch Commander.' He turns the log face-down."*`@21338`. §8 of the 2026-05-26 spec designed the transaction in detail (a `'bribe_available'` quest status, a *"Pay 20gp Bribe"* secondary button, an `onFail` callback offering it when `gold >= 20`); `git log -S "bribe_available"` returns **0 commits ever**. So the game **states a price and cannot be paid it**. **This is §AUDIT-03v's class exactly** — prose that names a transaction the engine never honours — and finding a second instance in two consecutive increments makes it a **pattern worth a detector rather than two hand-filed rows**: a player-facing string that names a specific gold amount as an offer, with no `cost`/`reward` bit or `sleepCost` exemption behind it. **Small design call, two options:** (a) implement it — the grammar has supported this since §VM-01-G4a: a `choice` on the fail branch with a `{kind:'cost', gold:20, refuse:'…'}` option, where `refuse` covers the can't-afford case under the shipped refuse-at-click contract; or (b) rewrite `failText` to refuse without naming a number. **Do not do neither.** **Risk:** low either way. **Verify:** (a) `./api.sh advise quest_courier_release` + a round-trip pin that 20gp leaves the purse exactly once; (b) grep the string is gone. **Consider closing §AUDIT-03v in the same increment** — same class, same decision shape. **⟶ FOURTH AND FIFTH INSTANCES, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02h), and they WIDEN THE DETECTOR PAST GOLD.** Both in §CROWN-01 Amendment A (`c0c952b`). (iv) **The Leviathan iodine burn CHARGES for a benefit that has no reader.** `_nodeHookHcaLeviathan@33107` renders a button labelled *"⚡🧂 Burn Charged Iodine Salt at the water's edge (+5 ATK this fight)"* (or *"+3"* for plain); clicking it **splices the item out of the inventory** and sets `` `S_story.iodineBuffActive = true; S_story.iodineBuffBonus = _hasChL ? 5 : 3;@33131` ``. In 38,707 lines `iodineBuffActive` has **exactly one reader** — `` `const iodineBonus = st.iodineBuffActive@22249` ``, inside `_rollSkill`. **No combat code reads it.** So the player pays a crafted item for a stated combat bonus that does not exist — and the buff **leaks**, since nothing at HCA consumes it, so it is silently spent by whatever unrelated skill check comes next. This is worse than §AUDIT-03v/w: those *promise* and don't deliver; this one *takes payment first*. (v) **`Marsh Seaweed`'s `desc`** — *"Burns to trace iodine salt at the inn hearth"* — names a craft no quest or handler performs (see §DX-02n (d)). **The consequence for the detector:** the class is not *"a player-facing string naming a gold price"*, it is ***a player-facing string naming any specific mechanical effect — gold, a to-hit bonus, a craft — with no bit, handler or reader behind it.*** Five instances in five increments; the gold-only formulation would have caught two of them. Note (iv) is currently masked by §AUDIT-03x (HCA is unarrivable) and goes live the moment that row is resolved — **do not let the mask defer it**.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03v — Brynn promises a free room twice and the engine charges 5gp both times (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02d).** Three sites, all live. (a) `TLL`'s node text has her say it on arrival: *"'Free,' she says. 'The room's free too, if you need it.'"* (`` `TLL:{ num:2@8431` ``). (b) `quest_brynn_ledger.onComplete` narrates the reward the 2026-05-22 spec called *"free lodging for 3 nights"*: *"🛏 Brynn offers 3 free nights"* (`` `Brynn offers 3 free nights@21234` ``). (c) `storySleep` charges `node.sleepCost` — **`sleepCost:5` at TLL** — with a single exemption hardcoded to a **different** node and a **different** flag: `` `node.code === 'INN' && S_story.freeBookingUnlocked@36208` ``, which is `quest_inn_06`'s reward and keys on `innmotherKindness >= 5`. **So the game states a price of zero in prose, restates zero as a quest reward, and bills 5gp on both.** This is the §SPARK-01-FU class inverted — not a double-pay, a double-*promise* with no payer — and no gate can see it, because nothing cross-checks a narrative string against the mechanic it describes. **Small design call, two clean options:** (i) **grant it** — add `S_story.brynnFreeNights` decremented in `storySleep`, exactly the shape `freeBookingUnlocked` already models, which also makes (a) true; or (ii) **rewrite both strings** so neither promises a free bed. **Do not do neither.** Prefer (i): the arc's whole point is that the relationship pays, and 5gp is the cheapest possible way to prove it. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** for (i), a Playwright pin that gold is unchanged across a TLL sleep with nights remaining and debited by 5 when they run out; for (ii), grep both strings. **⟶ SIXTH CLUSTER INSTANCE, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02j), and it is the widest surface yet — the sleep overlay promises FULL HP RECOVERY on every inn in the game.** The Long Rest button's own hint reads `` `full HP recovery@35759` `` on a first stay and `` `heal to full & advance a day@35758` `` on a return stay. **`storySleep` does not heal to full.** It rolls `` `const _rollCount = _isFirstSleep ? 2 : 1@36255` `` d10s plus the CON modifier, floors the result at half max HP (`` `_healTotal = Math.max(_healTotal@36263` ``) and caps it at `hpMax` — so a wounded player routinely pays the `sleepCost` and stands up short of full. Unlike v/w/y(b) this is **not** authored content on one node: it is a **fixed engine string on all 38 `sleep:true` nodes**, which makes it the cluster's highest-traffic member and the argument that the wanted detector belongs in the engine-string universe (§AUDIT-03s's premise), not in quest data. **Two options, same shape as the rest:** heal to full and make the string true, or restate the hint as *"a night's rest — roll to recover"*. **Prefer the second** — the dice are the design (§DOC-02j finding F3 shows the Hit-Die model migrated here deliberately from the short rest), so it is the sentence that is wrong. **⟶ SEVENTH INSTANCE, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02k), and the first that states the same unbacked mechanic TWICE.** `aurosBlueprintKnown` occurs **4 times** in 38,712 lines: the `_S_DEFAULTS()` declaration, one writer on `quest_d0206_a4`'s pass, and **two readers that do nothing but render the promise back at the player** — Act IV's passText (*"In the CO boss fight, Auros's left pauldron has -4 AC. Her armor was never finished there."*) and a persistent Workshop panel, `` `_wkHint.textContent = '📜 Blueprint known@31964` ``, which repeats *"Auros's left pauldron is AC -4 in the Convergence fight"* for the whole run. **No combat code reads the field**; Auros's AC is never reduced at `TLS` or anywhere else. What makes this the sharpest framing yet for the detector: the second surface is **gated on `!S_story.defeatedBattles['TLS']`**, so it is deliberately built to persist *until the fight it names* — a reminder of a mechanic that has never existed, shown right up to the moment the player would notice. **Same two options as the rest** (wire the −4 into the TLS encounter, or restate both strings as lore about unfinished armour); **prefer wiring it**, since the whole §D02-06 chain exists to earn it. Seven instances in seven increments now, across quest text, node text, engine strings and an item `desc` — the class is a **surface** problem, not a data problem.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03x — 172 of 416 nodes can never be arrived at, and 774 quests are authored to activate on them (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02f; 🟡 DESIGN CALL).** §WALK-1.5 merged 1°-collided cities into **locales**: `` `const CELL_GRID@9852` `` maps each `"r,c"` to an **array** of node codes, and its own comment names the contract — *"primaryOf = list[0] is the node you arrive at, the rest are intra-cell sub-locations."* Measured at HEAD: **416 nodes occupy 244 distinct cells, so 172 are non-primary** (worst cell `32,203` holds **17**: `SEA ATH LC1 LC2 LC3 LC4 LSO DS0 DS1 DSJ DSF DA0 DA1 DA2 DA3 HER IST`). **`S_story.currentCode` is assigned at exactly two sites in the whole file** — `` `S_story.currentCode = destCode@28368` ``, where `destCode = res.destCodes[0]` (the primary), and the respawn line `` `checkpointNode || 'LHR'@26009` `` — so **a non-primary code can never become `currentCode`**, and its `text`/`npc`/`battle`/`loot`/`sleep` never render. `cellCodes()` (the full list) is read **only** by the MUD/`MOVER:CORE` kernel; every browser call site uses `` `cellCode@9861` ``, the primary. **Blast radius via quests:** `` `_uqfActivateAtNode(node)@30132` `` keys on `node.code`, and **1,260 quests carry an `activateNode` on a non-primary node** — 486 are already held by the §AUDIT-03e guard, leaving **774 across 135 nodes** that would activate on arrival and cannot. Worst: **`BK` 89 quests** (sharing a cell with the *starting node* `LHR`), **`WM` 312** (behind `ERF`), `ROM` 58 (behind `FCO`), `LDN` 34, `HCA` (behind `WG0`). Content is stranded too: of the 172, **20 carry an `npc`, 11 `sleep:true`, 10 `loot`, 9 a `battle`**. **The tell that this is a real gap and not a design intent:** the waypoint system already compensates — `storyWaypoint`/`_travelTick` accept arrival **by cell** *"(co-located locale nodes share a cell, so currentCode equality alone can miss a genuine arrival)"* `` `@38092` `` — so the player is told *"📍 You have reached the waypoint: \<label\>"* for a node the engine then declines to render; and the MUD path is honest about the collision (`` `locales share this ground@10207` ``) while the browser path is silent. **Design call, three options:** (1) **aggregate** — `_uqfActivateAtNode` and the node panel iterate `cellCodes(cell)` rather than the primary alone, so a locale's quests and content surface on arrival at the cell; (2) **a locale surface** — the primary renders an explicit "also here: …" list that switches `currentCode` among its siblings (no movement, so invariant #1/#3 are untouched); (3) **DECIDED-STAY** — sub-locations are deliberately flavour-only, in which case the 774 `activateNode` values are the defect and should be repointed to their primaries. **Do not build before the call.** **Overlaps §AUDIT-03d** — that row's staging decision releases §AUDIT-03e's held quests, and it should be made knowing 774 of them are unreachable for a *second, independent* reason; consider sequencing this row first. **Verify:** whichever option, a Playwright pin on the **property** — step onto a locale cell, assert a sibling node's quest activated (or, for (3), assert no quest names a non-primary `activateNode`) — plus a `check:walk` phase asserting the chosen invariant so it cannot silently regrow. **⟶ EVIDENCE ADDED 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02h) — the first COMPLETE ARC measured end-to-end as a casualty of this row, and the archive proves the collision was not the arc's doing.** §CROWN-01 (The Three Crowns of the Swamp, `00fe35c`) authored **nine** nodes on **nine distinct cells** and argued the spacing cell-by-cell against four named neighbours; `git show 00fe35c:roll2hit-v3.html` confirms all nine coordinates — WG0 `{15,3}` … HCA `{32,3}` — plus EC `{19,2}`, SC `{19,4}`, OC `{23,4}`, AT `{25,6}`, EA `{27,6}` exactly as the lab report states. At HEAD the nine cells are **two**: `25,206` holds **12** nodes (`HJ2 HCA HJ1 OTP HJ3 DBN MSY HG1 HN1 SDQ HW1 WG0`) and `22,191` holds `KTM INN`. WG0 and INN are the primaries by `NODE_MAP` declaration order, so **HW1 · HJ1 · HG1 · HJ2 · HN1 · HJ3 · HCA are unarrivable — 7 of 9**. Measured blast radius for this one arc: **24 of its 34 quests cannot activate** (HW1 8 · HG1 8 · HN1 8; the 10 that can are all at INN); the 18-quest Crone Mark supply is entirely on unreachable nodes so `croneMarks` cannot exceed 0; the HG1 first-visit panel never grants Glut's Gift so `quest_glut_06` never lists; the `story-hca-marks` conversion and the Leviathan hook never render so **the arc cannot close**; two of the three combat nodes never render their battle; and `quest_shore_01` — the **§LXX Atlantean Shore entry quest**, `activateNode:'HCA'` + `gate:{battles:['HCA_BOSS']}` — can never list, so the collision strands a *downstream* arc's doorway too. **This is the argument for option (1) or (2) over (3):** repointing 774 `activateNode` values to primaries would put all 24 Crown quests on WG0, which is not where any of them takes place.
- [ ] **§EPIC-01 — the Epic Battleground quest ids are severed from the ids the runtime COMPUTES; 40 quests orphaned and the whole payment layer is unreachable (found 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02l). NO DESIGN CALL — highest player impact of any open row.** Seven sites derive an epic quest id by string concatenation from a node code — `` `const primaryId = 'quest_' + ebCode.toLowerCase() + '_primary';@30346` ``, `` `const returnId = 'quest_' + pb.nodeCode.toLowerCase() + '_return';@36612` ``, `` `'quest_' + node.code.toLowerCase() + '_primary';@35892` `` and four more at 28196 / 30382 / 35864 / 35865. **All seven are byte-identical to the earliest surviving build, where they resolved**: `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` was keyed `EF`, so `'quest_' + 'EF'.toLowerCase() + '_primary'` produced `quest_ef_primary`, which exists. `c1d5a94` (2026-05-29) renamed the `NODE_MAP` keys, the `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` keys, `` `const _EB_CODES = [@28030` ``, every `completion:{ battles:[…] }`, every `waypointNode` and even the `failText` prose — **and left the forty `QUEST_DB` ids as `quest_ef_*`**. The runtime now computes `quest_prn_primary`, which does not exist. **Blast radius:** (1) **all 40 authored epic quests are orphaned** — `` `if (!q || q.type === 'epic' || !q.activateNode) continue;@37020` `` excludes them from arrival-activation by design and the chip handlers use the other namespace, so 40 titles/descs/hints/passTexts/failTexts + 20 `reward` values never render; (2) **the payment layer is unreachable** — `` `function _storyEbReturnBeat(ebCode) {@30358` `` has exactly one caller, gated on a key whose sole writer is guarded by `if (QUEST_DB[returnId])` (falsy), so **no gold, no reward item and no return beat for any of the twenty contracts**, `ebReturnDone` stays empty and the `allEbReturns` milestone (≥5) plus both end-game tallies are permanently zero; (3) the parent-node chip **sticks** at *"⚔ EPIC … awaits"* forever because nothing completes a phantom key; (4) the accept toast falls back to the raw code, *"📋 PRN"*. The combat half is unaffected — `defeatedBattles[pb.nodeCode]` never goes through `QUEST_DB`, so the boss dies and the player simply is not paid. **75 days live.** **Fix:** rename the 40 `QUEST_DB` ids to the live codes (`quest_prn_primary` / `quest_prn_return`, …) — the ids are referenced by literal in only three places — or add one legacy→live map at the four derivation points; prefer the rename, since a second name for the same node is what caused this. **No gate can see it:** `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself and never evaluates a computed id — this is **§DX-02o one level deeper**, and the wanted check is *"every string a code path CONSTRUCTS as a quest id resolves"*. ***The durable lesson: a rename is applied to every place the code spells the identifier and missed where the code computes it — grep for the concatenation, not only for the name.***
- [ ] **§EPIC-02 — 40 Epic Battleground `failText` strings name a raw LIVE node code and put the NPC in the wrong place (found 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02l). NO DESIGN CALL, but blocked behind §EPIC-01.** Every one of the 40 epic quests carries a `failText` of the form *"Woodcutter Bram is at PRN, waiting for word that hasn't come."* — two defects in one player-facing sentence: it prints an **internal node code** to the player, and it places the NPC **at the battleground** when `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE`'s own `npcNode` puts them at the parent (Bram is at `FRO`; `PRN` is the Maw he refuses to enter). This is the §AUDIT-03s class inverted: those were *dead* codes in player-facing strings, these are **live** codes, so every existence check passes while the sentence is both jargon and wrong. Also fix the *"📋 PRN"* accept-toast fallback from §EPIC-01 (4). **Low urgency only because §EPIC-01 currently prevents any of these strings from rendering** — repairing §EPIC-01 without this makes 40 wrong sentences newly visible, so sequence them together.
- [ ] **§EPIC-03 — the Epic Battleground negotiation is strictly dominated, its failure branch inverts the spec on both halves, and the failure panel is hardcoded masculine for 20 NPCs (found 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02l). SMALL DESIGN CALL: what should failing cost?** `` `const accepted = _ebNpcNegotiated ? d.paymentCeiling : d.paymentFloor;@30343` `` — the CHA check (`` `S_story.abilityScores || {}).cha) || 8;@30290` ``, DC 17) gates only the **damage**, never the gold: declining pays the floor, passing pays the ceiling, **failing pays the ceiling and takes 1–4 non-lethal damage**. So negotiating is never wrong and the check has no economic consequence. The lab report specified *"failure to negotiate: not punished beyond losing the margin; NPC doesn't become hostile"* — HEAD punishes it (a scripted beating) **and** does not withhold the margin: both halves inverted. On **16 of 20** contracts `paymentCeiling === paymentFloor`, so the exchange is cosmetic there regardless. **Two riders, neither needing the design call:** (a) `` `Math.max(1, Math.floor(Math.random() * 4) + 1)@30310` `` and the d20 three lines above are **unseeded** `Math.random()` writing persisted `S_story.hp` → a named **§DX-02m** instance, same shape as §DOC-02k's `_cyRoll`; (b) the shared failure panel is written in hardcoded masculine prose (*"Then **he** moves — knee into your gut…"*, *"Then **he** crouches down"*) and renders for whichever quest-giver was asked — **ten of the twenty are women** (Rona, Gwynne, Wane, Tula, Maris, Draketide, Wren, Zephyrine, Dora Flint, Sigrid). Same class, two smaller sites: `quest_ea_return.passText` says *"Draketide grins once. Then **he** starts making lists"* two lines below a `desc` that says *"**She** is reading in the archive"*, and Ithiel is *"her"* in `quest_ek_*` but *"his"* in `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` and the node text. **(b) is a no-design-call fix and worth doing on its own.**
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03y — the legacy code `BK` survived a migration by resolving to the WRONG live node, and three registries followed it (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02g).** This is §AUDIT-03m's **"worse than dead"** class, found for the first time in **engine data** rather than doc prose: legacy `BK` was the **Broken Tooth Tavern, Visby** — live **`VBY`** (`num:25, name:'bar', act:5`, `npc:'Warlord Kael Mordus'`, and the Mordus/shaman-stalemate scene in its own node text) — but `BK` is *still a live key*, now naming **"Birka Shore — Northern Longship Landing"** (`num:241, name:"beach", act:1`). Because it resolves, `check:noderegs` passes and the sentence stays wrong. Three surfaces followed it: **(a)** `CORELLI_APPEARANCES` stop 3, whose opener says *"Visby. Well."* and *"Mordus runs a tight tavern"*, firing at a beach; **(b)** `NPC_DIALOGUE.BK` = *Warlord Kael Mordus*, quote *"You're not from Visby. But you're here"*; **(c)** `VENDOR_NODES` contains `'BK'`. `VBY` appears in none of them. Plus **9 × `activateNode:'BK'`, 2 × `waypointNode:'BK'`, 1 × `nodeCode:'BK'`** to adjudicate individually — some may legitimately mean the Birka shore. **(a) and (b) are no design call** (the prose names Visby explicitly). **Compounding, not causing:** `BK` also shares cell (10,197) with the starting node `LHR` and is non-primary, so it can never be arrived at (§AUDIT-03x) — which makes `encoded_letter` (80gp) **unobtainable**, since stop 3 is its only grant path, and with it the retroactive-decode payoff. Repointing to `VBY` fixes both and does **not** wait on §AUDIT-03x. **(b) `false_warrant` is a 200gp purchase with no surface** — its "skip a corridor encounter, disabled at `voidPressure >= 7`" mechanic shipped at `194a810` and was removed with its host `storyCorridorTravel` by `85cc43e` (§CELL-11A); the item is still sold at **reachable** stop 2, renders under 💊 Consumables, and has **no Use button** (the only consumable handler tests `effect === 'advantage_next_attack'`) and **no Read button** (`readableItems` filters `type === 'readable'`; it is `type:'consumable'`). 🟡 That half is the **same class as §AUDIT-03v and §AUDIT-03w** — third instance in four increments — so close all three together with a *detector*, not a fourth hand-filed row. **⟶ A FOURTH SURFACE FOLLOWED `BK`, AND IT IS THE FIRST THAT BREAKS A MECHANIC RATHER THAN A SENTENCE (2026-08-11, §DOC-02k).** `quest_d0205_a3` — Act III of the §D02-05 Void Fracture Maze, which this report's spec places *"as an EB approach room at BK"* — ships `` `completion:{ battles:['BK'] }@21579` ``, and Act IV gates on the same key. At HEAD `` `BK: { num:241@9011` `` is the Birka beach, and **its `NODE_MAP` record carries no `battle` field at all**; **nothing anywhere in 38,712 lines writes `defeatedBattles['BK']`**. So Acts III, IV and V of that chain are **unreachable**, and `` `S_story.mazeSolvedChecks = 3;@21582` `` — Act III's `onComplete` — can never run. **This is the sharpest argument yet for the row:** every other `BK` casualty is a sentence naming the wrong place, which a reader could notice; this one is a **five-act chain silently truncated**, and `check:noderegs` passes it perfectly because the code *resolves*. It is also **independent of §AUDIT-03x** — repointing BK's cell so it can be arrived at would not create a battle on a beach — so this half needs its own adjudication: either repoint Act III's `battles` at the node the maze actually occupies, or give `BK` the encounter its five sibling chains all have.
- [ ] **§DX-02q — `check:noderegs` cannot see a SEMANTIC collision (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02g).** Every phase of gate #13 asks *"does this code resolve?"* — and §AUDIT-03y is the failure that passes that test perfectly while being wrong. Wanted: a phase that asks whether the surrounding content **contradicts** the node the code resolves to — e.g. a place name in an adjacent string (`'Visby'`, `'Mordus'`) that appears in a *different* node's `label`/`text`/`npc` than the one the code names. Cheap high-signal seed: for each node-valued reference, compare capitalised place-words in the same entry against the target node's `label`; flag a mismatch that resolves elsewhere. Explicit classification in the #13/#14 house style, since flavour text legitimately names other places. **Its limit should be stated rather than discovered:** it can only catch a collision whose prose names the right place out loud.
- [ ] **§DX-02r — the activation-gate grammar can say *attempted* and *done* but not *failed*, and three quests authored as failure consolation therefore activate for everyone (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02h).** `'failed'` is a **real, written quest status** — `` `S_story.quests[questId] = 'failed'@7001` `` sets it when a non-retryable skill check misses — and `_matchActivationLeaf`'s own comment names it: *"questsAttempted → every listed quest has a non-empty status (active/done/failed/complete)"*. But the leaf grammar offers only `` `g.questsAttempted.every@22060` `` (non-empty) and `questsDone` (terminal pass). **`questsFailed` has 0 occurrences in the file.** The consequence is live in §CROWN-01 Amendment A, whose §A.4 designs the mechanic explicitly — *"A failed skill check does not strand the player — it redirects them… Activates if quest_X_01 resolved as fail"* — as three consolation errands (`quest_whisper_kelpie`, `quest_glut_mudcrab`, `quest_wane_spawn`). All three ship `gate:{ questsAttempted:[…] }`, so **a player who PASSES the check is dispatched after the Kelpie exactly as one who fails**, and the design's whole point — that the body goes and learns what the mind missed — is erased. This is an **invariant-#4 case in its cleanest form**: the capability is missing from the *grammar*, so the fix is a new leaf term, never a `_legacy_fn` closure or a per-quest workaround. **No design call for the term itself** (`questsFailed:[ids]` → `QS[id] === 'failed'`, mirroring `questsDone` line for line); **a small call on the three quests** — repoint them to `questsFailed`, or keep them universal and rewrite the three `desc`/`hint` strings that frame them as consolation. **Measure the corpus first:** grep every quest whose text implies a fail-branch prerequisite, since three found while verifying one report is not a search. **Risk:** low — a new leaf is additive and no shipped gate uses the key. **Verify:** a `quest-runtime-uqf` pin that `questsFailed` matches `'failed'` and rejects `'complete'`/`'active'`/absent, plus (if repointed) a Playwright pin that passing `quest_whisper_01` does **not** list `quest_whisper_kelpie` while failing it does.
