- [ ] **§DX-02s — the Doc Health Badge is the stalest artifact in the corpus and every row of it reads ✅ (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02i).** `index.md` §"Doc Health Badge" exists *because* `lab-report-documentation-system-design.md` recommended it (FC01, 2026-05-24) as *"a live count of sync-pass completion visible at a glance."* It shipped, and at HEAD it is wrong on every mechanical row: **HTML line count `36,933` vs live `38,712`** (+1,779), **"Lab reports on disk" `85` vs `107`** (+22), **"Lab reports in index" `85`** vs **87 unique filenames actually referenced** — and that last row is wrong in *both* directions. **26 reports on disk are absent from `index.md`** (the whole `vm01*` series a–g4, the `play-01*` series, `warrants-board`, `void-tide-bounties`, `death-loot-grave`, `javascript-mud`, `npc-card-map`, …) and **6 index rows name files that do not exist** — `lab-report-api-01-02-mechanics-combat-review.md`, `lab-report-loot-drop-weapon-economy.md`, `lab-report-plan-cleanup-v13.md`, `lab-report-plan-cleanup-v17.md`, `lab-report-plan-cleanup-world-builder-arc.md`, `lab-report-timeline-history-completed.md`, **all six deleted in one commit, `120d617`**, with their rows kept. The badge is not missing a procedure: `index.md` prints the repair instruction directly beneath it (*"Update this table at the start of each session: recount lab reports with `ls lab-reports/lab-report-*.md | wc -l`"*). **That is the finding — a check that depends on a human running it reports green when nobody ran it**, and the drift was invisible for a month because the ✅ column is hand-typed. **The same failure is present on day one:** at `59a9e0d` the repo held **22** lab reports while `index.md` referenced **23**, and the report says "23" twice because it counted the manifest instead of the disk. **Every one of these rows is mechanically computable**, which makes this the cheapest gate in the family: `check:docindex` = `wc -l roll2hit-v3.html` + `ls lab-reports/*.md | wc -l` + a two-way set difference between the disk and the filenames `index.md` references, failing on any drift and on any phantom row. Fold the live-entity row into `npm run stats` (§DX-01g) rather than re-deriving it. **No design call.** **Risk:** low — a new gate plus one doc edit. **Verify:** the gate goes red on today's `index.md`, green after the refresh; a positive-control test that adds a phantom filename and a deleted file and proves both directions fail. **⟶ (b) a second, unrelated member found the same pass, and it does need a small call: two favor storage shapes, one gate grammar.** `_setNpcFavor` writes the `npcFavorability` map and the gate leaf `` `if (g.favorMin)@22064` `` reads **only** that map — but `` `fav_corelli: 0@23143` `` is a real `_S_DEFAULTS()` field with its own writer (`` `S_story.fav_corelli = Math.min@31792` ``, = `min(3, corelli_purchase_count)`) and two readers, verified live by §DOC-02g. It is a **private scalar the gate grammar cannot see**, so no quest can ever gate on Corelli's favor without a `_legacy_fn` closure — invariant-#4 pressure with the declarative alternative already in the language. **The call:** fold `fav_corelli` into `npcFavorability.corelli` (one writer, one migration on load), or widen `favorMin` to consult both. Invariant #4 argues for the first, since the second entrenches a second shape.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03z — a live home doc that no gate can see, and a state field that has never existed propagated across a correct doc pointer (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02i).** Two members, one file class each, both instances of §AUDIT-03s's premise — *dead references outside `.md`*. **(a) `sources/froberger-journal-all-entries.txt` is a live home doc invisible to every gate.** The HTML names it itself: `` `const FROBERGER_JOURNAL = [@27184` `` carries `// → doc: froberger-journal-all-entries.txt`. But `scripts/legacy-codes.js` (gate #16) drives from an explicit `SWEEP` list of **eleven `.md` paths** and the string `.txt` does not occur in the script at all, so the file is neither SWEEP nor PENDING nor HISTORY — it is simply **out of the universe**. Consequence: all **41** of its entry headers still carry retired 26×16 codes (`[City Gate — CI]`, `[Tavern — TV]`, `[Crypt — CR]`, `[Bazaar — BA]`, `[Sewers — SE]`, `[Slums — SL]`, `[Midlands — MI]`, `[Island Settlement — IS]`, …) while `FROBERGER_JOURNAL` uses the live ones (`LHR`, `MHQ`, `KRN`, `LLA`, `SFT`, `BMA`, `MAN`, `PDL`) — the exact `CI→LHR / TV→MHQ / CR→KRN / BA→LLA / SL→BMA` remap the engine records in its own `birkaNpcs` note, recovered here independently. **And the same pass closed FC02, the 79-day-old "content comparison has not been done" item, by doing it: 39 of 41 entries are verbatim after quote/whitespace normalisation, and entries 17 and 29 are entirely different text in the two artifacts** — HTML 17 (`MAN`) *"The woman at the archive disagreed with my taxonomy of the eastern war…"* vs txt `[Midlands — MI]` *"The midland family fed me and gave me the grain-store loft…"*; HTML 29 (`PDL`) *"There is a question I should have asked before she left…"* vs txt `[Island Settlement — IS]` *"The island has seven families and one boat…"*. Not drift — the `.txt` retains superseded entries. **Fix: widen `SWEEP` to accept non-`.md` live docs** (the classification style is unchanged; only the extension filter moves), then sweep the 41 headers and re-sync entries 17 and 29 from the HTML, which is the source of truth. **No design call.** **(b) `fav_auros` — a field that never existed — is stated as a trigger condition in the engine AND in `world.md`, reached across a *correct* FC05 pointer.** `` `const S29_AUROS_THEORY =@27050` `` carries `// → doc: world.md §S29` and states the trigger as *"fires at CY when frobergerLastEntryRead && fav_auros >= 2"*. **`fav_auros` occurs exactly once in 38,712 lines — in that comment** — is not a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field, and has no reader; the live guard is `_npcFavor('auros') >= 2` at `` `s29LineDelivered) {@32669` ``. `world.md` repeats `fav_auros >= 2` at **three** lines, having inherited it across the pointer. The same comment names the node `CY`, which is not a `NODE_MAP` key (the §AUDIT-03p born-dead class; `world.md` annotates it correctly as *"at `HKG` (historical `CY`)"*, so the doc is right about the place and wrong about the field). **Neither error is reachable by any gate:** #16 scans `*.md` so an engine comment is out of scope, #13 phase 6 is comment-aware **by design** (the §AUDIT-03f lesson) and skips it, and nothing validates a state-field name in prose. **This is the sharpest available statement about the two-way-sync thesis: a bijection guarantees a link, not a truth.** Fix is three doc edits + one comment; **no design call**. Worth measuring the corpus for other `fav_<npc>` prose references before closing.
- [ ] **§DX-02t — the short rest is implemented twice, and the second copy silently withholds four of its consequences (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02j).** `` `function storyShortRest@25817` `` is the canonical Layer-13 path. A **second, independent implementation** lives inside the post-battle return row at `` `const _rests = () => S_story.shortRests@7133` ``, and the heal arithmetic is byte-identical between them — `` `const base = Math.floor(S_story.hpMax * 0.25)@7145` `` / `` `const heal = isInn ? base : base * 2@7146` `` against `` `const base  = Math.floor(S_story.hpMax * 0.25)@25838` `` / `` `const heal  = isInn ? base : base * 2@25839` `` (the pairs differ only in whitespace, which is exactly what a copy-paste divergence looks like). **Both spend the same `S_story.shortRests` allowance and both apply the 2× off-inn multiplier. Only one of them does anything else.** The battle-return copy does **not**: (a) call `_maybeAddKnowledgeBead`, so resting through it builds **no Necklace of Knowledge** — the collection subsystem's entire acquisition premise is *"the first time the player rests at each unique location"* and this path is a rest that does not count; (b) set `shortRestedAtNodes`, so no Necklace Token is granted **and no `restedAtMin` gate credit accrues** — and that leaf is real and consumed: `` `if (g.restedAtMin)@22074` `` gates `` `quest_d0206_a3: { id:@21790` `` on `` `restedAtMin:{ SZG:1 }@21793` ``, so **a player who rests at SZG via the post-battle button cannot open Act III of that arc** and has no way to learn why; (c) restore Action Surge charges, which `` `if (_lv >= 2) S_story.surgeCharges@25844` `` does and the engine's own comment at 24641 says is rest-gated — so the resource is spent and the recharge withheld; (d) print the Boy Scouts message, **though it applies the doubling anyway**, so the one path that most looks like roughing it is the one that never says so. **No design call** — the fix is to make the button call `storyShortRest(S_story.currentCode)` and delete the copy, which is a strict superset of what it does today. **Risk:** low, but note it re-renders (`storyRender` at the tail of `storyShortRest`) where the inline copy deliberately only calls `_updateHeal()`, so the button's own DOM row needs checking after the swap — §7½ applies. **Verify:** a Playwright pin that a post-battle short rest at a fresh node grants a bead and a token, and that `quest_d0206_a3` opens after resting at SZG by either route. **The durable half:** this is Hazard #2's shape in the control-flow dimension — two copies of one mechanic, where the copy is not *wrong* on the line it duplicates and is wrong in everything it omits, so a diff of the arithmetic proves nothing.

- [ ] **§DX-02u — `voidFluxCleared` has ZERO writers, so `quest_d0209` dies at Act III and `voidFluxActive` becomes PERMANENT (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02k). No design call.** `voidFluxCleared` occurs **5 times in 38,712 lines**: one `_S_DEFAULTS()` declaration, one quest `desc` that *describes* it being set (*"[voidFluxCleared flag set on victory]"*), and **three gate reads** — `` `completion:{ flags:['voidFluxCleared'] }@21636` `` on `quest_d0209_a3`, Act IV's `gate:{ flags:['voidFluxCleared'] }`, and Act V's `flagsAny`. **Nothing anywhere assigns it.** The gate leaf resolves against `S_story` directly (`g.flags.every(f => !!st[f])`), and no `mission_bit` carries that flag name, so the field is `false` for the whole run: **acts III, IV and V of the §D02-09 Void Flux chain are unreachable.** **The consequence is the row, not the stalled chain.** Act I's pass sets `S_story.voidFluxActive = true`, and Act V's pass is the **only** site that sets it back to `false`. So a player who passes a DC 10 INT/Arcana check is **permanently inside the inversion field**: both healing sites (the inventory potion Drink handler and the Brynn's-Loaf Eat handler) route through the `[Void Flux] Heal → Hurt` branch, whose arithmetic is `hp + heal - half - half` — a **net +0 or +1 HP** — for the rest of the run, with no surface anywhere telling the player why potions stopped working. **Fix, no design call:** make Act III match its five siblings, which all complete on a battle — either `completion:{ battles:['RAI'] }` (the node the whole chain targets) or write `voidFluxCleared` on the RAI victory. **The durable half, and it widens a proposed gate:** this is the **inverse** of the §DX-02n family. Those fields are **write-only** — saved, reloaded, never consulted. This one is **read-only** — three consumers and no producer — which is not dead weight but a **broken dependency**, and a `check:deadconsts` scoped to *unread* fields steps straight over it. A gate that wants to catch both must check **both directions of the reader/writer census**, and the cheapest place to run it is over gate-leaf flag names in `QUEST_DB`, where every name is a literal.

- [ ] **§DUNGEON-01-FU — §3's Tribble lore reversal is contradicted by the shipped item text, and the flag it says it removed never existed (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02k). Small design call: keep the revision or retire it.** The §DUNGEON-01 lab report's §3 is a locked decision — *"Tribbles do NOT multiply"*, *"no `tribbleCount` growth formula"*, *"not Star Trek references"*, *"they do not occupy inventory slots — `tribbleCount` is a bare counter"*, and the Brynn overflow line *"is removed from the IN node render block"*. **HEAD contradicts four of the five in player-facing strings.** (a) Every Fuzzy Tribble is an inventory object whose `desc` asserts multiplication — `` `You are fairly certain it is multiplying@21850` `` on the `quest_d0208_a2` grant and *"Soft. Multiplying."* on the three granted by `quest_d0208_a4`. (b) Those same sites increment `tribbleCount` **and** push inventory objects, so the "bare counter" is double-bookkeeping. (c) The Brynn line ships at `_tc >= 10` in the Mimic Meadows panel — *relocated* from the inn, which is why grepping the IN block finds nothing. (d) The specified 1–2 / 3–4 / 5+ bands shipped as `>= 5` / `>= 10`, and the 5+ band's **CY** flavour line was never built. **`tribbleOverflow` itself is clean — 0 hits and 0 commits ever**, which is the point: the flag §3 declares it is removing was never implemented, so the section describes undoing something that had never been done while the *content* it believed it was deleting shipped under a different threshold in a different block. **The design call is one sentence:** does the world keep the report's corruption-not-multiplication lore (in which case four `desc` strings and one threshold line are copy edits) or accept the shipped Star-Trek reading (in which case the lab report's §3 stays as a NOT-SHIPPED record and nothing changes in the HTML)? **No engine risk either way — all five sites are strings.** **The durable half → the 13th §DOC-02 instrument: a design doc's REVERSAL is a claim like any other.** A section that says *"the previous spec had X; this is cut"* asserts both that X existed and that it is gone, and `git log -S` is the only thing that can check the first half. Also note what makes this one invisible: §2's four invented mimic statlines have **0 commits each**, yet all four creatures live at `LIM` **as prose** in the node text — **the narrative was copied and the illustrative statblocks were not**, so a reader checking the meadow against the report sees the creatures and concludes the section shipped.

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03aa — "Boy Scouts" names two different mechanics with opposite rationales, in two spellings (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02j).** The 2026-05-21 balance report specified one award: *"when the player rests at a non-inn location… double the standard short-rest HP recovery,"* rationale *"sleeping rough is harder… a ranger sleeps better under stars than in a tavern."* **Two mechanics now carry the name and only one of them means that.** (a) **Short rest** — `` `Boy Scouts Award — doubled!@25858` ``, fired on `!isInn`. This is the spec exactly. (b) **Long rest** — `` `Boyscout Night! Double rolls@36317` ``, fired on `` `const _rollCount = _isFirstSleep ? 2 : 1@36255` ``: a **first-visit** bonus that doubles the sleep heal dice and **fires at an inn**, which is the precise inverse of the rationale the name was coined for. A player sleeping in the softest bed in the world is told they are camping. The two also disagree on spelling (`Boy Scouts` / `Boyscout`), so neither greps the other, and the report's own term *"Boy Scouts Camping Award"* has **0 commits in the file's history** — the name in the design doc never shipped at all. **Small design call: which mechanic keeps the name.** Recommend (a) keeps it — it is the one the name describes — and (b) is renamed to something first-visit-shaped (*"First Night"*), since its actual design is a novelty bonus for a new bed, which is a good mechanic wearing the wrong word. **Risk:** low, both are display strings plus one comment. **Verify:** grep both spellings to zero overlap, and a Playwright pin on each message firing on its own trigger. **Why it is an §AUDIT-03 row and not a typo:** this is §DOC-02c's retired-vocabulary hazard in its *live* form — there, §TIMELESS-01 removed "Hunt" and §KG-01 reintroduced a different mechanic under the same name 12 days later. Here both are live simultaneously, which is worse: the collision cannot be dated, and no doc distinguishes them, so every future sentence about "the Boy Scouts bonus" is ambiguous by construction.

- [ ] **§DESIGN-04 — nothing in the live game answers *"where do I find monster X?"* (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02c; 🟡 DESIGN CALL).** The 2026-05-21 battleground report was written to eliminate the "lost in a field" failure mode, and its instrument was `HUNTING_GROUNDS` — a bijective terrain→node table making the question an O(1) lookup. **§TIMELESS-01 (`7952752`, 2026-06-26) deleted it and nothing replaced it.** Measured at HEAD: no bestiary locator, no terrain index, no per-monster habitat surface — **398 monsters across 111 terrains and 416 nodes**, with the only route from "I need a wolf" to "I am fighting a wolf" being to walk until the seeded stream rolls one. By the original report's own criteria the failure mode it named is currently unaddressed. **This is a design call, not a defect**, because the modern game may legitimately answer the question a different way: quest `activateNode` puts the player where the content is, and the Warrant's Board (§BOARD-01) is already the social discovery surface. **The three options to put to the user:** (1) DECIDED-STAY — arrival-driven content is the answer and no locator is wanted; (2) a read-only **bestiary** surface listing, per monster, the terrains it appears in (data already exists — `WORLD_DB[t].monsters` inverted) with no navigation affordance; (3) route it through the Board as a bounty that names its terrain. **Do not build before the call.** Note the invariant fence on any option: a locator may *inform* movement but may never shortcut it (invariant #3, `` `no jump travel. checkpointNode@26048` ``).

- [ ] **§FISH-01 — the entire Yugurt Lake fishing sub-game is unreachable, because one node is declared 59 lines too late (found 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02m; 🔴 NO DESIGN CALL).** `` `const CELL_GRID = (() => {@9852` `` builds each cell's locale list by iterating `Object.keys(NODE_MAP)`, and only `list[0]` — the primary — can ever become `S_story.currentCode` (`` `S_story.currentCode = destCode;@28373` ``, where `destCode = res.destCodes[0]`). `` `LYR:{r:2,c:194}@9423` `` and `` `BOO:{r:2,c:194}@9422` `` occupy the same cell, and `` `LYR:{ num:41@8723` `` (Arctic Wastes — Detour, act 7) is declared **59 lines before** `` `BOO:{ num:75@8782` `` (Yugurt Lake, act 3). So `CELL_GRID['2,194'] = ['LYR','BOO']`, arriving there renders the arctic node, `` `const hasFish = node.isFishingLake;@35366` `` is never true, and `` `main: 'Cast a Line',@35370` `` — the **only** call site of `` `function storyFishing() {@30392` `` — never draws. `BOO` is `sleep:false`, so `checkpointNode` cannot reach it either. **Stranded: 11 quests** — 3 cannot activate (`quest_horned_shark` · `quest_shale_drop` · `quest_night_eel`, all `activateNode:'BOO'`) and 8 cannot complete, because their `completion` reads `fishingCatchLog`/`fishingQuestFlags` and `` `S_story.fishingCatchLog.unshift@30614` `` is the **only writer in the file** (`quest_fishing_guide`, `quest_fish_01`, and the six-part Emmer Finch arc `quest_guide_01`–`06`, whose whole subject is learning to fish). Plus **~100 authored data rows** — `` `const FISH_POOL = [@26504` `` (20) · `` `const NIGHT_FISH_POOL = [@26526` `` (5) · their 25 `MONSTER_POOL` statlines and 20 trophy drops · `` `const LAKE_MAGIC_DB = {@26536` `` (8) · `` `const BAIT_TABLES = {@26632` `` (3×6) · `` `const FISHING_RODS = [@26700` `` (4) · `` `const FISH_GOLD_VALUES = {@26692` `` (25) · `` `const FISH_SIZE_TIERS = [@26685` `` (5) — two flag writers only a fish battle reaches (`` `hornedSharkSlain = true;@7051` `` · `` `lanternEelLanded = true;@7052` ``) and the `` `id:'ke_eel_pouch'@26228` `` key event. **The cabin survives**: `` `SSJ:{ num:76@8786` `` is alone in cell `4,192`, so the Fisherman, the free bed (`sleepCost:0`) and the six-quest §XLV tournament all render — **the player can meet the Fisherman and never fish**, which is why this reads as content-missing rather than as a bug. **This is §AUDIT-03x's class and its worst single-node instance by stranded volume** (§CROWN-01 lost 7 nodes and 24 quests, but its mechanics existed elsewhere; here one non-primary node takes an entire self-contained sub-game). **It does NOT wait on §AUDIT-03x's design call** — the fix is two lines: move `BOO`'s `NODE_MAP` declaration above `LYR`'s (cheapest, and `CELL_GRID` order is the only thing that reads it), or relocate one of the two to a free adjacent cell. Verify by asserting `cellCode('2,194') === 'BOO'` and that the FISH section renders on arrival.

- [ ] **§DX-02v — three home docs describe the retired 2d20 fishing cast as the current mechanic (found 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02m).** `world.md:160` says *"**Current system (Layer 37):** 2d20 cast roll → fish rank 1–20 → immediate combat"*, `story.md:811` says *"**Current Mechanic (Layer 37):** … The player rolls 2d20 to determine which fish answers"*, and `monsters.md` says it three times (`:616` *"used exclusively by the fishing system (2d20 cast; fish rank must fall within [lo, hi])"*, `:753` in the milepoint trace, `:780` in the function table). **HEAD has not run that mechanic since Layer 47**, which replaced it with a four-phase Catch system (DEX cast vs DC 12 → Catch roll → size tier → Type roll for rarity) — see `` `function storyFishing() {@30392` ``. This is the §DOC-02j `LOOT_TABLE` rot direction and the more dangerous one: a reader greps the home doc and concludes the code still does it. Two riders in the same row: `` `const FISH_POOL = [@26504` ``'s own comment reads *"20 ranks, Needle Minnow → Yugurt's Leviathan"* while rank 20 is `Yugurt's Dread` and **no fish is named Yugurt's Leviathan** (rank 17 is `Barbed Leviathan Trout`); and `monsters.md:780` anchors `storyFishing()` at a bare **11537**, which is its *archive* line — the function is at 30392 — a bare-number anchor `check:anchors` cannot audit because it carries no symbol (§DX-01e). Fix: rewrite the three docs to the four-phase system, correct the engine comment, and migrate the bare anchor to `symbol@line`.

It carries live prose reading *"Obtained from The Fisherman at **YC** (free, no gold cost). Required to trigger `storyFishing()` at **YL**"* — two dead codes in an instruction a reader would follow, unreportable **by classification**, while the file's own comment above `PENDING` asserts *"every live doc is now gate-fenced."* Same class as §AUDIT-03z (a live home doc outside every gate's universe) in a sharper form: not outside the file filter, but inside it and misfiled. The call is which of the six move to `SWEEP` (all six are maintained; `index.md` and `quest.md` are generated-ish and may want a third classification) — then sweep them, remembering §AUDIT-03m's lesson that **annotation without verification launders a wrong claim into a confident-looking live one.**

### §DOC-02 — Lab-report verification program (NEW 2026-08-11)

> **Premise (user directive 2026-08-11):** the **107** files in `lab-reports/` are the project's design record, and most were written *before* the thing they describe shipped. They are HISTORY docs (`scripts/legacy-codes.js`) — never rewritten to hide what was believed at the time — but a spec that was never checked against the code reads exactly like a spec that was. The program: **take the oldest report by mtime, re-measure every claim against `roll2hit-v3.html`, rewrite it short and IEEE-shaped** (abstract · method · as-built inventory with `symbol@line` anchors · spec→shipped delta table · risk-register outcome · defects → BACKLOG rows), and file everything it finds. **The house rules that govern this work:** claims that did not ship are marked **NOT SHIPPED** and *kept*, never deleted (a silently removed claim reads as one that held); never read a node code off a doc table (§AUDIT-03m); and annotation without verification launders a wrong claim into a confident-looking live one (§AUDIT-03m-FU). **Ordering note (corrected 2026-08-11 by §DOC-02b — re-measured, the §DOC-02a figures were wrong):** the directory holds **107** `.md` files, not 109 (`git ls-files lab-reports/ | wc -l`; six were deleted by `120d617`). **44** of them shared one mtime (`2026-06-09 11:06:54`, a bulk copy) when the program opened — so "oldest" within that block is resolved alphabetically — and there are **45 distinct mtimes** in all. Each verified report gets a fresh mtime and leaves the block, so the block shrinks by one per increment (43 after §DOC-02a, 42 after §DOC-02b); **re-derive the ordering from `stat`, never from this note's arithmetic.**

The predictor is not *"does the passage cite code"* but ***"could the author copy it"*** — a table or a function body can be pasted; a ten-step call sequence must be reconstructed, and memory supplies plausible names the file never held. **Sharpest single measurement: the Hunt/Warp dialog was built, styled, and never once shown.** At `32c10c5` the overlay, card, and all three buttons exist with the exact labels the report quotes; every JS reference to it is two bulk close-all arrays and one `classList.remove('visible')` — **nothing ever adds `visible`**, no handler is bound, no field is written. - [ ] **§DOC-02k… — the remaining 94 reports.** Next by the ordering rule (re-derive from `stat`, see the preamble): `dungeon-ten-themes`, … through the 2026-06-09 block, then the distinct-mtime files oldest-first. **A sixth accelerator, added by §DOC-02f and mandatory for any claim about the past: read the ARCHIVE, not HEAD.** `git show <earliest-commit>:roll2hit-v3.html` is the only instrument that can say what the file actually contained when the report was written; HEAD can only say what it contains now, and the difference is where two of this program's corrections have come from. Pair it with `git log -S` (accelerator four): **zero commits proves non-existence; absence at HEAD proves nothing.** **A seventh, added by the same pass: weight a report's tables and function bodies as evidence, and its traces, walkthroughs and sequence diagrams as claims.** §DOC-02f's data listings had a zero error rate while its execution traces carried five deltas including three identifiers with no commits ever — in one file, by one author, on one day. The predictor is not whether a passage cites code but whether the author could **copy** it. **An eighth, and it is a gate hazard rather than a method: an anchor whose text is not a LITERAL substring of the line it points at is DEAD even when the line is perfectly alive.** §DOC-02f found **5 such anchors left by §DOC-02e** plus 1 of its own, and none of the six symbols had moved — all six lines existed, unchanged, at the cached number. The three ways to mint one, written here **without their `@line` suffixes so this row does not itself mint three more** — a counterexample inside backticks *is* an anchor to the gate, which is how §DOC-02f briefly re-broke it: **markdown table pipe-escaping** (an anchor reading `q.retryGateDays \|\| 1`, where `\|` is the table escape and the source says `||`), an **ellipsis** standing in for elided code (`checkLevelUp: () => { … _checkLevelUp(); }`), and **whitespace normalisation** (an anchor reading `const d20 = …` where the source has `const d20  =` with two spaces). The fix is always to shorten the anchor to the shortest unambiguous *verbatim* fragment — `` `q.retryGateDays@6811` ``, `` `checkLevelUp: () =>@22348` `` — never to widen it into a pretty quotation. **Related and worth stating plainly: §DOC-02e recorded `check:walk` 16/16 green while carrying those 5 dead anchors**, because the run hit the known harness detach artifact and never reached gate 15. *When the detach artifact fires, the gates that did not run are not green — re-run them individually before recording a result.* **Scope one report per increment** — §DOC-02a produced 2 audit rows + 2 doc corrections, §DOC-02b 1 defect row + 1 ordering correction, and §DOC-02c 2 defect rows, all from a single report each. **A fourth accelerator, added by §DOC-02c and now mandatory for any report with dead symbols:** run `git log -S "<symbol>" -- roll2hit-v3.html` on every symbol the census marks dead. It costs one command per symbol and separates **RETIRED** (shipped, later removed — the report was true when written) from **NOT SHIPPED** (never existed under that name — the report was written from intent, not from the file). §DOC-02c's headline finding came from exactly this: `stalkModal` and two of its DOM ids return **0 commits ever**, while the feature they describe shipped three days later under different names. *A report cannot make this distinction about itself; only history can.* **A fifth accelerator, added by §DOC-02d:** when a report names a character two ways, `grep` **both** names in the engine before writing the delta table — the split is usually still there as two keys, and it is the highest-value finding a content report can yield (§AUDIT-03n cost a whole row to repair one). **Three accelerators, now proven three times:** (1) the arc's *maintained* home doc (`story.md`/`world.md`) is usually already correct and annotated, so the lab report's job is the **delta table**, not a second copy of it; (2) the fastest first measurement is always `grep -c` on every key the report claims to have added — a missing entry is a delta, a present entry still needs its *fields* compared; and (3) **for any report that enumerates symbols, batch the whole name list through one `grep -c` loop before reading a line of it** — §DOC-02b's 197-symbol census took one command and immediately partitioned the report into 180 live claims and 17 dead subsystems, which is the shape of the delta table. **One thing to watch for that §DOC-02b found and §DOC-02a did not:** a long report can **contradict itself**, and the tiebreak is mechanical — *believe the section that cites a line number.*

Measured live: 3 bare single-attempt runs went **fail / pass / fail** (quest_muffat_01 is DEX Stealth DC 13; at DEX 8 the total is `d20+1`, so it's a ~45% coin flip — the earlier "reproduces on a bare re-run" reading came from the 2-retry config, not from determinism). `warrants-board:999` was subtler: **every standing assertion already passed** (ESCALATED `+4`, idempotent re-credit, `questDone`) — the only failure was `posAfter ≠ posBefore`, because the test hand-set `playerR/C = 5,9` while setting `currentCode = 'RVP'`, a state no step can produce. `storyRender`'s §CELL-03 sync (`31618`) then corrected the coords to RVP's real cell (17,180) and the test read that correction as a warp. **No jump travel, no player move — the invariant it guards is intact.** **Shipped (test-only, no HTML):** `effort-xp` now forces the fail through the engine's own dial — a scoped `forceFail(id, fn)` helper that raises `sc.dc` to 999 and restores it in a `finally` — the same idiom `warrants-board` itself already used for a forced PASS (`sc.dc = -100`); `warrants-board` derives the stand-on cell from `NODE_COORDS[q.activateNode]` instead of hardcoding one. **Evidence:** both suites **28/28 green on three consecutive runs with `--retries=0`** (so no flake masking); full Playwright **710 passed / 4 failed**, the four being the documented §DX-02d crud-arrays set; `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; `git diff --stat` = 2 test files, +21/−7, `roll2hit-v3.html` untouched (no §7½ — nothing a player sees changed). **Durable fix (the row's actual ask, §DX-02c's proposal): prompt.md §7 now records the FULL red set — four, in one file — with the date last re-proved**, plus the two lessons: *a test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws*, and (again, after §DX-02e) *pin the property, never a fabricated or generated coordinate*. **Original row:** A full `npx playwright test` is **708 passed / 6 failed**, not the 4 that §DX-02d documents. The two extras: `effort-xp.test.js:67` (*§XP-01 — failed skill-check grants 25% of reward XP, once per quest*, asserting at `:91`) and `warrants-board.test.js:999` (*§BOARD-01-FU8 — completing a Void hunt accrues the ESCALATED standing at the real onPass site*). **Both proved pre-existing at HEAD by stash-diff on 2026-07-30** (identical failures with the §DX-01c working tree stashed) and both are deterministic, not flaky — they reproduce on a bare re-run. Neither is in prompt.md §7, which still claims exactly ONE carried red. **This is the second time in three sessions a session has burned a stash-diff re-proving an undocumented red** (§DX-02d made the same complaint about the crud-arrays four) — so the durable fix is the one §DX-02c proposes: either fix them, or record the *full* red set in prompt.md §7 with the date it was last re-proved. Diagnose first — both touch shipped mechanics (effort XP, Warrant standing), so a genuine regression that predates this session is very much possible and would matter more than the bookkeeping. **Risk:** unknown until diagnosed (docs-only if they're stale tests; real if the mechanic drifted).
- [x] **§DX-02e ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — the road was never broken; the test pinned generated coordinates. Verdict: the §KG design promise holds, and the assertion now proves the property instead of the cells.** The row asked which is truth — re-lay the corridor or update the samples. **Measured, not guessed:** auto-travel routes with `_roadGridPathCore` (§NAV-01d road-weighted Dijkstra, road/lane = cost 1, else 2), and every one of the six legs `TLL→SPB→KMS→ZVD→FBR→TVR→SVO` walks **entirely on road** today — `TLL(10,204)→9,204→8,204…8,210→9,210→SPB(10,210)` (the §DX-01a row-8 reroute through TUO, off the Gulf-of-Finland sea cell `10,207`), then the SE staircase `10,211 · 11,212 · 11,213 · 12,214 · 12,215 · 13,216 · 13,217`. **Zero intermediate cells off-road on any leg**, so *"a player currently rolls encounters where the lab report says they shouldn't"* was **false** — nobody was ever rolling. Re-laying the old row-10/11 run was therefore rejected on a second ground too: `ROAD_RUNS` is **generated** (`scripts/build-roads.js — do not hand-edit`), so hand-restored cells die at the next `--apply`. **Shipped:** `kg-zones.test.js` "the corridor is real road" rewritten to call the engine's own router per leg and assert every intermediate cell is `ROAD_CELLS` or a settlement cell, with the full route printed in the failure message. **Evidence:** red reproduced first (1 failed / 4 passed, `10,205 is a road cell`); after — **kg-zones 5/5**, `navigation` + `minimap-nav` **36/36**, `audit03e-node-code` **7/7**, `check:walk` **11/11 exit 0**. **Negative control (temp test, deleted):** deleting `11,212`/`11,213` from the live `ROAD_CELLS` made the router detour `12,211 · 12,212` and the assertion reported both — it is not vacuous. **Docs:** the §KG lab report §2 gained a dated route-drift note (the §DX-02c template: the report's cell ranges are the build-time lay, not the live net); `prompt.md` §7 drops to **ONE** carried Playwright red (`worldbuilder-crud-arrays`, §DX-02d). **No HTML touched, no §7½ — this is a test + docs increment; the player-facing claim was verified through the real router in a live page load.** **The general lesson, recorded in prompt.md §7: never pin generated data by coordinate — pin the property it was generated to satisfy.** Original row follows.
  **§DX-02e (original) — `kg-zones.test.js` "the corridor is real road" is a carried Playwright red (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03e).** The test samples 6 cells the §KG lab report §2 documents as the SPB→TVR road; **4 of them are no longer in `ROAD_CELLS`** (`10,205`, `10,209`, `11,210`, `14,216` — only `11,212`/`12,214` survive). Proved pre-existing at HEAD on 2026-07-29 (identical result against `git show HEAD:roll2hit-v3.html` with zero working-tree changes), so it is **not** an §AUDIT-03e regression. Almost certainly a §DX-01a leftover: `build-roads.js --apply` rerouted TLL→SPB off the Gulf-of-Finland sea cell 10,207 via the row-8 TUO corridor and did not re-lay the old row-10/11 run. Decide which is truth — re-lay the corridor with `build-roads.js --apply` (the §KG design promises encounter-free travel between the zones, so a player currently rolls encounters where the lab report says they shouldn't) or update the test's sample cells to the new route — then clear it from prompt.md §7. Note this is the exact class §DX-02c proposes a rule for, and §DX-01a's ship record did not mention the test. **Risk:** low.
- [ ] **§DX-02c — Process rule: when a checker red is accepted as "known baseline", annotate the lab report whose invariant it violates (idea 2026-07-28).** The NAV-01 report's rule "the on-disk game always passes `check:roads`" was silently false for 20 days (§KG reds) with no note anywhere near the report; §VM-01-G §6 even codified the reds as *expected*. Propose one CONTRIBUTING.md line: accepting a baseline red REQUIRES a dated stale-note in the affected lab report(s) + prompt.md §7, so reports never claim invariants the repo knowingly violates (the 2026-07-28 §DX-01a retro-annotation of the VM-01-G report is the template). **Risk:** none (docs process).

- [ ] **§MESH-01-REVIEW — remaining audit items:**
  - **`nearby`+`world[]` are pos-only** — the `room` half of the surface asymmetry closed with §NAV-01f (`buildLook` shared on start/move/look/pos); fold presence arrays into a shared builder only if a MUD client ever needs them on `move`/`look`. Minor.
  - [x] **Presence Playwright tests share one server ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-07** — the flake root: all 7 tests in `multiplayer-presence.test.js` share the ONE throwaway server, and `ctx.close()`/disconnect fire only `player_left` — the session itself lingers in `SESSIONS` to the 30-min TTL, so a leaked session bled a phantom pid/name into the next test's `/api/session/who`. Fixes: (1) **`test.afterEach` sweep** — GET `/api/session/who` → POST `/api/session/end` for every local session id, so each test starts clean (guarded `try/catch` for the afterAll window when the server is already gone). (2) **Assert by pid, never by count** — the reload ghost-check was `who.sessions.filter(name==='Odys')).toHaveLength(1)` (a raw count a cross-test leak inflates); now captures `pid = MP.pid` (stable across resume, `pid = pidOf(sid)`) and asserts `who.sessions.filter(name==='Odys').map(s=>s.pid)` `toEqual([pid])` — a ghost surfaces a *second, different* pid under the name; a leak can inflate a count but never forge a pid. Verified: `multiplayer-presence` **7/7** in 6.9 s (server-independent; dev :1367 restarted after). Test-file-only; no product code touched.
  - **Refactors:** if `MESH.log` pressure ever shows, derive `moved` from `arrived` at fanout instead of a third event per move. *(✅ 2026-07-06 client pair SHIPPED: `_mpRepaintMaps` 100 ms trailing debounce — SSE bursts coalesce into one repaint, MP.on re-checked at fire time; `_mpBase()` defaults to `location.origin` on http(s) pages so a world served by a friend connects back to that server, `file://` keeps the localhost:1367 dev default, explicit `localStorage.mpServer` still wins.)* *(✅ 2026-07-06 `mesh.js` extraction SHIPPED: the 434-line §MESH-01 kernel — ACL · rate limit · gossip · tracker/federation/bootstrap, the block between the old §MESH sentinels — moved verbatim to `mesh.js` as a factory `require('./mesh')(deps)`; the server passes live surfaces (SESSIONS, SSE fanout, chat ring, manifest/serverId, ledger hooks) and destructures the same names back so all endpoint call sites are unchanged; the one outside write to the internal `_trackerDirty` flag became `trackerMarkDirty()`. `wbapi-server.js` 11,726→11,314 lines. Gates: mud-harness 251/251 · mesh Playwright 43/43 · check:walk 6/6 · live boot + `./api.sh mesh status` verified. Docs synced: index.md file table + docs/notes/docs-node-network.md §12.)*
